# Multi-Organization Authorization System — Complete Portable Specification

> **Purpose.** This document is a complete, self-contained specification of the account / organization / membership / role / permission / invitation system of the "Multi-Organization Skeleton" project (built in Laravel). It exists so the SAME system can be re-implemented on another stack (Python) **without access to the original codebase or its author**. Every rule, condition, constraint, default, exact guard order and — where the code or the design record gives one — the *reason* behind it is written down. Messages that the UI or the tests depend on are quoted **verbatim**; `{name}`-style placeholders mark interpolated values.
>
> Conventions: "422" = rejection with a message (two shapes exist — see §19.1); "403" = forbidden; "404" = hidden/not found (used deliberately so ids outside where the actor stands do not leak existence); "429" = rate-limited. "Actor" = the authenticated user performing a request. "Target" = the account / member / role / organization / invitation being acted on. Laravel-specific terms are translated in §24. The digital-signage features (screens, media, playlists, channels, campaigns, schedules) are out of scope except where they touch the people model.

---

## 1. Core concepts and vocabulary

The system is a multi-tenant admin panel in which **the tenant is an organization** — the industry model of Slack workspaces, GitHub organizations or Shopify stores. Power comes from **membership** alone: a person is a member of an organization with exactly one role there. **Nobody owns anybody**: no account records who created it, nobody sets or knows another person's password, and deleting a person deletes nothing anybody else holds. *Reason (owner, 2026-09-16): the previous model gave power through "whoever created you owns you", and a loophole hunt proved that an organization user could reset the password of — and cascade-delete — a person they had created after that person was promoted to Super-Admin.*

- **Account** (`users`) — an identity: name, phone, email, password. Changed only by its owner (Profile, §14.2) or by "forgot password" (§19.4). An account by itself gives no power, and until it has confirmed its email address it opens nothing but the pages that confirm it (§10.1a — owner's rule, 2026-09-29). One address is one account.
- **Organization** — the organization (tenant). It owns its memberships, its custom roles, its open invitations and its content (screens, media, playlists, its own channels, and its Ad Builder designs and assets).
- **Membership** — an `organization_user` row `(user_id, organization_id > 0, role_id)`: one role per person per organization (DB unique).
- **Platform membership** — an `organization_user` row on the **sentinel `organization_id = 0`** holding a platform (global) role. Its holder works *above* the organizations.
- **Two tiers, mutually exclusive.** An account is a platform account (has an organization-0 row), an organization account (has real-organization rows), or has no access at all — never both tiers. Every door that creates a membership refuses the other tier (§9.2, §9.3, §9.6, §10.2, §10.3, §13.4). *Reason (owner): mixing the tiers makes visibility and permissions too complex to reason about.*
- **Super admin** — a platform member whose platform role is THE Super-Admin role: the role named exactly `Super-Admin`, the name compared in application code and never through a database collation (§4.1). **A super admin holds every permission** — every permission check answers yes, whatever the role's rows say (§3.3) — while the rules that are not permissions still bind them. Only super admins make and change the organization roles and the platform roles (§7), look after every organization's custom roles from the platform (§7.3), put anybody in any organization with a role, change that role or take them out (Users → Organizations, §13.4), invite platform staff, remove platform roles, "Log in as", reach the permission catalogue and the TV app (Developer, §16a), and run network advertising.
- **Primary super admin** — the first person ever given Super-Admin (§4.2). Nobody may delete them or take their role; only they may delete another super admin, take Super-Admin from someone, or invite a new super admin.
- **Role kinds** (§2.3, §7 — owner's rules, 2026-09-17: **no role is built in but Super-Admin**): **organization roles** (`is_global = false`, no organization) — made by a super admin and offered in every organization; renamed, changed (in every organization at once) and deleted while nobody holds them; **the Owner role** — the organization role marked `roles.key = 'owner'`, whose holders own their organization; renamed and changed like any organization role, never deleted; **custom** roles (belong to one organization, made inside it); **platform** roles (`is_global = true`, Super-Admin included). Owner, Admin, Staff and Viewer are only the **starter** organization roles an installation begins with (§7.1).
- **Where a permission reaches is where the role sits** (owner's rules, 2026-09-16): on a platform role, every organization; on an organization's role (an organization role or a custom role), the one organization the member holds it in. An organization's role may also carry the platform permissions that can work inside one organization — organizations, channels, reading the activity log (`Permission::ORGANIZATION_SCOPED`) — and there they act on that organization alone (§3.2, §3.5). The accounts are the platform's alone: an organization's people are its Members page (owner's rule, 2026-09-17, §13).
- **The Owner role grants nothing by itself** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: "a role does not matter, its permissions do") — it marks who owns an organization: the organization's "at least one Owner" (§8.2), the role public signup, owner invitations and opening an organization give (§10). Deleting an organization is the permission `organization-destroy`, which the Owner role starts with (§7.1, §11.4); inviting to or giving the Owner role, or changing or removing an Owner, needs every permission the Owner role holds — the same reach rule as any other role (§8.1). There is no separate handover permission: giving the Owner role is how an organization changes hands (§11.3, §26.16).
- **Hierarchy** — one service, `OrganizationTeam` (§8): you hand out only roles whose permissions you hold, and you manage only members whose role's permissions you hold, never yourself — with no exception by role, the Owner role included (a super admin's Users → Organizations from the platform is bound only by "at least one Owner", §13.4). Any member may leave an organization — from the Members page, or from "Your organizations" (on Settings → Organizations, or on the profile) — except its last Owner. **At least one Owner, always** — changing a role or removing a member (on the Members page or from the platform's Users → Organizations), leaving and self-deletion all refuse an organization's last Owner; each is decided under a lock on the organization row so that co-owners acting at the same moment cannot both pass (§8.2). *Reach alone would not keep it: a member who is not an Owner but holds every permission of the Owner role reaches an Owner, so the Owner count is asked on its own.*
- **How people enter** — nobody is created by somebody else: public signup (the person creates their account and their organization and becomes its Owner, §10.1) or an **email invitation** whose link lets the invitee create their own account or sign in (§9). A member whose role carries `organization-store` may also open another organization from Settings → Organizations and becomes its Owner at once (§10.2), and a super admin may put an existing account holding no platform role straight into any organization with a role (§13.4). Whichever the door, the account proves its inbox before the panel opens: the link signup emails, or an invitation's link, or a completed password reset (§10.1a).
- **A paused organization** — the platform's Active switch off (`organizations.is_active`, §2.2; owner, 2026-09-30): closed to its own people, whose role there grants nothing meanwhile (§5.3), while the platform keeps looking after it and its screens keep playing.

**The sentinel row:** `organization_user.organization_id` has **no foreign key** and defaults to `0`; `0` never matches a real organization. Any query joining `organization_user` to `organizations` silently drops sentinel rows; code needing the platform row queries the pivot directly with `organization_id = 0`.

---

## 2. Data model (exact schema)

All tables have an auto-increment integer `id` PK and `created_at`/`updated_at` unless stated otherwise.

### 2.1 `users`
| column | type | constraints |
|---|---|---|
| first_name, last_name, phone | string | NOT NULL |
| email | string | NOT NULL, **UNIQUE** (DB) — one address is one account, compared as typed once lower-cased: no folding of dots or `+tags` (owner's rule, 2026-09-29). Written lower-cased by signup (§10.1), by invitation registration and by the profile, which lowers capitals rather than refusing them (§14.2, §19.2) |
| pending_email | string | nullable — a confirmed account's new address, waiting for the link sent to it (§10.1a, §14.2); the address in `email` stands meanwhile. No unique index: it reserves nothing (§26.23) |
| email_verified_at | timestamp | nullable. **NULL = the account has not confirmed its email**, and the `verified` middleware keeps it out of the panel (§10.1a — owner's rule, 2026-09-29). Set by: the signed link signup emails (`GET /verify-email/{id}/{hash}`); a super admin viewing as the account who presses **Confirm Email and Continue** (owner, 2026-10-06); accepting an invitation by its link, and registering from one (§9.5 — opening the link proved the inbox); completing a password reset (§19.4); opening the link sent to a changed address (§14.2); the seeder for its admin (§21). Never nulled any more: a confirmed account's changed address waits in `pending_email` instead. An installation upgraded from the old migration files had every account of its day marked confirmed by the migration that brought the rule (one exact stamp, which its `down()` undid; squashed away since, §22.4), so nobody was locked out |
| password | string | bcrypt, hash-on-write |
| remember_token | string | nullable — backs "remember me" (§19.3); rotated on password reset, and on logout when one is set |

- **Gone from the old model:** `created_by` (FK users, SET NULL). The create-table migration no longer adds it at all (§22), so no installation of this release has it. Nothing records who created or invited an account.
- **`name` is computed, not stored:** `trim(first_name + ' ' + last_name)` — used for display and for activity-log actor snapshots.
- **No soft delete** on users (owner decision). Deletion = hard delete of the row + foreign-key effects + the model's `deleted` hook, which removes what points at the account from outside the foreign keys (§15.3). An account never confirmed is deleted by itself after `User::UNVERIFIED_DAYS` (7) days, with the empty organization it made (§10.1a).
- Companion tables: `password_reset_tokens` (email PK, token, created_at) and a server-side `sessions` table (id PK, user_id nullable indexed, ip_address, user_agent, payload, last_activity indexed) — both created with `users` by the same migration. **There is no `personal_access_tokens` table:** it went with the Sanctum scaffold and the API that never shipped (§26.8), and a test asserts the table is absent (§22). A deleted account's rows in both tables are removed by the `deleted` hook (`sessions` only on the `database` session driver, §15.3).

### 2.2 `organizations`
| column | type | constraints |
|---|---|---|
| name | string | NOT NULL |
| slug | unsigned bigint | **UNIQUE**, auto-generated on create: current Unix time in microseconds, STRING-concatenated with a random integer 10–999, parsed as one number; regenerated while it collides. Any generator of unique unsigned bigints the caller never supplies is acceptable |
| street | string | NOT NULL |
| suite | string | nullable |
| city | string | NOT NULL |
| state | string(2) | NOT NULL |
| zip_code | string(10) | NOT NULL |
| country | string | NOT NULL |
| is_active | boolean | default true — **false = the platform has paused the organization** (owner, 2026-09-30: the Active switch, made to mean something), shown as "Paused" on the Organizations page and the chooser (§18). Changed only by the platform's organization form (§12.3), logged as `organization.paused` / `organization.resumed`; an organization opened from inside an organization starts active (§10.2), and Settings → Organizations never reads the field (§11.2). What a pause does is below |
| premium_templates_unlocked, platform_channels_unlocked | boolean | default **true** each (owner, 2026-10-08: "abhi sub k liya premium khula rakho", a new organization too) — what billing has unlocked (docs/BILLING-SPEC.md). Never mass-assignable; turned by the platform alone, with `billing-update` (§3.5, §12.1a) |
| accepts_network_ads | boolean | default false — signage; set only by super-admin tools, never by any form in this document |
| created_by | FK → users.id | nullable, ON DELETE SET NULL — **history only** (the self-registered owner, the platform user who created the organization, or the member who opened it from inside an organization); drives no power |

- **A paused organization** (`is_active = false`) is closed to its own people — exactly, and nothing more:
  - `User::pausedOrganization()` = the organization of `session.current_organization_id` when the person is a member of it, holds no platform role and the organization's `is_active` is false; null otherwise.
  - **Their role there grants nothing meanwhile** — `contextPermissionNames` reads no role while `pausedOrganization()` is set (§5.3) — so no page offers what would be refused.
  - **`EnsureOrganizationIsActive`** (alias `organization.active`) sits on every route of the signed-in panel (the admin group, §3.7) and on the chunked uploads: with a paused organization a page request is redirected to the dashboard, which says "{organization} is paused" — "Its pages are closed for now, and its screens keep playing. Contact support to turn it back on." (`dashboard-paused`, with **Choose Another Organization** for somebody of several, §18) — and a JSON request answers **403** `{message: "{organization} is paused. Contact support to turn it back on.", paused: true}` (a page then toasts it and opens the dashboard, §20.7).
  - **What stays open** (`EnsureOrganizationIsActive::OPEN`): switching to another of their organizations (`organization.switch`), leaving it (`members.leave`), and answering on the dashboard an invitation to another (`dashboard.invitations.accept` / `.decline`) — and, being outside that group, the dashboard itself, the chooser, their profile (leaving from "Your organizations" included), sign-out and the verification pages. "Log in as" one of them shows the same paused page.
  - **The platform is never stopped by it** (a platform account has no `pausedOrganization()`): it reads the organization's library and pages and turns it back on from the Organizations page ("Off pauses the organization: its people cannot open it, and its screens keep playing."). **The organization's screens keep playing**: the device API is not behind it (the owner has not asked for a paused organization's televisions to stop).
  - Tested (`PausedOrganizationTest`): the dashboard says so and the menu offers no page of it, `contextPermissionNames` is empty; every page redirects to the dashboard and every request is refused with exactly those words (an upload before a byte); another organization, leaving and the profile stay open; the screen's playlist still answers; the platform reads it and turning it back on opens it again; the log reads `organization.paused`, `organization.resumed`, `organization.updated`. `PausedOrganizationAttackTest` walks every route of the panel.
- **No soft delete** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: "A to Z"): deleting an organization purges what it owns and then deletes the row for good (§15.4); the people's accounts and the activity log stay. An older build had a `deleted_at` column that merely hid a deleted organization; the create-table migration no longer has it (§22), and a test asserts it is absent. A deleted organization is simply missing: every lookup of it answers exactly as for an id that never existed (e.g. 404 at route-model binding, §3.3).

### 2.3 `roles`
| column | type | constraints |
|---|---|---|
| name | string | NOT NULL, **NOT unique in the database** — the application keeps it new to every list the role is shown in, compared folded (§7.5) |
| key | string(32) | nullable, **UNIQUE** — `owner` on the Owner role; `admin` / `staff` / `viewer` on the other starter roles while they exist (§7.1); NULL on every other role — no HTTP path writes a key |
| is_global | boolean | default false |
| organization_id | FK → organizations.id | nullable, **ON DELETE CASCADE** — written by the create-table migration (an older build had SET NULL, §22) — a custom role goes with its organization and can never outlive it as an organization role offered in every organization (tested: deleting an organization row straight in the database takes its custom role). The purge of §15.4 already deletes an organization's custom roles before the row goes; the cascade is the backstop |
| created_by | FK → users.id | nullable, ON DELETE SET NULL — history only |

- **Gone from the old model:** `is_signup_default` — the create-table migration no longer adds it (§22), and a test asserts it is absent. Public signup finds the Owner role by `key`, never by a flag or a name.
- **Role kinds — invariants** (owner's rules, 2026-09-17; enforced by the code paths that create roles, and by the starter roles the migrations insert, §22). What a role is for never changes once it exists:

| kind | `key` | `is_global` | `organization_id` | created by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Super-Admin | NULL | true (forced by the seeder) | NULL | the seeder (§21) |
| Organization role | `owner` on the Owner role, a starter key on Admin / Staff / Viewer, NULL on the rest | false | NULL | the starter-roles migration (the four starters) and the seeder (the Owner role when missing), §21–§22; a super admin, `POST /roles` on the platform with `type: organization` (§7.4) |
| Custom | NULL | false | the organization | an organization member, `POST /roles` inside that organization (§7.4) |
| Platform | NULL | true | NULL | a super admin, `POST /roles` on the platform with `type: platform` |

- **Names are not unique in the database:** the role **id** is the identity and everything joins on it. The application keeps a name apart from every role shown in the same list (§7.5): "Cashier" may be a custom role of several organizations, but not also an organization role, and no role may be named like Super-Admin.

### 2.4 `permissions`
`name` string NOT NULL, **UNIQUE at the database level** (plus validation-layer uniqueness) — it is the authorization key · `label` string nullable — display name, regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9 ]*$`, message **"Label can only contain letters, numbers, and spaces."** Display name falls back to the raw `name` when `label` is null (exposed to clients as `display_name`).

### 2.5 `role_has_permissions`
`role_id` FK CASCADE · `permission_id` FK CASCADE · **UNIQUE (role_id, permission_id)** · timestamps.

### 2.6 `organization_user` (the heart)
`user_id` FK users CASCADE · `organization_id` unsigned bigint **NO FK, default 0** (so the sentinel 0 can exist) · `role_id` FK roles CASCADE · **UNIQUE (organization_id, user_id)** — the DB guarantee of one role per person per organization · timestamps (`created_at` is shown as the member's "Joined" date).

### 2.7 `invitations` (new)
| column | type | constraints |
|---|---|---|
| organization_id | FK → organizations.id | nullable, ON DELETE CASCADE — **NULL = an invitation to the platform team** (accepting creates an `organization_id = 0` membership) |
| email | string | NOT NULL — stored **lowercased and trimmed** |
| role_id | FK → roles.id | NOT NULL, ON DELETE CASCADE |
| token_hash | char(64) | **UNIQUE** — lowercase hex SHA-256 of the token; the token itself exists only in the email link |
| invited_by | FK → users.id | nullable, ON DELETE SET NULL |
| expires_at | timestamp | NOT NULL |

Indexes: `(organization_id, email)` and `(email)`. **There is no unique constraint on `(organization_id, email)`** — "one open invitation per email per place" is enforced by the application only (§9.2, §26.4). A row exists only while the invitation is open: accepting, declining, revoking and — for the Owner invitations of an organization that has no Owner — being replaced by the platform's "Invite owner", which invites another address or makes a member Owner (§10.3), delete it; deleting its role (§7.8) or its organization (§15.4) deletes it too. Expired rows are **never pruned** (§26.3).

### 2.8 `activity_logs`
`id` · `actor_id` nullable (FK **dropped** on MySQL when partitioned — §17.5; on other drivers ON DELETE SET NULL) · `actor_name` string NOT NULL (snapshot) · `organization_id` unsigned bigint nullable, **no foreign key** — the organization the entry belongs to (§17.1); NULL for personal and platform entries, and on a database that predates the column for everything logged before it was added (it is now created with the table, §22; no FK because the table is partitioned on MySQL, which allows none, and an organization's history outlives the organization) · `action` string(100) indexed · `subject_type` string(100) nullable (short class name: `User`, `Organization`, `Role`, `Permission`, …) · `subject_id` nullable · `description` string(1000) nullable · `created_at` indexed, **no updated_at** (DATETIME on MySQL). Index **`(organization_id, created_at)`** — an organization's reader always filters on the organization, then on the date range that prunes the yearly partitions.

### 2.9 `created_by` on content; a channel's organization
`media`, `screens`, `campaigns`, `channels`, `channel_ads`, `builder_ads` and `builder_assets` (like `organizations` and `roles`) carry `created_by` FK users nullable **ON DELETE SET NULL** (`screens.paired_by`, `builder_ads.updated_by`, `builder_fonts.installed_by` and the TV app's `player_app_releases.uploaded_by` likewise). It is history only: nothing is authorized, scoped or deleted through it anywhere.

`channels.organization_id` (created with the channels table, §22): FK → organizations.id, nullable, ON DELETE CASCADE (a backstop only — §15.4 deletes an organization's channels explicitly before the organization row goes, so that their ads' files are unlinked too). **NULL = the platform's channel**, offered to every organization; **set = that organization's own channel**, offered to its own screens alone (§6.6). `channels.name` carries **no unique index** (an older build had one): a name has to stand apart only within what one organization's list shows, checked by the application (§6.6).

---

## 3. Permission catalog (exactly 42)

Names follow `{module}-{action}`. The Permission model holds **three lists** that together cover every labelled permission exactly once (tested): `ORGANIZATION` (23), `PLATFORM` (12), `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` (7) — and a fourth that overlaps them, **`ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` (8)**: the platform permissions an organization's role may carry too, which there reach that one organization (§3.5). `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` is all of `PLATFORM` except `user-view`, `user-destroy`, `activity-destroy` and `billing-update` (tested in `DatabaseSeederTest`: `PLATFORM` minus `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` is exactly those four, in that order). Labels are the exact seeded values (`Permission::LABELS`).

### 3.1 The catalogue
| name | label | list | gates (method + path; every route also runs the admin stack `auth`, `verified`, `organization.active`, `throttle:admin`, §3.3) |
|---|---|---|---|
| organization-update | Update Organization Details | ORGANIZATION | `PUT /settings/organization` (organization context — the Organizations tab of Settings, whose page opens with `organization-view`, §11.1–§11.2); `PUT /organizations/{organization}` (with `global-tier`: any organization, §12.3) |
| member-view | View Members | ORGANIZATION | `GET /members`, `GET /members/data` |
| member-invite | Invite Members | ORGANIZATION | `POST /members/invitations`, `POST /members/invitations/{invitation}/resend`, `DELETE /members/invitations/{invitation}` |
| member-update | Change Member Roles | ORGANIZATION | `PUT /members/{user}` |
| member-remove | Remove Members | ORGANIZATION | `DELETE /members/{user}` |
| role-view | View Roles | ORGANIZATION | `GET /roles`, `GET /roles/data`, `GET /roles/assignable` |
| role-store | Create Roles | ORGANIZATION | `POST /roles` |
| role-update | Update Roles | ORGANIZATION | `PUT /roles/{role}` |
| role-destroy | Delete Roles | ORGANIZATION | `DELETE /roles/{role}` |
| screen-view | View Screens | ORGANIZATION | screens page, data, one screen, reading its playlist |
| screen-store | Pair Screens | ORGANIZATION | pairing a TV |
| screen-update | Update Screens | ORGANIZATION | renaming / re-orienting; the default-media picker |
| screen-destroy | Delete Screens | ORGANIZATION | deleting a screen (revokes its device) |
| screen-playlist | Change Playlists | ORGANIZATION | writing, previewing and copying a playlist; the playlist's own media and channel pickers |
| media-view | View Media Library | ORGANIZATION | media page + data |
| media-store | Upload Media | ORGANIZATION | upload |
| media-update | Update Media | ORGANIZATION | title, description, schedule |
| media-destroy | Delete Media | ORGANIZATION | delete a file |
| ad-view | View Ads | ORGANIZATION | the Ad Builder (`/builder/*`, out of scope): the Ads and Assets tabs and their data; one of the permissions the fonts list and the draft preview accept (below) |
| ad-store | Create Ads | ORGANIZATION | the Ad Builder: a new design, a copy, uploading an asset, the Premium Templates gallery and Use This Template (`GET /builder/templates`, `POST /builder/templates/{ad}`, inside an organization alone); one of the permissions the fonts list and installing a font accept (below) |
| ad-update | Update Ads | ORGANIZATION | the Ad Builder: opening, saving and publishing a design; one of the permissions the fonts list, installing a font and the draft preview accept (below) |
| ad-destroy | Delete Ads | ORGANIZATION | the Ad Builder: deleting a design (with the password, §19.7) or an asset |
| billing-view | View Billing | ORGANIZATION | `GET /settings/billing` (organization context — the Billing tab of Settings, §20.3; 404 to a platform account); `GET /organizations/{organization}/billing` (with `global-tier`: any organization's billing, read-only, §12.1a) |
| user-view | View Accounts | PLATFORM | `GET /users`, `GET /users/data` (with `global-tier`, §13.1) |
| user-destroy | Delete Accounts | PLATFORM | `DELETE /users/{user}` (with `global-tier`, §13.2) |
| organization-view | View Organizations | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | `GET /organizations`, `GET /organizations/data` (with `global-tier`); `GET /settings/organization` (organization context — the Organizations tab of Settings, §11.1) |
| organization-store | Create Organizations | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | `POST /organizations`, `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation` (both with `global-tier`); `POST /settings/organization/open` (organization context, §10.2) |
| organization-destroy | Delete Organizations | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | `DELETE /organizations/{organization}` (with `global-tier`); `DELETE /settings/organization` (organization context, §11.4) |
| channel-view | View Channels | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | channels page, data, one channel, its ads |
| channel-store | Create Channels | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | create a channel |
| channel-update | Update Channels | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | edit a channel; add / edit / reorder / remove its ads |
| channel-destroy | Delete Channels | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | delete a channel |
| activity-view | View Activity Log | PLATFORM · ORGANIZATION_SCOPED | `GET /activity`, `GET /activity/data`; `GET /activity/partitions` (with `global-tier`) |
| activity-destroy | Delete Old Activity Logs | PLATFORM | `POST /activity/partitions/maintain` (with `global-tier`) |
| billing-update | Change Billing | PLATFORM | `PUT /organizations/{organization}/billing` (with `global-tier`: the Premium Templates and Platform Channels switches, §12.1a) |
| permission-view | View Permissions | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `GET /permissions`, `GET /permissions/data` (with `super-admin-tier`) |
| permission-store | Create Permissions | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `POST /permissions` (with `super-admin-tier`) |
| permission-update | Update Permissions | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `PUT /permissions/{permission}` (with `super-admin-tier`) |
| permission-destroy | Delete Permissions | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `DELETE /permissions/{permission}` (with `super-admin-tier`) |
| player-app-view | View TV App | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `GET /developer`, `GET /developer/app/releases/data` (with `super-admin-tier`, §16a) |
| player-app-store | Upload TV App | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `POST /developer/app/inspect`, `POST /developer/app/releases` (with `super-admin-tier`); opening a chunked upload for the TV app (`/uploads`, purpose `app-release`: `super-admin-tier` and this permission, asked in the upload service) |
| player-app-destroy | Delete TV App Versions | SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY | `DELETE /developer/app/releases/{release}` (with `super-admin-tier`) |

**"Any of", in the controller:** three Ad Builder routes carry no `can:` — a route's `can:` cannot say "any of" — and their controllers answer 403 unless the actor holds one of the listed permissions: `GET /builder/fonts` → `ad-view`, `ad-store` or `ad-update`; `POST /builder/fonts` → `ad-store` or `ad-update` (the route keeps `throttle:font-install`, §19.6); `GET /builder/{ad}/preview` → `ad-view` or `ad-update`, an ad out of reach still answering 404. *Reason: the editor opens with Create Ads or Update Ads, and neither needs View Ads.*

**The later permissions** (each with a migration of its own, which grants it to Super-Admin by the exact name compared in PHP, §22.2b): `billing-view` and `billing-update` (owner, 2026-10-08, `2026_10_08_100300_insert_the_billing_permissions` — View Billing also to the Owner and Admin roles by key, docs/BILLING-SPEC.md) and `player-app-view`, `player-app-store`, `player-app-destroy` (owner, 2026-10-10, `2026_10_10_100100_insert_the_player_app_permissions` — Super-Admin alone).

**Permissions the old model had, and no installation of this release has** (history — the catalogue a fresh install ends with is exactly the 42 above: the baseline inserts 41, the four `daypart-*` are taken away again by the migration that put the time on the schedule rule, §22.2a, and the two migrations above add 2 + 3): `daypart-view`, `daypart-store`, `daypart-update` and `daypart-destroy`, which went with the Dayparts page on 2026-10-01 — the time of day is typed on the schedule rule, and whoever may change a playlist (`screen-playlist`) sets its hours (docs/SCHEDULE-SPEC.md §21); `user-organization`, `user-update`, `user-organization-view`, `user-organization-assign`, `user-organization-unassign`, mapped onto the `member-*` permissions and dropped in the 2026-09-16 rebuild; and `organization-transfer` ("Transfer Organization Ownership"), added on 2026-09-17 for a handover form on the Organizations tab and deleted the same day with every grant of it (owner's rules, 2026-09-17, third round: an organization changes hands on its Members page, §11.3). **Never on an organization's role:** `user-view` and `user-destroy` — refused by `permissionsFor` (§7.6), and the `/users` group keeps `global-tier` whatever a role holds; the accounts are the platform's (§13) — and `billing-update`, likewise refused, its route inside the `global-tier` group: an organization never unlocks itself. *An installation upgraded on 2026-09-17 had them stripped from its organization roles and custom roles by a migration since squashed away (§22), so only a row written straight into the database can still carry one.* **Naming gotcha:** `organization-store` means CREATE an organization (on the platform for a customer, from Settings → Organizations for the member themself — §10.2); `organization-update` is an ORGANIZATION permission that is dual-use (§3.5). **Labels:** `user-view` and `organization-view` read "View All Accounts" / "View All Organizations" in an older build; the catalogue now inserts **"View Accounts"** and **"View Organizations"**, because on an organization's role they no longer mean "all". No migration ever rewords a label now, but a re-seed resets every catalogued label to the value above (§21), so a rewording done on the Permissions page does not survive one.

### 3.2 Which role may carry what
*Owner's rules, 2026-09-16: the super admin sees every permission and decides who holds what; an organization's people work within their organization.*
- **Where a permission reaches is where the role sits** (§3.5): on a platform role, every organization; on an organization's role (an organization role or a custom role), the one organization the member holds it in.
- An **organization's role** — an organization role (the Owner role included) or a custom role, whoever writes it: a member inside the organization, or a super admin on the platform (§7.4, §7.7) — carries only what can work inside one organization: `belongsToOrganizations(name) = name ∈ ORGANIZATION ∪ ORGANIZATION_SCOPED`. A member giving a custom role permissions inside an organization may give, on top of that, only what they hold there themselves (§7.6).
- **Never on an organization's role:** `user-view` and `user-destroy` (the accounts are the platform's alone — an organization's people are its Members page; owner's rule, 2026-09-17), `activity-destroy` (yearly maintenance drops every organization's history at once), `billing-update` (an organization never unlocks itself — owner, 2026-10-08), a permission in **no list** (one created on the Permissions page — it counts as platform-only), and `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY`.
- `billing-view` is an **organization** permission: on an organization's role it opens Settings → Billing for the organization the member works in; the Owner and Admin roles hold it from its migration on (§3.1, §22.2b).
- A **platform role other than Super-Admin** carries anything except `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` — organization permissions included (§3.5).
- `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` — the catalogue (`permission-*`), and the TV app (`player-app-*`, owner 2026-10-10: "the super admin alone sees it") — goes on **no role but Super-Admin** — not even another platform role (owner's decision, kept).
- The **Super-Admin role** is given every permission row by the seeder and cannot be edited or deleted through HTTP (§7.7) — but its rows no longer matter for authorization: a super admin passes every permission check anyway (§3.3).
- **Campaigns are not a permission row:** `campaign-manage` is a hand-written super-admin gate (§3.4) and stays so (owner's decision).
- Every refusal holds **twice**: the role write refuses a misfit (§7.6) AND what only works above the organizations keeps a tier lock on its route (§3.4) — `global-tier` on the Organizations page and its writes (§12, the Billing dialog's two routes among them), the accounts pages (the whole `/users` group, §13), the activity log's partitions and maintenance, giving an organization an owner, "Log in as", the platform's Users → Organizations and the platform team; `super-admin-tier` on the catalogue, Developer → TV App (`/developer`, §16a), platform invitations, platform-role removal and Users → Organizations. *Reason: a row can reach the wrong role by other means (written before the rule, or straight into the database) — tested: an organization user whose role somehow holds `activity-destroy` still gets 403 from the partitions and maintenance routes, an Owner whose role somehow holds `user-view` and `user-destroy` gets 403 from `GET /users`, `GET /users/data` and `DELETE /users/{user}`, an organization's own people never unlock their organization whatever their role holds (`BillingAttackTest`), and a platform role given `player-app-view` straight in the database gets 403 from `/developer` and is offered no Developer link (`PlayerAppReleaseTest`).* A platform account holding every permission row is still not a super admin: the `super-admin-tier` lock keeps it out of the catalogue (tested).
- *Why the catalogue is never delegated (owner): every permission name is written in code on the routes, so renaming a row either breaks that feature for everybody but the super admins (whom the bypass of §3.3 lets through) or turns a harmless permission a person already holds into a powerful one.*

### 3.3 Authorization wiring
- At boot, **one gate per permission ROW** is registered: `allowed(actor) = name ∈ contextPermissionNames(actor)` (§5.3). Route middleware `can:{name}` enforces per endpoint. An ability with no gate (e.g. a deleted row) denies — except for a super admin (next point).
- **A super admin holds every permission** (owner's rule, 2026-09-16: "sab matlab sab"). A single before-hook runs ahead of every ability check for an authenticated user: for the ability `network-ads-toggle` it abstains; otherwise, when `actor.isSuperAdmin()`, it answers **yes**; otherwise it abstains and the ability's own gate decides. So a super admin passes every `can:` middleware, `@can` and `can()` — whatever the Super-Admin role's rows say, for a permission made later on the Permissions page or one renamed, and for the hand-written `global-tier` / `super-admin-tier` / `campaign-manage` gates too (tested: a super admin whose role holds no rows reads the channels, activity and organizations data, and `can('report-export')` is true for a permission created afterwards).
  ```
  before(actor, ability) = None if ability == 'network-ads-toggle' else (True if actor.isSuperAdmin() else None)
  ```
  *It answers permission checks only:* the rules that are not permissions still stand for a super admin — `network-ads-toggle` (a place — "inside an organization, while logged in as one of its people" — not a permission; tested: 403 in an organization without impersonation), the primary super admin's protection (§13.2, §13.3), Super-Admin never changed and the Owner role never deleted (§7.7, §7.8), an organization's last Owner, a super admin never deleting themselves (§14.2), and every "is this row within reach / inside where you stand" guard (a super admin is not a member of any organization, so `currentOrganization()` still answers 404, §6.1).
- **Middleware order** (the framework sorts the web group and the route's middleware by its priority list — load-bearing for status codes):
  1. `RejectMalformedText` — prepended to **every** route, the device API's too: query, form, uploaded file names or User-Agent that are not UTF-8 → **400** "The request carried text that is not valid UTF-8.";
  2. cookies and the session, then CSRF (**419**);
  3. authentication (guest → 401 JSON / redirect to login);
  4. rate limiting (**429**);
  5. route-model binding (a missing id, a deleted organization's too → **404**);
  6. `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization` (end of the web group): for a signed-in person with no platform role, forgets a session organization they are no longer in, and chooses their organization when they have exactly one and none is chosen (§5.1);
  7. `RefuseAStaleTab` (end of the web group): a request from a page drawn for another person or another organization → **409** / redirect back (§20.4a);
  8. `verified` (an account that has not confirmed its email → redirect to "Check your inbox", JSON **403** "Your email address is not verified.", §10.1a);
  9. `organization.active` (a paused organization's people → redirect to the dashboard, JSON **403** with `paused: true`, §2.2);
  10. `can:` gates in declared order, group before route (**403**);
  11. controller guards.

  Consequences: a nonexistent id answers 404 even to someone who lacks the permission, or whose tab is stale; a refused attempt still spends the rate-limit budget. A refusal's words are rewritten for a page that wants JSON (§19.1), and a non-JSON `GET` refused 403 for somebody of several organizations who has chosen none is sent to the chooser instead, which returns them to that page (§18).

### 3.4 Hand-written gates (never grantable)
| gate | true when | locks |
|---|---|---|
| `global-tier` | `actor.globalRole() != null` | the whole `/organizations` group but the switch: `GET /organizations`, `GET /organizations/data`, `POST /organizations`, `PUT` / `DELETE /organizations/{organization}`, `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation`, `GET` / `PUT /organizations/{organization}/billing` (§12, §12.1a; the sidebar's Organizations link is likewise offered to platform accounts only, §20.2); `GET /activity/partitions`, `POST /activity/partitions/maintain`; the whole `/users` group (§13; the sidebar's Users group is likewise offered to platform accounts only, §20.2): `GET /users`, `GET /users/data`, `DELETE /users/{user}`, `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role`, `POST /users/{user}/impersonate`, `GET` and `POST /users/{user}/organizations`, `PUT /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}/role`, `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}`, `/users/invitations/*`; the Activity Log page's storage panel (with `activity-destroy`, §17.5) |
| `super-admin-tier` | `actor.isSuperAdmin()` | `/permissions/*`, `/developer/*` (Developer → TV App, §16a, and the TV app's chunked upload), `/users/invitations/*`, `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role`, the four Users → Organizations routes (`GET` / `POST /users/{user}/organizations`, `PUT /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}/role`, `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}`, §13.4); sidebar Permissions and Developer; the Users page's tabs and the platform-team invite (§13.1) |
| `campaign-manage` | `actor.isSuperAdmin()` | `/campaigns/*`, `PUT /network-ads/organizations`; sidebar Advertising; the advertising column and counts of the organizations listing |
| `network-ads-toggle` | `session.impersonating_original_id` is set AND `session.impersonating_user_id` equals the actor's id AND the original user exists and `isSuperAdmin()` — i.e. only inside a live "Log in as" session (§14.1) | `PUT /network-ads/organization`, `PUT /network-ads/screens` |

*Reasons: `global-tier` guards what only works above the organizations — the Organizations page and its writes (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: an organization's own people change the organization they work in from the Organizations tab of Settings, §11), the accounts pages (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: an organization's people are its Members page, §13), yearly log maintenance and the partition status (which counts every organization's rows), giving an organization an owner, "Log in as", the platform team and putting people in organizations, changing their role there or taking them out from the platform (Users → Organizations) — and closes those doors during "Log in as", where the person acting is an organization member. Channels and the activity log themselves are NOT tier-locked: an organization's role may carry those permissions, and their controllers then answer for that one organization (§3.5). There is no gate for the Organizations tab of Settings: it opens with the permission `organization-view` and each card asks its own permission (§11) — the `organization-settings` gate that once opened it by `organization-update`, `organization-destroy` or the Owner role is gone. `campaign-manage` is not a row because a campaign has no organization — a grantable row could reach an organization's role and show every brand's contract.* The super-admin before-hook (§3.3) also answers these for a super admin — except `network-ads-toggle`, which it leaves to its own test.

### 3.5 Dual use — a permission reaches where its role sits
**Organization permissions on a platform role:**
- `organization-update` on a platform role = edit **any** organization's details (`PUT /organizations/{organization}`, §12.3); on an organization's role it edits the details of the organization the member works in (the Organizations tab of Settings, §11.2) — never through the Organizations page, which is the platform's (§12).
- `billing-view` on a platform role = read **any** organization's billing — Billing beside Edit on the Organizations page (`GET /organizations/{organization}/billing`, with `global-tier`, §12.1a); on an organization's role, Settings → Billing for the organization the member works in (`GET /settings/billing`, which answers a platform account 404). `billing-update` — the switches — is a platform permission that no organization's role may carry (§3.2). Tested (`BillingTest`): a platform role holding `organization-view` and `billing-view` reads an organization's billing (`can.billing` true) but gets 403 turning a switch, and its dialog shows no switch; one without `billing-view` gets `can.billing` false and 403 on the summary; one also holding `billing-update` turns them.
- Content permissions (`screen-*`, `media-*`, `ad-*`) on a platform role reach **every** organization (§6.6; the Ad Builder's designs and assets through `BuilderAd::visibleTo` / `BuilderAsset::visibleTo`, the same rule). *Design record: "a support person with `media-view` reads every organization's library".*
- `member-*` on a platform role opens nothing (the members endpoints answer 404 — a platform account has no current organization, §6.1); `role-*` on a platform role that is not Super-Admin opens nothing (roles endpoints 403, §6.5).

**Platform permissions on an organization's role (`ORGANIZATION_SCOPED`) — that organization alone** (owner's rule, 2026-09-16):

| permission | on a platform role | on an organization's role (an organization role or a custom role) |
|---|---|---|
| `organization-view` | every organization (§12.1) | the Organizations tab of Settings for the organization the person works in — its details, and "Your organizations": every organization they belong to (§11.1); the Owner and Admin roles start with it (§7.1) |
| `organization-store` | create an organization for a customer with an Owner invitation (§10.2); "Invite owner" for an organization with no Owner (§10.3) — both with `global-tier` | Create organization on that tab: a new organization they own at once — no invitation, no owner email, and the active switch stays the platform's (§10.2); never "Invite owner" |
| `organization-destroy` | delete any organization (§12.4) | Delete Organization on that tab: the organization they work in (§11.4); the Owner role starts with it (§7.1) |
| `channel-view` / `-store` / `-update` / `-destroy` | every channel; one made there is the platform's, offered to every organization (§6.6) | the organization's own channels, made there and offered to its own screens alone (§6.6) |
| `activity-view` | every entry (§17.4) | the entries carrying this organization's id (§17.4) |
| `user-view` / `user-destroy` (not `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED`) | every account — support sees organization accounts only (§13.1); delete an account (§13.2) | **never** — the accounts are the platform's; an organization's people are its Members page (owner's rule, 2026-09-17). `permissionsFor` refuses both on an organization's role (§7.6), an upgrade stripped the rows an older build had left (§22), and the `/users` group keeps `global-tier` whatever a role holds (§13) |
| `activity-destroy` | yearly maintenance (§17.5) | **never** — it drops every organization's history at once |
| `billing-update` (not `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED`) | the Premium Templates and Platform Channels switches of any organization, with `global-tier` (§12.1a) | **never** — an organization never unlocks itself (owner, 2026-10-08; tested, `BillingAttackTest`) |

- A controller answering an organization's role acts only on the **organization the person is working in** — `currentOrganization()` (404 unless they are a member of it, §6.1), the same organization whose role the route's gate has just read. No endpoint lets an organization's role act on another organization: the Organizations page and its writes keep the `global-tier` lock (§12), and a member works on another of their organizations by switching to it (§12.6) (owner's rule, 2026-09-17).
- *Adding a permission to `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` is done only once its controller answers an organization's role for that organization alone (convention).*

### 3.6 Capability-complete principle (owner convention)
A permission must be self-sufficient for its task: option lists feeding an action come with the action's own module, with minimal fields, never through another module's `-view`. Current examples:
- The invite and change-role pickers get their roles from `GET /members/data` (`assignable_roles`, under `member-view`) — never from the Roles page's `role-view`.
- The role form's checklist comes from `GET /roles/assignable` (under `role-view`).
- The playlist picker lists files through `/screens/{screen}/available-media` and channels through `/screens/{screen}/available-channels` (the platform's and the screen's own organization's, each marked `is_organization_channel`, §6.6), both under `screen-playlist`, never `media-view` / `channel-view`; the default-media picker uses `/screens/{screen}/media-options` (under `screen-update`); a channel's Add-ad pickers read `/channels/{channel}/library` (under `channel-update`, never `media-view`), answering only what a tile shows.
- The platform-staff invite picker's roles are rendered server-side into the Users page for super admins.
- The platform's Users → Organizations dialog gets the person's organizations, the organizations they could join, the organization roles and every organization's custom roles from `GET /users/{user}/organizations` (§13.4), never through the Roles page.
- The Organizations tab's "Your organizations" (every organization the person belongs to, with their role in each, §11.1) is rendered server-side into the page, never fetched through the Organizations page.
- The organizations listing carries each row's `can` (`update`, `destroy`, `billing`, `invite_owner`), so its buttons need nothing else (§12.1); the Billing dialog's summary comes from its own `GET /organizations/{organization}/billing` under `billing-view` (§12.1a).
- The dashboard's "Invitations for you" are rendered server-side into the dashboard, from the invitations to the person's own address (§18), never through the Members page.

### 3.7 Endpoint index (people model)
Every route below runs on the session + CSRF web stack, with `RejectMalformedText` in front of everything and `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization` and `RefuseAStaleTab` at the end of the web group (§3.3). `{user}`, `{organization}`, `{role}`, `{invitation}` and `{release}` segments are numeric-constrained: a non-numeric value matches no route (404). `{permission}` is not constrained but is still bound by id (404). The screens' device API (`routes/device.php`) is a separate stateless stack, out of scope, and so are the signage routes (`/screens`, `/media`, `/campaigns`, `/network-ads`, `/builder` (the Ad Builder, gated by `ad-*`), `/channels` — the last gated by `channel-*` alone, no tier lock, §6.6 — and the chunked uploads `/uploads`, behind `auth`, `verified`, `organization.active`, `throttle:uploads`). **There is no `routes/api.php`** (owner decision).

**The admin stack** = `auth`, `verified`, `organization.active`, `throttle:admin` — one route group, every signed-in page and request of the panel (*owner, 2026-09-29 and 2026-09-30: an account that has not confirmed its email, and a paused organization's people, reach none of it*).

| method + path | middleware (beyond the web stack) | § |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /` | — (redirects to `/login`) | |
| `GET /player-app/latest.json` | `throttle:player-app` — open: a television has no session | 16a |
| `GET /invitations/{token}` (token `[A-Za-z0-9]{64}`) | `throttle:invitation-response` | 9.5 |
| `POST /invitations/{token}/accept` | `throttle:invitation-response`, `auth` | 9.5 |
| `POST /invitations/{token}/register` | `throttle:invitation-response`, `guest` | 9.5 |
| `POST /invitations/{token}/decline` | `throttle:invitation-response` | 9.5 |

| `GET /login`, `POST /login` | `guest` | 19.3 |

| `GET /verify-email` ("Check your inbox") | `auth` | 10.1a |
| `GET /verify-email/{id}/{hash}` (`{id}` numeric; signed) | `auth`, `throttle:verification-link` | 10.1a |

| `GET /settings/organization` | the admin stack, `can:organization-view` | 11.1 |
| `PUT /settings/organization` | the admin stack, `can:organization-update` | 11.2 |

| `PUT /organizations/{organization}/billing` | the admin stack, `can:global-tier`, `can:billing-update` | 12.1a |
| `GET /roles`, `GET /roles/data`, `GET /roles/assignable` (optional `?role=` / `?type=`, §7.9) | the admin stack, `can:role-view` | 7 |
| `POST /roles` (on the platform with `type`, §7.4) / `PUT /roles/{role}` / `DELETE /roles/{role}` | the admin stack, `can:role-store` / `can:role-update` / `can:role-destroy` | 7 |
| `GET /activity`, `GET /activity/data` | the admin stack, `can:activity-view` | 17.4 |

| `GET /permissions`, `GET /permissions/data` | the admin stack, `can:super-admin-tier`, `can:permission-view` | 16 |
| `POST /permissions` / `PUT /permissions/{permission}` / `DELETE /permissions/{permission}` | the admin stack, `can:super-admin-tier`, `can:permission-store` / `-update` / `-destroy` | 16 |
| `GET /developer`, `GET /developer/app/releases/data` | the admin stack, `can:super-admin-tier`, `can:player-app-view` | 16a |
| `POST /developer/app/inspect`, `POST /developer/app/releases` | the admin stack, `can:super-admin-tier`, `can:player-app-store` | 16a |
| `DELETE /developer/app/releases/{release}` | the admin stack, `can:super-admin-tier`, `can:player-app-destroy` | 16a |
| `POST /impersonate/stop` | `auth`, `throttle:admin` — not `verified`: "Log in as" an account that has not confirmed must still have its way back | 14.1 |

`HttpSurfaceSweepTest` pins the doors a signed-in account keeps before it confirms its email — exactly the rows above with `auth` and no `verified`: `DELETE profile`, `DELETE profile/email`, `DELETE profile/organizations/{organization}`, `GET confirm-email/{id}/{hash}`, `GET profile`, `GET verify-email`, `GET verify-email/{id}/{hash}`, `PATCH profile`, `POST impersonate/stop`, `POST invitations/{token}/accept`, `POST logout`, `POST profile/email/resend`, `POST verify-email/confirm-for-them`, `POST verify-email/resend`, `PUT password` — so a new signed-in route outside `verified` fails that test until it is added on purpose.

Beyond the middleware, these endpoints **re-confirm the actor's password** inside the controller, all through one check with one shared wrong-password limit (§19.7): the big deletes `DELETE /members/{user}`, `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}`, `DELETE /organizations/{organization}`, `DELETE /users/{user}`, `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role`, `DELETE /roles/{role}` and `DELETE /permissions/{permission}` (and, among the signage routes, `DELETE /channels/{channel}`, `DELETE /campaigns/{campaign}` and `DELETE /builder/{ad}`), and the HTML forms `DELETE /settings/organization`, `DELETE /profile` and `PUT /password` (§11.4, §14.2, §19.5). (`POST /settings/organization/transfer-ownership` no longer exists — 404, §11.3.)

---

## 4. Identity rules

### 4.1 Super-Admin
**The authorization anchor is a name — `Role::SUPER_ADMIN = 'Super-Admin'` — and it is compared in application code, never in SQL.** *Reason (a real hole, now closed): MySQL's accent- and case-insensitive collation (`utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci`, the configured one) reads "Súper-Admin" as this very name, so a `WHERE name = 'Super-Admin'` let a look-alike custom role make every holder a super admin.*

- `Role.isSuperAdmin()` = `role.name == 'Super-Admin'` — exact string equality.
- `Role::superAdminId()` = the id of THE Super-Admin role: take the rows the database returns for `name = 'Super-Admin'` ordered by id (a folding collation may return look-alikes too) and keep the first whose name is exactly `Super-Admin`; null when there is none. Not memoized — one query per call.
- `User.isSuperAdmin()` = the user's **platform role** (§4.3) exists AND `isSuperAdmin()` — **only the organization-0 row counts** (Super-Admin is a platform role and the tiers are exclusive). Memoized per request.

```
user.isSuperAdmin() = user.globalRole() is not None and user.globalRole().name == 'Super-Admin'
```

Where the anchor is used: `Role::isSuperAdmin()` (exact name), `Role::superAdminId()` (exact name after the database narrows), `User::isSuperAdmin()` (through the platform role — and with it the before-hook that answers every permission check, §3.3), `User::primarySuperAdminId()`, `Role::scopeAvailableInOrganization`, `Role::scopeOrganizationRoles` and `Role::scopePlatform` (all four by that id, §4.2, §7.2), the users listing's per-row flag (exact compare on the platform row's role name, §13.1) and the seeder (by that id, §21). Consequences:
- The Super-Admin role is **the one role built in**: it cannot be renamed, edited or deleted through HTTP, from any context (on the platform 403 **"The Super-Admin role always holds every permission, and is never changed."**; inside an organization it is out of scope like any role that is not the organization's own — 404, §7.7). Its permission rows do not decide anything: its holders pass every permission check (§3.3).
- No other role may take a name that folds to Super-Admin's — accents, case, spacing and punctuation folded away (§7.5: **"That name belongs to the Super-Admin role. Choose another."**) — so no look-alike can be created or renamed into.
- A look-alike that exists anyway (written straight into the database) grants nothing (tested: an account holding a platform role named `super-admin` is not a super admin and gets 403 on `/permissions`, while `superAdminId()` and `primarySuperAdminId()` still point at the real role and person).
- **Migrations name the role only in PHP** (§22): the squashed set inserts the catalogue and the four starter organization roles, and the Super-Admin role is the seeder's alone (§21), which finds it by `Role::superAdminId()`; the two permission migrations since — billing's `2026_10_08_100300` and the TV app's `2026_10_10_100100` (§22.2b) — grant their permissions to Super-Admin when the role already exists, picking it out of the global roles (ordered by id) by the exact name in PHP. The upgrade migrations that once granted it permissions, or spared it an organization-role translation, compared the exact name in PHP for the same reason; a port writing its own data migration must do likewise.

### 4.2 Primary super admin
`primarySuperAdminId()` = with `role_id = superAdminId()` (§4.1): the `user_id` of the `organization_user` row holding that role with the **lowest pivot `id`** (earliest membership, not lowest user id); **null when there is no Super-Admin role or nobody holds it**. `isPrimarySuperAdmin() = user.id == primarySuperAdminId()`. Not memoized. Used by account deletion (§13.2), platform-role removal (§13.3), platform invitations and the invite picker (§9.3, §13.1) and the users listing flags (§13.1). On a fresh install the primary is the seeded admin (§21).

### 4.3 Platform role
`globalRole()` = the Role on the pivot row `(user_id, organization_id = 0)`, else null — a **raw pivot query** (organization 0 has no `organizations` row). "Platform account" = `globalRole() != null`. A super admin is also a platform account.

### 4.4 Role helpers
- `isOwner()` = `key == 'owner'` · `isSuperAdmin()` = `name == 'Super-Admin'` · `isGlobal()` = `is_global OR isSuperAdmin()` · `isOrganizationRole()` = `NOT isGlobal() AND organization_id IS NULL` · `isCustomRole()` = `NOT isGlobal() AND organization_id IS NOT NULL`. A role's **kind** (§7.1): `platform` when `isGlobal()`, else `organization` when `isOrganizationRole()`, else `custom` — payloads call Super-Admin's kind `super_admin`.
- `description()` — shown in pickers (Members, Users → Organizations), on the Roles page and on the invitation page. It is computed from what the role allows, so it stays true whatever the role is called or has been changed to:
  - the Super-Admin role → **"Full control of the platform: every organization, every account and the permission catalogue."**;
  - otherwise `labels` = the display names of the role's permissions in catalogue order (permission id), and `allows` = **"Allows nothing yet."** when there are none; **"Allows {labels}."** for one to three, joined with ", " and " and " before the last; **"Allows {first three labels joined with ', '} and {n − 3} more."** beyond three;
  - the Owner role → **"Owns the organization. {allows}"**; any other platform role → **"Platform team. {allows}"**; an organization role or a custom role → `{allows}`.

  Tested: Staff changed to `screen-view`, `screen-store` and `media-view` reads exactly "Allows View Screens, Pair Screens and View Media Library."
- `Role::starter(key)` = the role with that key, or 404 (`firstOrFail`) — while the installation still has it; `Role::owner()` = `Role::starter('owner')`. `Role::starterPermissions(key)` = the key's list in `Role::STARTERS` (§7.1), where a `null` list (Admin) means every **current** `Permission::ORGANIZATION` permission, plus `organization-view` (§26.11).

### 4.5 Per-request memoization (performance contract)
`isSuperAdmin()`, `globalRole()` (negative results too, via a "resolved" flag) and the per-context permission-name list are memoized **per user object per request**; an explicit refresh of the object clears all three. (`Role::superAdminId()` is not memoized: every role scope that uses it runs one extra query.) Tests assert that the users listing runs the **same number of queries** whatever the number of rows, and that the organization-selection page runs the same number for ten organizations as for two (`DashboardTest` — equality, since a ceiling could not catch one query per organization) — preserve "O(1) queries per repeated check".

---

## 5. Session organization context and permission resolution

### 5.1 `session.current_organization_id`
The organization an organization member is working in — always written as an **integer** organization id.
- **Written by:** `POST /organizations/switch` (§12.6); accepting an organization invitation — by link or on the dashboard, including the already-a-member case — or registering from one (§9.5, §9.5a); **`ChooseTheOnlyOrganization`**, on every request of the web group, for a signed-in person with no platform role who belongs to exactly one organization and has none chosen (QA round, 2026-10-09: an Owner who signed in from a link and landed on a screen was told "Your role does not allow it"); the dashboard's own auto-select, which does the same (§18).
- **Cleared by:** `POST /members/leave` (§8.6); `DELETE /profile/organizations/{organization}` when it is the organization being left (§8.6); `DELETE /settings/organization` (§11.4); accepting a platform invitation (§9.5); impersonation start and stop (§14.1); logout (the whole session is invalidated); and `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization`, when the session names an organization the person is no longer a member of (taken out by someone else, or the organization deleted) — after which a person left with one organization works in it at once.
- **Not cleared at the moment** someone else removes the member (a colleague, or a super admin from Users → Organizations) or deletes the organization: the stale id is forgotten at the start of the person's next request, as above. A platform account's session is never touched by `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization`, and an organization left there resolves to nothing anyway (§5.3). Tested (`ChooseTheOnlyOrganizationTest`): an Owner of one organization opens a screen from a link straight after signing in; a session naming an organization the person was taken out of is forgotten and the one left is chosen; the platform team is never put in an organization.

### 5.2 `currentRole()`
Null when the session value is 0/unset; otherwise the role on the pivot row `(user, current_organization_id)`, resolved **through the organizations relationship** — a missing (deleted) organization yields null.

### 5.3 Permission resolution (core algorithm)
```
platform_role = globalRole()
memo_key      = 'platform' if platform_role else 'organization:' + int(session.current_organization_id)
role          = platform_role if platform_role else (currentRole() if session.current_organization_id and pausedOrganization() is None else None)
names         = {p.name for p in role.permissions} if role else {}
allowed(permission) = permission in names
```
- **A paused organization grants its people nothing** (owner, 2026-09-30): while the organization of the session is one the person belongs to and its `is_active` is false (`pausedOrganization()`, §2.2), no role is read — so every gate and every `@can` answers no there, and `organization.active` sends them to the dashboard, which says why. A platform role is never affected.
- **For a super admin this list is never consulted by a permission check:** the before-hook of §3.3 answers yes first. The resolution above decides for everyone else. (The organization-scoped reads of `OrganizationTeam` — reach, pickers, the role checklist — read the membership in a real organization, which a super admin never has.)
- **A platform role always wins:** an organization id left in a platform account's session never swaps the platform role for no role (tested). *Reason: the tiers are exclusive — the platform team never works inside an organization.*
- An organization member with **no organization selected has zero permissions** — which is why switching organizations carries no permission gate, why `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization` chooses a lone organization on every request (§5.1), and why a page refused for somebody of several organizations who has chosen none sends them to the chooser instead of a 403 (§18).
- The same person holds different roles in different organizations → different powers per organization; switching flips them (tested: one person, Staff in Alpha and Admin in Beta — the members list is 403 in Alpha and 200 in Beta, showing Beta's people only).
- Losing a membership takes effect **on the next request** (checks re-derive from the pivot every request, subject only to the per-request memo).

---

## 6. Visibility and scoping

**The rule:** every write resolves its target **inside where the actor stands**, and an id outside it answers **404**. Route middleware alone is never enough. *Reason: IDOR — otherwise anyone holding e.g. `member-remove` in one organization could act on any id.*

### 6.1 `currentOrganization()` — the organization an organization page works in
`organization_id = int(session.current_organization_id)`; the organization = the organization with that id when > 0; **404 unless the organization exists AND the actor has an `organization_user` row for it** (no organization chosen, a deleted organization, or one the person has left or been removed from). Used by every `/members` endpoint, every `/members/invitations` endpoint, every `/settings/organization` endpoint (Create organization included, §10.2) and `GET /settings/billing` (after its own 404 for a platform account) — and, for an actor with no platform role, by the organization-scoped paths of the Channels and Activity Log pages (§6.6, §17.4). A platform account never has one (tested: a platform user holding `member-view` gets 404 from `GET /members/data`; one holding `organization-view` and `organization-update` gets 404 from `GET /settings/organization`) — a super admin neither: passing every permission gate (§3.3) still leaves these organization pages at 404.

### 6.2 The members directory (organization context)
Every membership of the current organization, **whoever brought them in**, the viewer included (marked `is_you`) — never anybody outside the organization and never a platform account (they have no real-organization rows). The organization's own open invitations only. This is where an organization's own people see each other: an organization has no accounts page of its own (§6.3, §13). Target resolution: a member → `OrganizationTeam::roleOf(target, organization)`, **404** when not a member (a member of another organization simply does not exist here — tested); an invitation → `invitation.organization_id == current organization id`, else **404**.

### 6.3 Accounts (§13)
The accounts pages are the **platform's alone** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: an organization's people are its Members page, §6.2): the whole `/users` group carries `global-tier` (§3.4), so an organization account gets **403** from every `/users` route whatever its role holds, and `user-view` / `user-destroy` never go on an organization's role (§3.2, §7.6). On the platform a **super admin** sees every account (themselves included) — whole, or one side of it with the Users page's tabs (owner, 2026-10-07: **All · Platform Team · Organization Members**, `group` = `platform` / `organization`, anything else all; Organization Members is everybody outside the platform team, an account in no organization included), each tab saying how many it holds (`counts`, §13.1). Any other platform account holding `user-view` sees **organization accounts only**: every account holding an organization-0 row is excluded (so they do not see themselves or any super admin), `group` is not read and no counts are sent. Write guard on `DELETE /users/{user}`: a non-super actor targeting an account that holds a platform role → **404**.

### 6.4 Organizations
The Organizations page and its writes are the platform's (`global-tier`, §12): the listing shows every organization and its writes take any organization; an organization account gets **403** from every `/organizations` route but the switch, whatever its role holds (tested). An organization member reaches their own organizations through the header switcher, the selection page and the switch endpoint, which answers 403 for a non-member (§12.6), and sees every organization they belong to under "Your organizations" — on the Organizations tab of Settings, or on the profile for whoever has no such tab (§11.1, §14.2). An organization's own people change the organization they work in — its details, deleting it, and opening another organization from it — on the Organizations tab of Settings, each by its own permission in that organization (§11, §10.2); an organization changes hands on its Members page (§11.3); no endpoint lets their role act on another of their organizations (leaving one, §8.6, and switching to it, §12.6, need no permission).

### 6.5 Roles
Where the viewer stands — `RoleController::context()`, asked first by every Roles endpoint (the page, the list, create, update, delete and the checklist):
1. the session organization exists AND the actor is a member of it → **inside that organization**;
2. else the actor is not a super admin → 403 **"Roles are managed inside an organization, or by a super admin."** (tested: a platform role holding `role-view` and `role-store` gets 403 on the list; a platform user holding `role-store` cannot create a role);
3. else → **on the platform** (a super admin is never a member of an organization, so an organization left in their session changes nothing).

No parameter moves the context (owner's rules, 2026-09-17: the old `organization` parameter and its "Roles of" organization picker are gone — the platform lists every organization's custom roles with the rest, §7.3). Inside an organization, another organization's custom role and a platform role answer **404** on update and delete (§7.7–§7.8). What each context sees and edits is in §7.3–§7.9.

**Role ids inside member and invitation payloads** are resolved differently: through `Role::availableInOrganization(current organization)` (an organization role, or this organization's custom role) → otherwise **422** on `role_id` **"Choose a role that exists in this organization."** (not 404) — the platform's Users → Organizations likewise, against the organization it adds the person to or names in its path (§13.4); for a platform invitation through `Role::platform()` → otherwise 422 **"Choose a platform role."**

### 6.6 Organization-scoped content (signage, summary)
Media and screens (with their playlists) are stamped with the organization from the session on create, and every read and write lookup goes through `visibleTo(actor)`: `actor.isSuperAdmin() OR actor.globalRole()` — i.e. any platform account, since a super admin is one (§4.1) → **unscoped** (every organization); otherwise no current organization → an **empty** result (`0 = 1`); otherwise `organization_id = current organization`. A row of another organization therefore answers 404. Membership of the current organization is enforced by the permission gate itself (a non-member has no role there → 403 before the scope runs).

**The platform's library** (docs/CHANNEL-CONTENT-SPEC.md, owner 2026-09-19): a media row may belong to no organization — `media.organization_id` NULL is the platform's own library, files under `media/platform/`, kept apart from every organization's by the same `organization_id = current organization` that scopes the rest, which never matches a NULL. Above the organizations the Media page reads one library at a time (`/media/data?library=platform` by default, or an organization's id) and an upload joins the one chosen (`organization_id` posted, none for the platform's). An organization member never lists, changes, deletes or plays a platform file (404 on update and delete, 422 on a playlist line — tested).

**Channels** (owner's rules, 2026-09-16) — two kinds by `channels.organization_id` (§2.9):
- `Channel::visibleTo(actor)` decides every **change**: a platform account → **every** channel; otherwise no current organization → empty; otherwise `organization_id = current organization` — so inside an organization every write to the platform's channels or another organization's answers **404** (the channel form request answers it before validation; the ads controller in a controller middleware, before an upload is even validated) (tested: update, delete, an ad added, the library picker).
- `Channel::listableIn(actor)` decides every **look** (owner, 2026-09-19): a platform account → every channel (the listing names each one's organization, or none for the platform's); inside an organization → its own channels **and the platform's**, the latter listed and opened read-only — `read_only` on the row ("From the platform", no Edit or Delete), the ads page without Add, Edit, the arrows or ×, the screen counts of that organization alone and no name of whoever on the platform made it — while another organization's answers **404** (tested).
- A channel's ads are **library rows**, held by `channel_ads.media_id` (required, cascades on delete): either chosen (`media_id` — a file, or a published Ad Builder page) or uploaded (`file`, which joins the channel's library first: the organization's for an organization's channel, the platform's for the platform's). An organization's channel takes its organization's rows only; the platform's channel its own library or any organization's; anything else → 422 on `media_id` **"Choose a file from this channel's library."** (tested). Taking an ad out, or deleting the channel, leaves the file in its library. A file a channel shows cannot be deleted — 422 on `file` **"Still used by the channel {name}. Take it out of that channel first."** (the plural names up to three and counts the rest) — nor the Ad Builder design whose published page it is (422 on `name`, before any password); the Media listing carries the same words (`in_channels_message`) so the page says them before any confirmation (tested).
- Create: above the organizations the channel is the platform's (`organization_id` NULL); inside an organization `currentOrganization()` (404) and the channel is stamped with it (tested).
- A screen's playlist may carry the platform's channels and **its own organization's** only: `Channel::availableTo(screen)` feeds the picker (an organization's own marked `is_organization_channel`), and a save naming another organization's channel → 422 on `items` **"One of those channels has been deleted, or is not offered to this organization. Reload the page to see the current list."** (tested).
- A name has to stand apart within what one organization's list shows: a platform channel's name among the platform's channels (**"There is already a channel with this name. Every organization sees the name, so it has to be different."**), an organization's channel's name among the platform's and that organization's own (**"There is already a channel with this name in this organization's list. Choose a different name."**) — so another organization's names never matter, and an organization's name never blocks the platform's (tested).
- Deleting an organization deletes its own channels (§15.4); a channel's entries in the activity log carry its organization (§17.1).

**Activity entries** carry the organization they belong to (§17.1); inside an organization the log reads that organization's entries only (§17.4).

### 6.7 Deliberate exceptions
- The impersonation target is looked up by raw id, unscoped (§14.1).
- The organization endpoints (`PUT`/`DELETE /organizations/{organization}`, `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation`) are the platform's alone (`global-tier`) and take any organization — the platform spans every organization.
- On the platform the Roles endpoints reach every role — every organization's custom roles included (§7.3) — and Users → Organizations takes any organization: adding the person to it, or changing or ending a membership they hold there (§13.4).
- `POST /invitations/{token}/decline` finds the invitation by token whatever its state (§9.5).

---

## 7. Roles

*Owner's rules, 2026-09-17: no role is built in but Super-Admin. The super admin makes a role and says what it is for — an **organization role**, offered in every organization, or a **platform role** for the team above the organizations — and renames it, changes what it allows and deletes it while nobody holds it; what a role is for is fixed once it exists. The Owner role is an organization role like the others, except that it marks who owns an organization and is never deleted. Organizations make custom roles of their own, as before.*

### 7.1 Role kinds and the starter organization roles (`Role::STARTERS`)
| kind (`kind` in payloads) | what it is | who makes and changes it |
|---|---|---|
| Super-Admin (`super_admin`) | the platform's top role, by its exact name (§4.1) | the seeder (§21); never renamed, edited or deleted through HTTP (§7.7) |
| Organization role (`organization`) | not global, no organization: offered in **every** organization; what it allows reaches the member's own organization | a super admin on the platform (`type: organization`, §7.4); renamed and changed for every organization at once (§7.7); deleted while nobody holds it (§7.8); read-only inside an organization |
| — the Owner role | the organization role with `key = 'owner'` (`Role::owner()`, `Role::isOwner()` — never found by its name): whoever holds it owns that organization | renamed and changed like any organization role, its key kept; **never deleted** (§7.8) |
