| Platform role (`platform`) | global: held on the `organization_id = 0` row; what it allows reaches every organization | a super admin on the platform (`type: platform`, §7.4) |
| Custom role (`custom`) | belongs to one organization and is offered there alone | a member holding `role-store` inside that organization (§7.4); changed and deleted by any member of that organization holding `role-update` / `role-destroy` within reach, and by a super admin from the platform (§7.7, §7.8) |

**The starter organization roles** — where an installation begins; after that they are ordinary organization roles:

| key | name | permissions to start with |
|---|---|---|
| owner | Owner | every `ORGANIZATION` permission, `organization-view` and `organization-destroy` |
| admin | Admin | every `ORGANIZATION` permission and `organization-view` (a `null` list in the code) |
| staff | Staff | `screen-view`, `screen-playlist`, `media-view`, `media-store`, `media-update`, `media-destroy` |
| viewer | Viewer | `screen-view`, `media-view` |

- **A starter key gives no permission.** `owner` marks the Owner role; `owner` and `admin` also pick a badge colour on the Members and Users pages (§20.6) — nothing else reads `admin`, `staff` or `viewer`. Admin, Staff and Viewer are renamed, changed and deleted like any organization role, and nothing brings a deleted one back (§21).
- **The starting permissions are written once**, when a starter role is created — by the starter-roles migration `2026_10_01_202300_insert_permissions_and_starter_roles`, which creates all four on a fresh install with its own copy of the lists (§22), or by the seeder, which creates the Owner role alone when it is missing (§21) — and never re-applied afterwards: re-seeding keeps what the super admin changed (tested). The migration writes the lists above literally; `Role::starterPermissions` (read by the seeder and the tests) computes Admin's from a `null` list, meaning "every `Permission::ORGANIZATION` permission, and `organization-view`" at the moment it is asked. A permission added later reaches Owner and Admin only through a migration that grants it to them by key — as billing's did for `billing-view`, now in `Permission::ORGANIZATION` (§22.2b) — or the super admin's edit (§26.11).
- With the starting permissions, the difference between Owner and Admin is `organization-destroy` (deleting the organization, §11.4) — a permission, so the super admin may take it from the Owner role or give it to another role (owner's rules, 2026-09-16 and 2026-09-17: a role does not matter, its permissions do). Because Admin lacks it, the Owner role lies outside every Admin's reach: an Admin can neither give it (so no Admin hands an organization over, §11.3) nor change or remove an Owner (§8.1). **Nothing is tied to the Owner role's key but ownership itself** — who counts as an Owner ("at least one Owner", §8.2) and the role signup, owner invitations and a newly opened organization give (§10) — and no rename or permission edit moves that. What reach (§8.1) and the gated pages allow follows the permission rows actually held.

### 7.2 Role sets
- `availableInOrganization(organization_id)`: `is_global = false AND id != superAdminId() (when a Super-Admin role exists) AND (organization_id IS NULL OR organization_id = organization_id)` — every organization role and that organization's own custom roles: what a member of that organization may hold. The Super-Admin role is excluded **by id**, never by a name comparison (§4.1).
- `organizationRoles()`: `is_global = false AND organization_id IS NULL AND id != superAdminId()` (the last part only when a Super-Admin role exists) — the organization roles, the Owner role among them.
- `OrganizationTeam::rolesOf(organization)` — picker order: the Owner role, then the other organization roles, then the organization's own custom roles, each group by lower-cased name; permissions loaded.
- `Role::platform()`: `is_global = true OR id = superAdminId()` (the second part only when a Super-Admin role exists).

### 7.3 `GET /roles/data` (`role-view`)
After `context()` (§6.5), returns **one list** `{roles: [...]}` — **not paginated**:
- **inside an organization:** `availableInOrganization(organization)` — the organization roles and this organization's own custom roles;
- **on the platform:** every role — Super-Admin, the organization roles, the platform roles and **every organization's custom roles**.

Order: by position — Super-Admin 0, the Owner role 1, the other organization roles 2, platform roles 3, custom roles 4 — then by the lower-cased name of a custom role's organization, then by the lower-cased role name.

- Row shape: `{id, name, kind, is_owner_role, organization_name, description, holders_count, invitations_count, permissions: [{id, name, label}], can_edit, can_delete}` — `kind` ∈ `super_admin` / `organization` / `platform` / `custom` (§4.4); `is_owner_role` = `isOwner()`; `organization_name` = a custom role's organization name, null for every other kind; `description` per §4.4; permissions sorted by name, each `label` being its display name.
- `holders_count` (integer, never null) = the number of `organization_user` rows holding the role — **inside an organization, that organization's rows only** (a Staff member of another organization is not counted — tested), on the platform every row (every organization and the sentinel).
- `invitations_count` (integer) = the open invitations to the role, expired ones included — on the platform over every organization's invitations, inside an organization over **this organization's alone**, like `holders_count`. Tested: an invitation to Staff in Alpha and two in another organization read 1 inside Alpha and 3 on the platform.
- `can_edit` / `can_delete`:
  - **on the platform** (the actor is a super admin, §6.5): `can_edit` = the role is not Super-Admin; `can_delete` = not Super-Admin AND not the Owner role — whatever the kind (an organization's custom role included) and **whether or not anybody holds it**: a held role offers Delete too, and deleting it says it must be unassigned first (§7.8, owner's rule 2026-09-17);
  - **inside an organization:** an organization role → both false (organization roles are the super admin's, §7.7); this organization's custom role → with `within_reach = roleIsWithinReach(actor's permissions in the organization, role)` (§8.1), `can_edit = within_reach AND actor.can('role-update')` and `can_delete = within_reach AND actor.can('role-destroy')` — held or not.
- Tested: in an organization (a platform role and another organization's custom role existing) the names read `Owner, Admin, Staff, Viewer, Cashier`, the four `organization` rows carry no edit or delete, Owner's `is_owner_role` is true, Cashier is `custom`, Staff counts 0 and Admin 1 in this organization; on the platform the first five names read `Super-Admin, Owner, Admin, Staff, Viewer`, Super-Admin can be neither edited nor deleted, Owner edited but not deleted, Viewer (held by nobody) edited and deleted, Admin (held in an organization) offered Delete too — deleting it with the password answers 422 exactly "Please unassign Admin from everyone first: 1 person still holds it." and Admin stays; a custom role of Beta Deli reads `kind: "custom"`, `organization_name: "Beta Deli"`, editable and deletable.
- `GET /roles` renders the page with `organization` = the organization the viewer is inside (null on the platform); `context()`'s 403 applies to the page too (browser-tested: a platform team member who is not a super admin sees the 403 page, §20.8).

### 7.4 Create — `POST /roles` (`role-store`), exact order
1. `context()` → 403 (§6.5). The kind of the new role: **inside an organization**, a custom role of that organization (a `type` sent is not read); **on the platform**, the `type` sent — `organization` or `platform`.
2. Validation (§7.5) → 422 field errors — on the platform `type` included (while `type` is missing or invalid the name is compared with no list: only the Super-Admin rule applies to it).
3. `permissionsFor(ids, kind, the organization the actor works in)` (§7.6) → 422 on `permissions`.
4. In one transaction: create `{name, key: NULL, organization_id: the organization (inside an organization) or NULL (on the platform), is_global: (kind == platform), created_by: actor}` and set its permissions exactly.
5. After commit: log `role.created` (subject = the role, so a custom role's entry carries its organization, §17.1) — **"Created organization role {name}, offered in every organization"** / **"Created platform role {name}"** / "Created role {name} in {organization}" (a custom role); **201** **"Role {name} created."**

Tested: on the platform without `type` → 422 on `type` **"Choose what this role is for."**; "Floor Lead" as `organization` with `screen-view` + `channel-view` → 201, not global, no organization, offered in a brand-new organization's `availableInOrganization`; "Support" as `platform` with `user-view` + `activity-destroy` → 201, global, not offered in an organization; the two log lines exactly as quoted; "Auditor" as `organization` with `activity-destroy`, or with `user-view`, → 422 on `permissions`; inside an organization a supervisor creates "Helper" (its `organization_id` = the organization) but not "Uploader" with `media-store`, which they do not hold (422), and an Owner cannot create "Snoop" with `activity-view` (422). Browser-tested: the form opens with "Organization role" chosen, and saving toasts "Role Shift Lead created." / "Role Support created.".

### 7.5 Name, type and payload validation (create and update)
- `type` — **create on the platform only**: required, one of `organization`, `platform` → **"Choose what this role is for."** (both the required and the in message). Never read on update: what a role is for is fixed once it exists (*its holders are already on one side or the other*).
- `name`: required, string, max 255 — **not unique in the database** — then, in this order:
  1. `fold(name) == fold('Super-Admin')` (= `superadmin`) → 422 on `name`: **"That name belongs to the Super-Admin role. Choose another."** — for every kind, on create and on rename.
  2. **The name must be new to every list the role is shown in** (skipped on a create whose `type` is missing or invalid — there is no list yet; tested: `Owner` with `type` = `everywhere` → 422 on `type` only). The first role of that list — the role being edited excluded — whose folded name equals `fold(name)` is a clash:
     - a **platform** role is checked against `Role::platform()` (Super-Admin and the platform roles);
     - an **organization** role against every non-global role: every organization role and **every organization's custom roles** (an organization role is listed in all of them);
     - a **custom** role against `availableInOrganization(its organization)`: the organization roles and that organization's own custom roles.

     The message: saving an organization role whose name an organization's custom role has → **"{that custom role's organization name} already has a custom role called {clash name}. Choose another name."**; saving a custom role whose name an organization role has → **"There is already an organization role called {clash name}, and every organization has it. Choose another name."**; any other clash → **"There is already a role called {clash name}. Choose another name."**

  `fold` = transliterate to ASCII (`Súper` → `Super`, `Ä` → `A`), lower-case, then remove every character outside `a-z0-9`. *Reasons: a name must mean one role in every picker it is shown in ("Súper Admin", "O-W-N-E-R" or " STAFF " cannot pose as another role), and the Super-Admin anchor must not be reachable by a look-alike (§4.1).* Consequences: organizations' custom roles may share a name with each other, a platform role may share a name with an organization role or a custom role, and a rename that only changes the capitals, spacing or punctuation of the role's own name is allowed.

  Tested: inside an organization `Owner`, `admin`, ` STAFF ` and `Viewer` are refused on create (the organization roles are in every organization's list); `cashier` beside a custom `Cashier` → exactly "There is already a role called Cashier. Choose another name."; another organization creates its own `Cashier` (201); on the platform an organization role `Cashier` → exactly "Alpha Mart already has a custom role called Cashier. Choose another name.", while a platform role `Cashier` is created (201); `Súper-Admin`, `SUPER ADMIN` and `super_admin` → exactly "That name belongs to the Super-Admin role. Choose another." on create, and 422 on renaming a custom role (the name unchanged); `Ówner`, `O-W-N-E-R` and `Ädmin` are refused on create; `Organization Admin Assistant` is allowed.
- `permissions`: required, array, min 1 → **"Choose at least one permission."** (both the required and the min message); each element integer, distinct. Not an array, or an element that is not an integer or is repeated → **"Choose the permissions from the list."** (*the checklist sends ids it listed: anything else came from a request made by hand* — `ValidationWordsTest`).
- An update validates `name` and `permissions` for every role it may change, whatever its kind (§7.7).

### 7.6 `permissionsFor(ids, kind, memberOrganization)` — in this order, all 422 keyed `permissions`
`kind` = the kind of the role being written (organization / platform / custom); `memberOrganization` = the organization the actor works in — set only for a member inside an organization, none on the platform (a super admin changing an organization's custom role from the platform included).
1. any id that is not an existing permission → **"One of those permissions does not exist."**
2. any `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` permission — **on every kind** → when every one of them is a catalogue permission (`permission-*`): **"Permission management belongs to the Super-Admin role alone."** (tested exactly on a new platform role, a new organization role and the Owner role; 422 on a custom role inside an organization); otherwise they are named: **"{labels} belongs to the Super-Admin role alone."** for one, **"{labels} belong to the Super-Admin role alone."** for several — `{labels}` their display names joined with ", " and " and " before the last (tested exactly: "Upload TV App belongs to the Super-Admin role alone." for `player-app-store` on a new platform role, `PlayerAppReleaseTest`).
3. **an organization role or a custom role** (kind ≠ platform): the permissions that are not `belongsToOrganizations` (§3.2) → **"An organization role cannot hold {labels}: it works above the organizations only."** — `{labels}` = their display names joined with ", " and " or " before the last — `user-view`, `user-destroy` and `activity-destroy` among them (tested exactly: "An organization role cannot hold Delete Old Activity Logs: it works above the organizations only." for `activity-destroy` on Owner and on Staff; "An organization role cannot hold Delete Accounts: it works above the organizations only." for `user-destroy` on the Owner role; "An organization role cannot hold View Accounts: it works above the organizations only." for `user-view` on a custom role an Owner creates inside an organization — this step answers before step 4; a permission made on the Permissions page on Staff → 422).
4. **inside an organization** (`memberOrganization` set): any permission the actor does not hold in this organization → **"You can only give a role permissions you hold yourself."** (tested exactly: an Owner giving `activity-view`, `channel-view` or `channel-store`, none of which the Owner role starts with; 422 for a supervisor giving `media-store`).

Otherwise the set is accepted: a platform role takes organization, platform and uncatalogued permissions alike; an organization role — or a custom role changed from the platform — anything that passed step 3 (tested: the super admin gives the Owner role `organization-view`, `organization-store`, `channel-view`, `channel-store` and `activity-view` on top of its starting permissions; an Owner, who holds `organization-destroy`, creates a custom role "Closer" with `organization-destroy` and `organization-update`).

*Reason: privilege escalation — otherwise a member could mint a stronger role and hand it to a puppet.* The UI checklist (§7.9) only mirrors this.

### 7.7 Update — `PUT /roles/{role}` (`role-update`), exact order
1. `context()` → 403 (§6.5).
2. `ensureManageable(role, context, deleting = false)` — shared with delete (§7.8); it also yields the role's kind:
   - (a) **on the platform**, the role is Super-Admin (`isSuperAdmin()`) → 403 **"The Super-Admin role always holds every permission, and is never changed."** — before anything else;
   - (b) **on the platform:** when deleting, the Owner role → 403 (§7.8); otherwise every role is manageable — an organization role (the Owner role included), a platform role, or any organization's custom role;
   - (c) **inside an organization:** an organization role → 403 **"Organization roles are changed by the super admin, for every organization at once."**; not this organization's custom role (a platform role — Super-Admin included —, another organization's custom role) → **404**; not within the actor's reach (§8.1) → 403 **"You cannot change a role that has access you do not have."**
3. A custom role's organization is looked up → 404 when it is missing (which cannot happen: a custom role goes with its organization — the purge of §15.4 deletes it, and `roles.organization_id` cascades, §2.3).
4. Validation (§7.5): `name` — clash-checked against the lists the role is shown in, itself excluded — and `permissions`; a `type` sent is not read.
5. `permissionsFor(ids, kind, the organization the actor works in — none on the platform)` (§7.6) → 422 on `permissions`.
6. In one transaction: update the name, and set the permissions exactly.
7. After commit: log `role.updated` (subject = the role, so a custom role's entry carries its organization), where `{was}` = " (was {old name})" when the name changed, else empty:
   - an organization role → **"Updated organization role {name}{was}, in every organization"**;
   - a platform role → **"Updated platform role {name}{was}"**;
   - a custom role changed on the platform → **"Updated role {name}{was} in {organization}, from the platform"**;
   - a custom role changed inside its organization → "Updated role {name}{was}".
8. 200 **"Role {name} updated in every organization."** for an organization role (the Owner role included), else **"Role {name} updated."**

Tested: the super admin renames Staff to "Cashier" with `screen-view`, `screen-store`, `media-view` → 200 exactly "Role Cashier updated in every organization.", the name Cashier, exactly those three permissions, the description "Allows View Screens, Pair Screens and View Media Library." (§4.4) and the log line exactly "Updated organization role Cashier (was Staff), in every organization"; `activity-destroy` on it → 422 (§7.6); the Owner role given `organization-view`, `organization-store`, `channel-view`, `channel-store`, `activity-view` → 200, but `activity-destroy`, `user-destroy` or `permission-view` → 422 (§7.6); the Owner role renamed "Proprietor" → 200, still the Owner role, its holder still an Owner; Super-Admin → 403; Beta Deli's custom role "Cashier" renamed "Till" from the platform → log exactly "Updated role Till (was Cashier) in Beta Deli, from the platform"; inside an organization an Admin renames a custom role the Owner made (logged), a supervisor lacking one of a role's permissions → 403, and an organization role → 403 (Viewer keeps its name). Browser-tested: editing Staff shows its name editable and no type choice, and renaming it "Crew" with `screen-store` added toasts "Role Crew updated in every organization." and shows the new name in its row.

Any member holding `role-update` manages a custom role of their organization **whoever created it**, and a super admin manages every role but Super-Admin from the platform; a role survives its creator. Changing a role's permissions changes its holders' powers on their next request — an organization role's holders in every organization at once. *(The role form keeps ticked, when its checklist loads, only the permissions the checklist lists — a permission the role cannot hold never stays ticked out of sight, §20.8.)*

### 7.8 Delete — `DELETE /roles/{role}` (`role-destroy`), exact order
1. `context()` → 403 (§6.5).
2. `ensureManageable(role, context, deleting = true)` (§7.7 step 2): on the platform Super-Admin → 403 "The Super-Admin role always holds every permission, and is never changed." and the Owner role → 403 **"The Owner role is never deleted: it is how an organization has an owner. Rename it or change what it allows instead."**; inside an organization an organization role → 403 "Organization roles are changed by the super admin, for every organization at once."; then the 404 (Super-Admin among them inside an organization) / reach checks. **Neither Super-Admin nor the Owner role is ever deleted**, whatever the password (tested: both from the platform, the Owner role with that exact message) — every other role may be, the other starter roles included (tested: the super admin deletes Viewer, held by nobody; inside an organization Staff → 403 with exactly the organization-role message).
3. `holders` = number of `organization_user` rows holding the role (any organization, sentinel included) > 0 → 422 message-only **"Please unassign {name} from everyone first: {n} {person|people} still {holds|hold} it."** — e.g. "Please unassign Cashier from everyone first: 1 person still holds it." / "Please unassign Cashier from everyone first: 3 people still hold it." — answered **whatever the password** (tested: exactly "Please unassign Held from everyone first: 1 person still holds it." inside an organization with no password sent, and "Please unassign Admin from everyone first: 1 person still holds it." on the platform with the password). *The page offers Delete on a held role anyway and shows these same words at once, before any password is typed (§20.8).*
4. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
5. In one transaction: delete every open invitation to the role (`n` = how many, expired ones included), then delete the role (its permission links cascade). *Reason: the foreign key would take those invitations anyway — revoking them in the open lets the log and the person deleting both say what happened to them.*
6. `note` = when `n > 0`: " {n} pending invitation[s] to it {was|were} revoked." (else empty). Log `role.deleted` (subject NULL; organization = the role's `organization_id` — a custom role's organization, none for an organization role or a platform role) with **"Deleted role {name}.{note}"**, from any context; 200 **"Role {name} deleted.{note}"** — tested exactly: "Role Cashier deleted. 2 pending invitations to it were revoked." and the log line "Deleted role Cashier. 2 pending invitations to it were revoked."; an organization's custom role deleted from the platform → the entry carries that organization (tested). Browser-tested: a platform role deleted through its password dialog toasts "Role Support deleted.".

*(An earlier build also served `GET /roles/{role}/permissions`, scoped the same way. It is **gone**, with `RoleController::permissions` — the route answers 404 — because the roles payload of §7.3 already carries every role's permissions and nothing ever called it.)*

### 7.9 `GET /roles/assignable` (`role-view`)
*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17: the checklist lists only what the role in the form can hold — never a greyed-out checkbox.* After `context()` (§6.5), the kind the list is for:
- **inside an organization** → a custom role of that organization (`role` and `type` are not read);
- **on the platform** → the kind of the role named by `role` (the id of the role being edited, looked up with no scoping; a missing or unknown id counts as none); otherwise `type=platform` → a platform role, and anything else (`type=organization`, or no `type`) → an organization role.

What is listed:
- for a **platform role** → every permission row except `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` — uncatalogued ones included;
- for an **organization role, or a custom role edited on the platform** → every permission row that `belongsToOrganizations` (§3.2);
- **inside an organization** → the permissions the member holds in this organization that `belongsToOrganizations`.

A plain array ordered by name of `{id, name, label}` (`label` = the display name) — there is no `unavailable` field: a permission the role cannot hold is simply not listed. It feeds the role-form checklist; the form sends `role` when it opens for an existing role and `type` for a new role on the platform, and loads the list again when that type changes (§20.8).

Tested: editing Staff → exactly the `belongsToOrganizations` rows, with no `unavailable` key; editing Owner → `organization-destroy`, `channel-store`, `activity-view` and `media-view` listed, `user-view`, `user-destroy`, `activity-destroy` and `permission-view` not; `type=platform` → every row but the seven `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` ones (`activity-destroy`, `activity-view`, `channel-view`, `member-invite`, `user-view` and a permission made on the Permissions page listed; `permission-update` and `permission-view` not); `type=organization` → `channel-view` listed, `user-view` and `activity-destroy` not; a permission made on the Permissions page is not listed for Staff; inside an organization the Owner is offered `organization-destroy` but not `channel-view`, `user-view` or `activity-destroy`, and a supervisor holding `role-view`, `screen-view` and `media-view` exactly those three.

---

## 8. Team hierarchy (`OrganizationTeam`)

### 8.1 Primitives
- `roleOf(user, organization)` — the role on the `(organization, user)` pivot row, or null. `isMember(user, organization)` — that row exists.
- `permissionsOf(user, organization)` — the permission names of `roleOf`, empty when not a member.
- `isOwner(user, organization)` — `roleOf(...).key == 'owner'`. `ownerCount(organization)` — rows in the organization whose role key is `owner`.
- `roleIsWithinReach(actor_permissions, role)`: **every permission of the role is in `actor_permissions`** — whatever the role; the Owner role gets no special case (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: a role does not matter, its permissions do).
- `mayAssign(actor, organization, role)` (may give this role here — invite or change role): the role is in `availableInOrganization(organization)` AND `roleIsWithinReach(permissionsOf(actor, organization), role)`.
- `mayManage(actor, organization, member)` (may change or remove this member): `actor != member` AND `roleOf(member, organization)` exists AND `roleIsWithinReach(permissionsOf(actor, organization), roleOf(member, organization))`. The Owner count is not read here: the Members page asks it on its own (§8.2, §8.4, §8.5).
- `organizationsSolelyOwnedBy(user)` — the organizations where the user holds Owner and exactly one Owner row exists; ordered by name.
- `membershipsOf(user)` — the organizations the person belongs to, ordered by name, each as `{organization, role (null when unresolvable), is_sole_owner}` (`is_sole_owner` = the organization is among `organizationsSolelyOwnedBy(user)`) — what "Your organizations" lists (§11.1, §14.2).
- `changeTeam(organization, change)` — run `change` in a transaction after `SELECT id FROM organizations WHERE id = ? FOR UPDATE`, and return its result. *Reason: two co-owners demoting, removing or leaving at the same moment would otherwise both pass the "at least one Owner" test on what they read before either wrote, and the organization would be left with none — so the facts a change depends on must be read INSIDE `change`.*
- `lockOrganizationsOwnedBy(user)` — inside a transaction: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (ordered by id) every organization where the user holds the Owner role, before deciding whether they may go.
- `mayLeave(user, organization)` = NOT (`isOwner(user, organization)` AND `ownerCount(organization) == 1`).
- `leave(user, organization)` → bool: inside `changeTeam(organization)`: `mayLeave` false → return false and change nothing; else delete the membership row and return true.

Consequences, with the starter roles holding their starting permissions (§7.1): an **Admin** (every organization permission and `organization-view`) may give Admin, Staff, Viewer and any other role of the organization holding only permissions Admin holds, and may manage the members holding those roles — never the Owner role or an Owner, because the Owner role carries `organization-destroy`, nor a role carrying another permission Admin lacks (tested for giving Owner, touching an Owner and managing Staff). An **Owner** may do everything within the permissions the Owner role holds — so a role carrying a platform permission the Owner role lacks (say `channel-store` — an organization role the super admin made, or a custom role the super admin changed from the platform, §7.4, §7.7) is beyond every Owner's reach in that organization: they can neither give it, nor edit or delete it (a custom role), nor change or remove its holders (§26.13). Reach always compares the permission rows actually held, so a super admin's change to an organization role — the Owner role included — changes these outcomes in every organization (§26.11). A **custom role** reaches only roles inside its own permission set (tested: a supervisor holding `member-update`, `screen-view`, `media-view` cannot give Staff — 422, Staff can upload — nor change a Staff member — 403 — but can move a Viewer to a "Helper" role holding only `screen-view`). **Reach reads permissions alone:** a member who is not an Owner but whose role holds every permission the Owner role holds — Admin given `organization-destroy` by the super admin, or a custom role an Owner made with all of the Owner role's permissions — may give the Owner role, and change or remove Owners — never an organization's only Owner (§8.2; tested with a custom "Deputy" role holding all of the Owner role's permissions, §8.4). Giving the Owner role is how an organization changes hands (§11.3).

### 8.2 At least one Owner, always
- **Change role / remove on the Members page** (§8.4, §8.5): the organization's only Owner is refused — **"{name} is the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner first."** — asked on its own, apart from reach: *reach reads permissions alone (§8.1), so a member who is not an Owner but whose role holds every permission of the Owner role reaches an Owner, and only the Owner count keeps the last one.* Removing asks it before the password and again inside `changeTeam`; changing a role asks it inside `changeTeam`; and the members list never offers that row (`can_manage` false, §8.3).
- **Leave:** refused for the last Owner, inside `changeTeam` (§8.6). **Self-delete:** refused while the sole Owner of any organization, with those organizations locked (§14.2). **An organization changing hands** is ordinary Members-page work — the new Owner is given the Owner role first, and only then is the old one's role changed, or the old one removed or leaving (§11.3) — so these same refusals keep an Owner in between.
- **The platform's Users → Organizations** (§13.4) refuses to take the Owner role from an organization's last Owner — by changing their role or by taking them out of the organization — re-checked inside `changeTeam`.
- Each of these paths locks the organization row(s) first and reads the owners under the lock — concurrent requests are serialized per organization.
- **By design, an organization can still be ownerless:** when the platform deletes its only Owner's account (§13.2 — the response's `ownerless_organizations` and the log line name those organizations), and an organization created from the platform has no member at all until its Owner invitation is accepted (§10.2). The platform's "Invite owner" — offered for exactly such an organization, one with no Owner (§10.3) — or Users → Organizations putting somebody in with the Owner role (§13.4), is the repair.

### 8.3 `GET /members/data` (`member-view`)
After `currentOrganization()` (404):
```
{
  members:     [{id, name, email, role, joined_at, is_you, can_manage}],   // ordered by first_name, last_name
  invitations: [{id, email, role, invited_by, expires_at, is_expired, can_manage}],  // newest first
  assignable_roles: [role, ...],                                          // picker order (§7.2)
  owner_count: int
}
role = {id, name, key, description} or null
```
- `members`: every `organization_user` row of the organization joined to its user; `joined_at` = the membership's `created_at` as ISO-8601 (or null); `role` = the payload of the membership's role if it is in `rolesOf(organization)`, else null.
- `can_manage` (member) = the row is not the viewer AND the row's role (from `rolesOf`) is within the viewer's reach AND NOT (that role is the Owner role AND `owner_count == 1`) — the client mirror of `mayManage` and of the last-Owner refusal (§8.2), so the organization's only Owner is never offered to anybody (tested: the viewer's own row false; an Owner's row false for an Admin; a Staff row true for an Admin; an Admin's row true for an Owner; the only Owner's row false for a "Deputy" whose custom role holds every permission of the Owner role, and true once a second Owner exists).
- `owner_count` = `ownerCount(organization)` — the same count `can_manage` reads.
- `invitations`: this organization's open invitations (expired ones included); `invited_by` = the inviter's name or null; `expires_at` ISO-8601; `can_manage` = the invitation's role is within the viewer's reach (an Owner invitation is not manageable by an Admin — tested).
- `assignable_roles` = `rolesOf(organization)` filtered by the viewer's reach (tested: an Admin gets `[admin, staff, viewer]`, an Owner `[owner, admin, staff, viewer]`, plus this organization's custom roles within reach; another organization's custom roles never).

### 8.4 Change a member's role — `PUT /members/{user}` (`member-update`), exact order
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. `roleOf(target, organization)` → **404** when the target is not a member of this organization (pre-check, kept outside the lock for the status order).
3. Validate `role_id`: required, integer → 422.
4. `role = availableInOrganization(organization).find(role_id)` → null → 422 on `role_id` **"Choose a role that exists in this organization."**
5. Target is the actor → 403 **"You cannot change your own role."**
6. Inside `changeTeam(organization)` — decided under the organization's lock, so a co-owner changing roles at this very moment is seen:
   1. `current = roleOf(target, organization)` → 404 when the target has meanwhile left;
   2. `NOT mayManage(actor, organization, target)` → 403 **"You cannot change the role of someone who has access you do not have."** — an Owner's row included (no message of its own);
   3. `NOT mayAssign(actor, organization, role)` → 422 on `role_id` **"You can only give a role whose access you have yourself."** — the Owner role included;
   4. `current` is the Owner role AND the new role is not AND `ownerCount(organization) == 1` → 422 on `role_id` **"{name} is the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner first."** (§8.2);
   5. if the role differs from `current`: update the pivot row's `role_id`.
7. After commit, when the role changed: log `member.role_changed` (subject = the member; organization = this organization, §17.1). An unchanged role writes nothing and logs nothing.
8. 200 **"{name} is now {role}."** (returned in both cases).

*Status pattern: who the actor is relative to the target → 403; the requested role, or the last Owner it would take away → 422.* Note the order: an invalid or foreign role id answers 422 even when the actor targets themselves.

Tested: a "Deputy" — a custom role holding every permission of the Owner role — changing the organization's only Owner to Admin → 422 on `role_id` with exactly "{name} is the only Owner of Alpha Mart. Make someone else an Owner first.", the Owner kept; once a partner Owner exists, the same change → 200, the first Owner now Admin and the partner still Owner. This endpoint is also how an organization changes hands (§11.3).

### 8.5 Remove a member — `DELETE /members/{user}` (`member-remove`), exact order
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404. 2. `roleOf(target, organization)` → 404 when not a member (pre-check).
3. Target is the actor → **422** message-only **"To leave this organization, use Leave Organization."**
4. `NOT mayManage(actor, organization, target)` → 403 **"You cannot remove someone who has access you do not have."** — an Owner included.
5. The target holds the Owner role AND `ownerCount(organization) == 1` → **422** message-only **"{name} is the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner first."** (§8.2). Steps 4 and 5 are pre-checks, so nobody types a password for a refused removal.
6. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
7. Inside `changeTeam(organization)`: `roleOf(target, organization)` again → 404; `NOT mayManage` again → 403 with the same message; the last-Owner refusal of step 5 again → 422 with the same message; delete the membership row only (§15.2).
8. After commit: log `member.removed` (subject = the member; organization = this organization); 200 **"{name} was removed from {organization}."**

Tested: the "Deputy" of §8.4 removing the organization's only Owner, the password sent → 422 with exactly "{name} is the only Owner of Alpha Mart. Make someone else an Owner first.", the Owner kept.

### 8.6 Leave an organization
Any member may leave any organization they belong to, whatever their role — except its last Owner. Two entry points share `OrganizationTeam::leave` (§8.1):

**`POST /members/leave`** (the current organization; the admin stack, no permission gate — open in a paused organization, §2.2):
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. `leave(actor, organization)` returns false (the actor is its only Owner) → 422 **"You are the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner before you leave."** (tested exactly).
3. Clear `session.current_organization_id`; log `member.left` (subject = the organization); 200 `{message: "You left {organization}.", redirect: <dashboard URL>}`.

**`DELETE /profile/organizations/{organization}`** ("Your organizations" — on the Organizations tab of Settings, or on the profile for whoever has no such tab, §11.1, §14.2; auth only, not throttled; *reason: Staff and Viewers hold no `member-view`, so they have no Members page to find the button on*):
1. Route-model binding: a missing organization (a deleted one included) → 404.
2. The actor is not a member of that organization → 404.
3. `leave(actor, organization)` returns false → redirect **back** (to the page the form was sent from) with an error in bag `organizationMembership`, key `organization`: **"You are the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner before you leave."**
4. `left_current` = `session.current_organization_id` is that organization — if so, clear it. Log `member.left` (subject = the organization, "Left {organization}"). Redirect with status **"You left {organization}."** — to the profile when `left_current` (*the Organizations tab went with the organization, so the profile lists what remains*), otherwise back to the page it was sent from. Tested: from the Organizations tab, leaving another organization returns to `/settings/organization` with exactly "You left Beta Deli.", and leaving the organization being worked in lands on the profile with the session organization cleared.

---

## 9. Invitations

### 9.1 The token and the row
- **Open** `(organization or null, email, role, inviter)`: `token` = 64 random characters from `[A-Za-z0-9]`; insert `{organization_id, email: lower(trim(email)), role_id, token_hash: sha256_hex(token), invited_by: inviter.id, expires_at: now + 7 days}`; return the row and the plain token, which goes into the email **and nowhere else**. *Reason: a leaked database row cannot be turned into an accepted invitation.*
- **Find by token**: the row whose `token_hash = sha256_hex(token)`, whatever its state.
- **Renew** (Resend): a new token, its hash and `expires_at = now + 7 days`; the old link stops working at once.
- `isExpired` = `expires_at` is in the past · `isForPlatform` = `organization_id IS NULL` · `isFor(user)` = `lower(trim(user.email)) == invitation.email`.
- **Send the link** — `sendLink(token)` → bool: send the email (§9.4); on ANY exception, report it to the error log and return false — never throw. *Reason: the invitation already stands and can be sent again, while a 500 would leave the person inviting unsure whether anything had happened at all.* Every endpoint that sends one answers `email_sent: true|false` with a message saying which (§9.2, §9.3, §10.2, §10.3); the page shows a false as an error toast (§20.7). The activity row is written either way.
- Lifetime constant: **7 days** (`LIFETIME_DAYS`).

### 9.2 Organization invitations (organization context)
**Create — `POST /members/invitations`** (`member-invite`, `throttle:invitations`), exact order:
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. Validate: `email` required, string, email, max 255; `role_id` required, integer → 422 field errors.
3. Normalize the email (lower + trim).
4. `role = availableInOrganization(organization).find(role_id)` → null → 422 on `role_id` **"Choose a role that exists in this organization."**
5. `NOT mayAssign(actor, organization, role)` → 422 on `role_id` **"You can only invite people to a role whose access you have yourself."** — the Owner role included (no message of its own).
6. `account` = the user whose `lower(email)` equals the address (or none).
7. `account` holds a platform role → 422 on `email` **"This email belongs to a platform account and cannot join an organization."**
8. `account` is already a member of this organization → 422 on `email` **"{email} is already a member of this organization."**
9. An invitation of this organization for this email already exists — **expired ones included** → 422 on `email` **"An invitation is already waiting for this email — resend it instead."**
10. Open the invitation (§9.1); `sent = sendLink(token)`; log `member.invited` (subject = the organization); **201** `{message, email_sent: sent}` with message **"Invitation sent to {email}."** when sent, else **"The invitation for {email} was created, but the email could not be sent. Use Resend to try again."** (tested with a refusing mail server: 201, the invitation stands, the exception is reported).

An account with no memberships, or one that belongs to other organizations, may be invited.

**Resend — `POST /members/invitations/{invitation}/resend`** (`member-invite`, `throttle:invitations`): (1) `currentOrganization()` → 404; (2) `invitation.organization_id != organization.id` → 404; (3) `NOT mayAssign(actor, organization, invitation.role)` → 403 **"You cannot manage an invitation to a role whose access you do not have."**; (4) renew (§9.1), `sent = sendLink(new token)`, log `invitation.resent` (subject = the organization); 200 `{message, email_sent: sent}` with **"Invitation sent again to {email}."** when sent, else **"A new link for {email} was made, but the email could not be sent. Try again in a moment."**

**Revoke — `DELETE /members/invitations/{invitation}`** (`member-invite`): the same three guards; delete the row; log `invitation.revoked` (subject = the organization); 200 **"Invitation for {email} revoked."**

### 9.3 Platform invitations (super admins only)
**List — `GET /users/invitations`**: `{invitations: [{id, email, role, invited_by, expires_at, is_expired}]}` — every open platform invitation, newest first; `role` = the role's name (or null), `invited_by` = the inviter's name (or null), `expires_at` ISO-8601. Not paginated.

**Create — `POST /users/invitations`** (`throttle:invitations`), exact order:
1. Validate: `email` required, string, email, max 255; `role_id` required, integer.
2. Normalize the email.
3. `role = Role::platform().find(role_id)` → null → 422 on `role_id` **"Choose a platform role."** (an organization role or a custom role is refused — tested for the Admin organization role).
4. The role is Super-Admin AND the actor is not the primary super admin → 422 on `role_id` **"Only the primary super admin can invite a super admin."** *Reason: making a super admin is kept with whoever can undo it — only the primary takes Super-Admin away (§13.3).* (The Users page offers Super-Admin in the picker to the primary alone, §13.1.)
5. `account` (by lower-cased email) holds a platform role → 422 on `email` **"{email} is already on the platform team."**
6. `account` has any real-organization membership → 422 on `email` **"This email belongs to an organization account, which cannot join the platform team."**
7. An open platform invitation for this email exists (expired included) → 422 on `email` **"An invitation is already waiting for this email — resend it instead."**
8. Open with `organization = null`; `sent = sendLink(token)`; log `platform.invited` (subject NULL); **201** `{message, email_sent}` — the same two messages as an organization invitation (§9.2).

**Resend / revoke — `POST /users/invitations/{invitation}/resend`, `DELETE /users/invitations/{invitation}`**: not a platform invitation → 404; then renew + `sendLink` + log `invitation.resent` (subject NULL) → 200 `{message, email_sent}` with the same two messages as an organization resend (§9.2), or delete + log `invitation.revoked` (subject NULL) → 200 **"Invitation for {email} revoked."**

### 9.4 The email
Sent synchronously through `sendLink` (§9.1), on demand, to the invited address (no account needed); mail channel only. `{place}` = the organization's name, or **"the {app name} team"** for a platform invitation. Drawn in the app's own email shell — an HTML page and its plain-text twin, tables and inline rules, no image, one button (`resources/views/emails/invitation.blade.php`, `invitation-text.blade.php`; `EmailTemplatesTest` renders it):
- Subject: **"You're invited to join {place}"**; heading "You're invited to join {place}".
- Line: **"{inviter name} has invited you to join {place} as {role}."** — or, when the inviter no longer exists, "You have been invited to join {place} as {role}." Then: "Open the link below to accept. If you don't have an account yet, you'll set your password on the same page — nobody else ever sets it for you."
- Button **"Accept Invitation"** → `/invitations/{token}`, and the same address in full beneath it ("Or paste this address into your browser:").
- "This invitation expires on {date}, and the link can only be used once. If you weren't expecting it, you can safely ignore this email — nothing happens until you open the link." with the date formatted like `Wed, Sep 23, 2026`.
- Footer: "This invitation was sent to {email}."

### 9.5 The public link
`openInvitation(token)` = the row found by hash, with its organization, role and inviter — **null** when there is no row, it is expired, its role is gone, or it is an organization invitation whose organization is gone (a backstop: deleting an organization deletes its invitations, §15.4, and the foreign key cascades too, §2.7). *Reason: an invitation to a deleted organization is as dead as an expired one* (tested: the link of a deleted organization's invitation shows the invalid page).

**`GET /invitations/{token}`** (open to guests):
- `openInvitation` null → the invalid page: **"This invitation is no longer valid"** / "It may have expired, already been used, or been cancelled. Ask the person who invited you to send a new one." with a button "Go to dashboard" (signed in) or "Go to sign in". The page is identical in every case, so a guessed link learns nothing.
- Otherwise the page shows **"Join {place}"**, "{inviter} invited {email} to join as {role}." (or "You were invited …"), the role's description and "Expires {date}.", then one of four **states** (exposed as `data-state`):

| state | when | shows |
|---|---|---|
| `accept` | signed in AND `isFor(user)` | "You're signed in as {email}." + **Accept Invitation** (POST accept) |
| `mismatch` | signed in with another email | "You're signed in as {email}, but this invitation is for {invited email}. Sign out, then open the link again." + **Sign Out** |
| `login` | signed out AND an account with that email exists | "An account already exists for {email}. Sign in with it and you'll come straight back here to accept." + **Sign In to Accept**; the link is stored as the session's intended URL, so a successful login returns here |
| `register` | signed out AND no account | "Create your account to accept. You'll sign in with this email and the password you choose." — first name, last name, phone, password + confirmation sharing one Show/Hide (the email shown read-only and never posted; checked in the browser first, §20.5) → **Create Account and Join** |

  Every state except `mismatch` also offers **Decline Invitation**, which asks first — "Decline? This link stops working, and you would need a new invitation to join." with **Keep It** and **Decline** (*one stray tap under the main button must not end it*). A flashed `error` (from accept) is shown in a red alert on this page.

**`POST /invitations/{token}/accept`** (`auth`; a guest is redirected to login), exact order:
1. `openInvitation` null → redirect to the link (which renders the invalid page).
2. `NOT isFor(actor)` → 403 **"This invitation is for a different email address."**
3. **The link confirms the address** (owner's rule, 2026-09-29: it came to that inbox, as the signup's link would): an account whose `email_verified_at` is NULL is marked confirmed now and `account.verified` is logged ("Confirmed their email {email} by an invitation's link") — whatever the steps below decide (tested, `EmailVerificationTest`: an unconfirmed account accepting an Owner invitation is confirmed).
4. An **organization** invitation AND the actor is already a member → delete the invitation, set `session.current_organization_id` to that organization, log `invitation.accepted` (subject = the organization, explicit actor = the user, "{name} ({email}) used an invitation to {organization}, where they were already a member"), redirect to the dashboard with status **"You are already a member of {organization}."** — the existing role is kept (tested).
5. `whyCannotJoin` (§9.6) returns a reason → redirect to the link with flash `error` = the reason.
6. **Join** — one transaction: take the invitation row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`; when it is gone (used a moment ago — a double submit, or another tab) → nothing changes and the request redirects to the link, which now renders the invalid page. Otherwise insert the `organization_user` row `(actor, organization_id or 0, role_id)` and delete the invitation.
7. After commit: set `session.current_organization_id` to the organization (organization invitation) or clear it (platform invitation); log `invitation.accepted` (subject = the organization, or the user for a platform invitation; explicit actor = the user; "{name} ({email}) joined {place} as {role}"); redirect to the dashboard with status **"Welcome to {place}!"**

**`POST /invitations/{token}/register`** (`guest`; a signed-in user is redirected to the dashboard), exact order:
1. `openInvitation` null OR an account with that email exists → redirect to the link (which explains what to do — tested: no second account is created).
2. Validate: `first_name`, `last_name` required string max 255; `phone` required numeric digits:10; `password` required, confirmed, min 8 → HTML errors (§19.1).
3. ONE transaction under the invitation's lock: take the invitation row `FOR UPDATE`; when it is gone OR an account with that email now exists → nothing is created and the request redirects to the link. Otherwise create the user with the invitation's email and hashed password, set `email_verified_at = now` (*reason: opening the link proved the inbox*), insert the membership and delete the invitation. *Reason: a double submit or a second tab can neither make a second account for this email nor use the link twice.*
4. Log in; regenerate the session id; log `user.registered` (subject and explicit actor = the new user).
5. Session organization and `invitation.accepted` exactly as accept step 7 (the tier check is unnecessary for a brand-new account). No `account.verified` is logged: the account is born confirmed.
6. Redirect to the dashboard with status **"Welcome to {place}!"**

**`POST /invitations/{token}/decline`** (open to guests):
1. Find by token — **expired invitations included**.
2. If found: signed in AND `NOT isFor(actor)` → 403 **"This invitation is for a different email address."**; else delete it and log `invitation.declined` (subject = the organization, or NULL for a platform invitation). The actor is the signed-in user; for a guest, the account whose lower-cased email is the invitation's (*a guest holding the emailed link speaks for that inbox, like a password-reset link* — tested); `'System'` only when no such account exists.
3. Always redirect — to the dashboard when signed in, else to login — with status **"Invitation declined."** (an unknown token does nothing but the redirect).

**The link alone never logs anybody in**: an existing account always signs in with its own password first.

### 9.5a On the dashboard — "Invitations for you" (owner, 2026-10-07: "haan bana do, decline par sirf log, dashboard card kaafi ha")
An organization's invitation can also be answered on the dashboard by the account it was sent to. Every page of the panel stands behind `verified` (§10.1a), so that account has proved this inbox as the emailed link would — which is why the card may exist now (*it was refused while signup confirmed no address: anybody could have registered somebody else's address and accepted that person's invitations from a list*).
- **Listed** (server-side, above every dashboard of an account with no platform role — the empty one, the paused one and the organization's, §18): `Invitation::sentTo(user)` (`organization_id` not NULL AND `email` = the account's own email lower-cased and trimmed — never a `pending_email`) `->stillOpen()` (`expires_at` in the future AND its organization AND its role still there), oldest first, each "{organization} invited you as {role}", "Sent by {inviter} · Expires {in …}" with **Accept** and **Decline**. **The platform team's invitations are never listed**, nor shown to a platform account: they are answered by their emailed link alone.
- **`POST /dashboard/invitations/{invitation}/accept`** (the admin stack; open in a paused organization): `sentHere(id)` = `sentTo(user)` with its organization and role still there → otherwise **404** (anybody else's invitation, and the platform's); expired (a page left open past the week) → back to the dashboard with flash `invitation-problem` **"The invitation to {organization} has expired. Ask them to send it again."**, nothing joined; already a member → exactly the link's already-a-member step (§9.5 step 4); `whyCannotJoin` (§9.6) → the reason as `invitation-problem`; otherwise the link's join (step 6, under the invitation's lock) and its log, then the dashboard with status **"Welcome to {organization}!"** — or, when a double submit used it a moment ago, the dashboard as it stands.
- **`POST /dashboard/invitations/{invitation}/decline`** (the admin stack; open in a paused organization): the page asks first ("Decline the invitation to {organization}?" — "The invitation goes, and nobody is emailed. To join later, someone in {organization} has to invite you again."); `sentHere(id)` → 404 otherwise; **expired ones included**; delete it; log `invitation.declined` (subject = the organization, actor = the user, "{email} declined the invitation to {organization} on their dashboard") — **nobody is emailed**; the dashboard with status **"Invitation declined."**
- Tested (`DashboardInvitationsTest`): the card lists the invitations sent to this person and nobody else's; no invitation, no card; somebody in no organization sees it above the empty dashboard; a platform account is never offered one; Accept joins at once, opens the organization and logs it; Decline takes it away and only logs it (the actor the person, nobody emailed); an invitation to an organization the person is in already is used up and opens it; one that expired on a page left open is said on the dashboard and nothing is joined; a paused organization's person may still answer an invitation to another.

### 9.6 Tier rules at acceptance (`whyCannotJoin`)
| invitation | actor | reason (flashed as `error`) |
|---|---|---|
| platform | already holds a platform role | **"You are already on the platform team."** |
| platform | has any real-organization membership | **"Your account is a member of one or more organizations. An organization account cannot join the platform team."** |
| organization | holds a platform role | **"Platform accounts cannot join an organization."** |

### 9.7 Throttles
`throttle:invitations` — **60 per hour per user** — on creating and resending organization and platform invitations, and on the platform's two Owner doors that email one: creating an organization for a customer (§10.2) and "Invite owner" (§10.3). `throttle:invitation-response` — **20 per minute per IP** — one shared bucket for opening, accepting, registering and declining by the link (tested: the 21st `GET` in a minute answers 429). Revoking, and answering on the dashboard (§9.5a), are not throttled beyond `throttle:admin`.

---

## 10. Ways in

Nobody creates an account for somebody else. There are exactly these doors — and, for an existing account that holds no platform role, two more ways into an organization: opening a new one of their own from Settings → Organizations when their role there carries `organization-store` (§10.2), or being put in one by a super admin from the platform (§13.4).

### 10.1 Public signup — `GET /register`, `POST /register` (`guest`; the POST `throttle:signup`: 10/min per IP)
- `GET` passes `signupOpen` = "a role with key `owner` exists" (the Owner role, whatever it is called). When closed, the page shows **"Registration is not available right now. Please contact the administrator."** and disables the submit button. The form carries **two robot traps** (`<x-auth.robot-trap>`; owner, 2026-09-30: made-up names signed up on the live site all day, each sending a confirmation email to somebody's real address): a text field `website` inside a `display:none` box, `tabindex="-1"`, `autocomplete="off"` — no person, keyboard or screen reader ever meets it — and a hidden `form_started`, the moment the form was drawn (a Unix timestamp), sealed with the application's encryption.
- `POST`, exact order:
  1. **The robot check runs first** (`RegisteredUserController::looksLikeARobot`): `website` filled → a robot; otherwise, while `signage.signup_min_seconds` is above nought (`SIGNAGE_SIGNUP_MIN_SECONDS`, **3** by default, none under the test suites unless set): `form_started` missing, not a string, not decryptable, or the form came back fewer than that many seconds after it was drawn, or more than `signage.signup_form_lifetime_seconds` (two hours) after → a robot. A robot is sent back with the old input (never the passwords, `website` or `form_started`) and an error on **`form`**: **"Please try again."** — nothing written, logged or emailed. *Reason: said like any form to send again, so a person the trap ever caught by mistake simply sends it again, and a robot learns nothing.* A robot that gets past both makes an account nobody confirms, which the seven-day prune takes (§10.1a).
  2. **The Owner role lookup runs BEFORE validation.** No role with key `owner` → redirect back with old input and an error on **`email`**: **"Registration is not available right now. Please contact the administrator."** — nothing is created, even for an invalid submission.
  3. **Lower-case the submitted email** (when it is a string) — *one address is one account whatever its capitals, kept lowercased the way invitations and the profile keep it, so "Sana@" and "sana@" can never become two people on any database* (tested: a second signup as `SANA@example.com` fails on `email`).
  4. Validate (§19.2): `first_name`, `last_name` required string max 255; `phone` required numeric digits:10; `email` `bail`, required, email, max 255 (*the column's size: a longer address failed only at the INSERT, as a 500* — tested), regex `^\S+$` (**"Email cannot contain spaces."**), unique in users — an address already taken, confirmed or not, is refused (tested, `VerificationAttackTest`); `password` required, confirmed, min 8; `organization_name` required string max 255; `street` required string max 255; `suite` nullable string max 100; `city` required string max 100; `state` required, string, exactly 2, one of the 50 US state codes (DC and territories rejected); `zip_code` required, string, max 10, digits only (**"Zip code can only contain numbers."**). `country`, `is_active` and any role id are **not read** (a submitted `role_id` is ignored — tested).
  5. One transaction: create the user (`email_verified_at` NULL); create the organization `{name: organization_name, street, suite, city, state, zip_code, country: 'USA', is_active: true, created_by: the new user}`; insert the membership `(user, organization, Owner)`. An invalid organization half creates nothing.
  6. Log in; regenerate the session id; log `user.registered` (subject = the user; actor = the new owner themself; organization = the new organization, §17.1).
  7. **Send the link that confirms the email** (`User::sendVerificationLink`, §10.1a) and redirect to **"Check your inbox"** (`verification.notice`) — with flash `error` = the reason when the link could not go out (a mail server that refuses: "The email could not be sent just now. Send the link again in a minute."; the visitor over the budget: "Too many emails asked for. Try again in …"). The account stands either way, and the page sends the link again.
- *Reason: a customer creates their account and their organization in one form and becomes its Owner — server-chosen, never client input — and proves the address before anything that uses the server's space (owner's rule, 2026-09-29).*
- Tested (`RegistrationTest`, `SignupRobotAttackTest`): the field no person sees filled → back with exactly "Please try again." on `form`, nothing written, no `user.registered`, nobody emailed; with the time trap on, no sealed moment, a forged one or one that is not a string is refused; the same sealed form refused at once goes through 20 seconds later (to "Check your inbox"); a sealed moment three hours old is refused; the page carries both traps out of sight.

### 10.1a Confirming the email (owner's rules, 2026-09-29 — "we will do email verification when anyone will create account", "no aliases, delete after 7 days"; and 2026-10-06)
`User` implements the framework's "must verify email" contract; `email_verified_at` NULL = not confirmed (§2.1).
- **The wall.** Every signed-in page of the panel stands behind `verified` — the dashboard, the chooser and the whole admin stack (§3.7), the chunked uploads too: an account that has not confirmed is redirected to **"Check your inbox"** (`GET /verify-email`, `verification.notice`), and a request that wants JSON is refused **403** "Your email address is not verified." Only the doors `HttpSurfaceSweepTest` pins stay open (§3.7): its profile (and correcting, resending or giving up its email there), leaving an organization from it, the verification pages and links, accepting an invitation by its link (which confirms it, §9.5), its password, sign out and "stop" after "Log in as".
- **"Check your inbox"** (a guest-layout page; a confirmed account is redirected to the dashboard): "We sent a link to {email}. Open it to confirm your email, and your account is ready: screens, pictures, videos and ads.", "Nothing yet? It can take a minute or two — look in spam or junk too.", **Send the Link Again** (`POST /verify-email/resend`), "Wrong address? Change it in your profile, and a new link goes to the right one.", "An account that is not confirmed is removed, with its organization, after {created_at + 7 days, like `Wed, Sep 23, 2026`}." and **Sign Out**. A flashed `error` shows in a red alert, `verification-link-sent` as "A new link is on its way to {email}." While "Log in as" is viewing the account, the impersonation banner is drawn on it too (§14.1).
- **The link** (`VerifyEmailNotification`, the app's own email shell, subject "Confirm your email for {app name}"): a temporary **signed** URL to `GET /verify-email/{id}/{hash}` — `id` the account's id, `hash` = `sha1(email)` of the address it proves — lasting `auth.verification.expire` minutes (60 by default). The email carries no text anybody typed but the recipient's own address — no name: *it goes to whatever address a visitor types, and a name is where signup spam puts its words.* **The link opens nothing by itself**: the route is `auth` (a guest is sent to sign in, tested), `throttle:verification-link`. Exact order: already confirmed → the dashboard with status "Your email is already confirmed."; `whyNot` → back to "Check your inbox" with the reason as `error`: an invalid or expired signature **"That link has expired or is not complete. Send a new one below."**, another account's id **"That link is for another account. Sign out and sign in with the account it was sent to."**, a hash that is not the account's current address **"That link was sent to an address this account is not using now. Send a new one below."**; otherwise mark it confirmed, log `account.verified` ("Confirmed their email {email}"), and open the dashboard with status **"Your email is confirmed. Welcome!"** A signup link's signature opens no other route (tested: not the one that confirms a changed email).
- **Send the Link Again** (`POST /verify-email/resend`, from "Check your inbox" or the profile; a confirmed account → the dashboard): `User::sendVerificationLink()` → back with status `verification-link-sent`, or with `error` = its refusal. Not logged (a mail request, like forgot-password, §17.3).
- **Confirm Email and Continue** (owner, 2026-10-06: "waha ek button ho jo super admin ko hi show ho jis per click karne se email verify ho jaye aur agay chale jaye"): "Log In As" an account that has not confirmed lands on "Check your inbox" (§14.1), and only there, only when `ImpersonateController::superAdminViewingAs(account)` answers — both impersonation keys in the session, `impersonating_user_id` the account viewed, whoever started it still existing and still a super admin — does the page show "As a super admin, you can confirm this email yourself." and the button. `POST /verify-email/confirm-for-them` (`auth`, `throttle:admin`) asks the same again: nobody viewing → **403 "Only a super admin viewing as this account can confirm its email."** (the account itself, or a session that says anything else — tested case by case in `VerificationAttackTest`); not yet confirmed → mark it confirmed and log `account.verified` with the **super admin as actor** ("Confirmed the email {email} of {name} for them, by Log In As"); then the dashboard with status **"{email} is confirmed."** Confirming an account already confirmed changes nothing and logs nothing (tested).
- **What else confirms the address** — what came to the inbox: accepting an invitation by its link (§9.5 step 3) or registering from one (§9.5), and completing a password reset (§19.4), which also ends every session of the account (`User::endEverySession`), so *somebody who signed up with another person's address and kept a session open loses it the moment that person resets the password* (tested, `VerificationAttackTest`).
- **A changed address** (§14.2): a confirmed account's new address waits in `pending_email` until the link sent to the NEW address (`ConfirmNewEmailNotification`, "Confirm your new email for {app name}", `GET /confirm-email/{id}/{hash}`, the same signature and `whyNot`, `auth`, `throttle:verification-link`) is opened; an account not yet confirmed simply corrects its address and the signup's link goes to the new one.
- **One account per address, compared as typed** — no folding of dots or `+tags`; capitals are lowered at every door (§2.1).
- **The mail budget** — every email that confirms an address draws on one budget, counted inside `User::sendALink` so no door can skip it: the signup's link, Send the Link Again on either page, and a new address typed on the profile. Per account `User::LINKS_PER_ACCOUNT` = **3 a minute and 20 an hour** (cache keys `confirm-links:account:minute:{id}`, `confirm-links:account:hour:{id}`), per visitor `User::LINKS_PER_VISITOR` = **30 an hour per IP, across every account they make** (`confirm-links:visitor:hour:{ip}`) — fixed windows, like the password limiter (§24). `linkRefusal()` (which counts nothing) answers **"Too many emails asked for. Try again in {a second | n seconds | n minutes}."** (minutes from 90 seconds up); `sendALink` asks it, then counts the email in every budget **whether the mail server takes it or not**, and reports a refusing mail server rather than throwing: **"The email could not be sent just now. Send the link again in a minute."** The profile asks `linkRefusal()` BEFORE it saves a new address, so a refusal leaves the account as it was. Tested (`VerificationAttackTest`, `EmailVerificationTest`): three emails from three doors, the fourth refused at every door (a new address before anything is saved); twenty an hour spaced a minute apart; ten accounts from one address use thirty, and an eleventh is refused there and served from another address.
- **Never confirmed → removed** (`accounts:prune-unverified`, daily at 03:15 by the scheduler's cron, `withoutOverlapping`): every account with `email_verified_at` NULL and `created_at` at least `User::UNVERIFIED_DAYS` (7) days ago — to the second — walked by id. Each in one transaction: lock its organizations (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, by id) and then its own row, as every change to a team or a library does; gone meanwhile or confirmed a moment ago → skipped; **kept** — and named in the run's output ("Kept {email}: {why}.") — when it holds a platform row ("they are on the platform team"), an organization of theirs has other people ("an organization of theirs has other people in it"), or one holds anything (`WHAT_AN_ORGANIZATION_HOLDS`: `media`, `screens`, `channels`, `builder_ads`, `builder_assets`, `invitations`, `roles` → "an organization of theirs is not empty ({table})"); otherwise delete each organization (its purge runs while the person is still its member, §15.4), then the account (§15.3), and log `account.pruned` (subject NULL, actor 'System', organization = the first organization removed: "Removed {email}, never confirmed within 7 days, with the organization {names}"). Tested (`PruneUnverifiedAccountsTest`): removed after seven days with its organization; a confirmed or newer account never touched; an organization somebody put people or files in kept, with the account, and the run says why; many at once all removed; scheduled every night.
- **The accounts that existed before the rule** were marked confirmed by the migration that brought it (one exact stamp, which its `down()` undid — squashed away since, §22.4), so nobody on the live site was locked out.

### 10.2 A new organization — `POST /organizations` (the platform) and `POST /settings/organization/open` (inside an organization), both `organization-store`
Two routes, one per tier (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: the Organizations page is the platform's; an organization's own people open an organization from the Organizations tab of Settings).

**From the platform — `POST /organizations`** (`global-tier` + `organization-store`; `throttle:invitations`, since it emails, §9.7) — an organization for a customer:
1. Validate the organization details (§12.3 rules, `is_active` optional boolean) plus `owner_email` required, string, email, max 255 → 422 (tested: every required field including `owner_email`).
2. Normalize the owner email; an account with that email holding a platform role → 422 on `owner_email` **"This email belongs to a platform account and cannot own an organization."**
3. One transaction: create the organization (`created_by` = actor; `is_active` from the request or the DB default) and open an **Owner** invitation to the email (inviter = actor).
4. `sent = sendLink(token)` (§9.1); log `organization.created` (subject = the organization) "Created organization {name} and invited {email} to own it"; **201** `{message, email_sent: sent, organization}` with message **"Organization created. An invitation to own it was sent to {email}."** when sent, else **"Organization created, but the invitation email to {email} could not be sent. Use Invite owner to send it again."**

The organization has **no member at all** until the invitation is accepted (tested; browser-tested: its row on the Organizations page counts 0 members and offers "Invite owner", and once the invited owner has registered from the link it counts 1 and offers "Invite owner" no more — §10.3).

**From inside an organization — `POST /settings/organization/open`** (`organization-store` in the current organization — none of the starter roles starts with it; the super admin decides who holds it) — **Create organization** on the Organizations tab (§11.1), a plain HTML form (§19.1): a new organization the member owns.
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. Validate in bag `newOrganization` the organization details under **`organization_`-prefixed names**: `organization_name` required, string, max 255; `organization_street` required, string, max 255; `organization_suite` nullable, string, max 100; `organization_city` required, string, max 100; `organization_state` required, string, size 2, one of the 50 US codes; `organization_zip_code` required, string, max 10, digits only (**"Zip code can only contain numbers."**); `organization_country` required, string, max 100 — the framework's messages naming them "organization name", "street", "suite", "city", "state", "zip code" and "country" (e.g. **"The organization name field is required."**). Neither `is_active` nor any owner email is read (tested: `is_active: false` and an `owner_email` are ignored). A failure redirects back with the errors in `newOrganization` and the old input, and the page reopens its dialog. *Reason for the prefix: the details form of the organization being worked in sits on the same page and repopulates from old input, so a new organization's details must never read back as its own* (tested: a refused submit leaves no `name` or `zip_code` error).
3. One transaction: create the organization `{…details without the prefix, is_active: true, created_by: actor}` and insert the membership `(actor, organization, Owner)`. **Nobody is invited.**
4. Log `organization.created` (subject = the new organization, so the entry carries it) "Opened organization {name}, owned by {actor name}"; redirect to the settings page (`/settings/organization`) with status **"{name} is open, and you are its Owner. Switch to it from the organization menu."** (a success toast, §20.7). The session's current organization does not change.

Tested: with `organization-store` on the Owner role, "Alpha Mart East" → a redirect to the settings page with exactly that status, the organization active although `is_active: false` was sent, its street saved, the person its Owner, the session still in Alpha Mart, no invitation, and the `organization.created` entry carrying the new organization; the Owner role without `organization-store` → 403; `organization_name` empty and `organization_zip_code` `7A` → back to `/settings/organization` with exactly "The organization name field is required." and "Zip code can only contain numbers." in `newOrganization`, and no organization created.

*Reason (owner, 2026-09-16): opening an organization is off by default — the super admin decides who may, so free screens are not opened for everybody; whether an organization is active stays the platform's switch.*

### 10.3 Giving an organization an Owner — `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation` (`global-tier` + `organization-store`; `throttle:invitations`)
*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17 (third round): for an organization with **no Owner** — one left without an Owner, or made for a customer who never accepted. An organization that has an Owner is not offered this: more Owners — partners — come from its own Members page (§8.4, §11.3) or from the platform's Users → Organizations (§13.4).* `replaced` below = every **Owner** invitation of this organization addressed to any *other* email; invitations to other roles are always left alone. *Reason: an organization with nobody in charge has one way in as its Owner at a time — a mistyped address stops working the moment the right person is invited or made Owner.* Exact order:
1. Validate `email`: required, string, email, max 255; normalize (lower + trim).
2. The organization has an Owner (`OrganizationTeam::ownerCount(organization) > 0`) → 422 on `email` **"{organization} already has an Owner."** — nothing is changed, replaced or sent. The count is read without a lock (§26.4).
3. If an account with that email (compared lower-cased) **is a member of the organization**: in one transaction its membership becomes **Owner at once** (no consent step) and `replaced` is deleted; log `organization.owner_assigned` (subject = the organization) "Made {name} ({email}) an Owner of {organization}" followed by ", replacing the invitation for {emails}" when any were replaced; **200** **"{name} is now an Owner of {organization}."** followed, when any were replaced, by **" The earlier invitation for {emails} no longer works."** No email is sent and the response carries no `email_sent`.
4. Otherwise: the email belongs to a platform account → 422 on `email` **"This email belongs to a platform account and cannot own an organization."**
5. One transaction:
   - **replace** — delete `replaced`;
   - **renew** — when this organization already has an invitation for this email (any role; open or expired): set its role to Owner and `invited_by` to the actor, and renew it (new token, new week — §9.1);
   - **open** — otherwise open a new Owner invitation (inviter = actor).
6. `sent = sendLink(token)`.
7. Log `organization.owner_invited` (subject = the organization): "Invited {email} to own {organization}" — or, when renewed, "Sent the invitation to own {organization} to {email} again" — followed by ", replacing the invitation for {emails}" when any were replaced.
8. Respond `{message, email_sent: sent}` with status **201** for a new invitation, **200** for a renewed one. Message: **"An invitation to own {organization} was sent to {email}."** when sent, else **"The invitation to own {organization} is ready, but the email to {email} could not be sent. Try again in a moment."** — followed, when any were replaced, by **" The earlier invitation for {emails} no longer works."**

The listing offers the button only where this can succeed: `can.invite_owner` = `organization-store` AND the organization's `owners_count` is 0 (§12.1). The page's dialog is headed **"Give {organization} an owner"** and, after a success, reloads the list — so an organization that has just been given an Owner loses its button.

Tested: Orphan Mart (a Staff member, no Owner) — `partner@example.com` → 201, an Owner invitation; the Staff member's email in capitals → 200 with exactly "{name} is now an Owner of Orphan Mart.
