The earlier invitation for partner@example.com no longer works.", the member an Owner, no invitation left, and the log line exactly "Made {name} ({email}) an Owner of Orphan Mart, replacing the invitation for partner@example.com"; then that member's email or another address → 422 on `email` with exactly "Orphan Mart already has an Owner.", no invitation created. The same address again, even after the first link expired and in other capitals → 200 with `email_sent: true`, the same row with a new token hash and a fresh expiry, a second email sent; a different address → 201 with exactly "An invitation to own Typo Treats was sent to gina@example.com. The earlier invitation for gina@exmaple.com no longer works.", the mistyped link now invalid, while a Staff invitation to a third address survives; an address the organization had invited as Staff → 200, the same row now an Owner invitation. An organization that has an Owner (Busy Bakery) → 422 with exactly "Busy Bakery already has an Owner.", the co-owner invitation its Owner sent kept, and no email sent.

### 10.4 Platform staff
Super admins invite by email with a platform role (§9.3); accepting creates the `organization_id = 0` membership (§9.5).

### 10.5 What does not exist
No admin-created accounts, no editing of another person's name / email / phone / password, no organization assignment or unassignment endpoint beyond the super admin's Users → Organizations (§13.4 — which moves an existing account holding no platform role into or out of an organization and never creates an account), no onboarding endpoint, no signup-default role flag, no way for anybody but a super admin viewing as the account to confirm somebody else's email (§10.1a), no API (tested: `PUT /users/{id}` answers 405). (An invitation list with Accept buttons on the dashboard did not exist while signup confirmed no address; it does now, for the account's own confirmed address alone, §9.5a.)

---

## 11. Settings → Organizations (the current organization, and the person's organizations)

Plain HTML forms (redirects, named error bags — §19.1). *Owner's rules, 2026-09-17: an organization's own people work on the organization they are in — and see every organization they belong to — on the **Organizations** tab of Settings, beside Profile (§20.3); the sidebar has no link of its own, and the Organizations page is the platform's (§12). A role does not matter, its permissions do:* the tab opens with `organization-view` in the current organization, and each card and form asks its own permission there — the details `organization-update` (read-only without it), Create organization `organization-store` (§10.2), Delete Organization `organization-destroy`. There is no handover here: an organization changes hands on its Members page (§11.3). (The routes keep the names `organization-settings.*` under `/settings/organization`.)

### 11.1 `GET /settings/organization` (`organization-view`)
`currentOrganization()` → 404. Renders the page headed "Settings", on its Organizations tab (§20.3), with these cards:
- **Organization Details** — the form (`organization-details-form`) prefilled with the organization when the actor holds `organization-update`; otherwise the details read-only (`organization-details-readonly`: the name, and the address with its country), with **"Your role here does not let you change the organization's details."**
- **Your organizations** (`your-organizations`) — always: every organization the person belongs to, with their role in each and a way out — the same card the profile shows to whoever has no Organizations tab (§14.2; `membershipsOf`, §8.1) — plus **Create organization** (`open-organization-button`, opening the dialog of §10.2) for holders of `organization-store`.
- **Delete Organization** (`delete-organization`) — for holders of `organization-destroy` only, laid out like Delete Account on the Profile tab, saying what goes with the organization: "Permanently deletes {organization} and everything in it: {n} screen(s) (they stop playing at once), {m} media file(s), playlists, **the roles made in it**, the organization's own channels, open invitations, and the access of all {k} member(s). **The people's accounts are not deleted.**" Its dialog (`delete-organization-form`) asks "Are you sure you want to delete {organization}?" — "The organization and everything in it are removed for good, and its screens stop playing immediately. It cannot be undone." — then the typed name and the password (§11.4).

There is no "danger zone" grouping and no handover card (§11.3). Tested: Staff and a Viewer (no `organization-view`) get 403 — the Viewer's "Your organizations" staying on their profile; an Admin opens the page with both Settings tabs (`settings-tab-profile`, `settings-tab-organization`); out of the box an Owner sees "Delete Organization" and an Admin does not; a role holding only `organization-view` sees exactly that read-only text, no details form, `your-organizations`, and no delete or Create organization — its `PUT /settings/organization` is 403, and its profile carries the Organizations tab and no "Your organizations"; a role holding `organization-view`, `organization-destroy` and `organization-store` sees `delete-organization` and `open-organization-button`, whatever the role is called; the Owner role without `organization-view` → 403, and no tab; an Owner whose role lost `organization-destroy` sees no "Delete Organization"; an Admin given `organization-destroy` sees "Delete Organization"; "Your organizations" lists the person's organizations (Alpha Mart, and Gamma Grill as Staff) and no other; a platform user holding `organization-view` and `organization-update` gets 404; the page shows no "Transfer Ownership" (`transfer-ownership`).

### 11.2 Update details — `PUT /settings/organization` (`organization-update`)
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. Validate in bag `organizationDetails`: `name` required string max 255; `street` required string max 255; `suite` nullable string max 100; `city` required string max 100; `state` required, string, size 2, one of the 50 US codes; `zip_code` required, string, max 10, digits only (**"Zip code can only contain numbers."**); `country` required string max 100.
3. Update **only those fields** — `is_active`, `accepts_network_ads` and `created_by` are never read (tested).
4. Log `organization.updated` (logged on every successful submit, changed or not); redirect to the settings page with status `organization-updated`, which the page shows as the success toast **"Organization details saved."** (§20.7).

Each action reads the role of the organization being worked in (tested: an Owner of Alpha who is Staff in Beta gets 403 on `PUT` and `DELETE /settings/organization` while working in Beta, and changes Alpha's details — never `is_active` — while working in Alpha).

### 11.3 An organization changes hands on the Members page
*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17 (third round): making somebody an Owner is changing their role. There is no Transfer Ownership card, no `POST /settings/organization/transfer-ownership` route (it answers 404) and no `organization-transfer` permission any more — it is not in the catalogue the migrations insert, and the upgrade that deleted it with every grant of it has since been squashed away (§3.1, §22).* An organization changes hands with the endpoints of §8, under their usual rules:
1. Whoever may give the Owner role in the organization — `mayAssign` (§8.1): their role there holds every permission the Owner role holds, and the route asks `member-update`; out of the box an Owner — changes another member's role to the Owner role with `PUT /members/{user}` (§8.4). The organization now has one more Owner; somebody who is not yet a member is invited to the Owner role first (§9.2).
2. The new Owner now reaches the first one (`mayManage`, §8.1) and may change their role with `PUT /members/{user}` or remove them (§8.5); or the first Owner leaves (§8.6) — or simply stays, and the two own the organization together.
3. The last-Owner refusals (§8.2) keep the organization from being left without an Owner at any point in between.

Nothing else hands an organization over from inside it; from above the organizations, a super admin's Users → Organizations gives any role the organization has, the Owner role included (§13.4), and "Invite owner" serves an organization with no Owner (§10.3). `organization.ownership_transferred` is no longer logged — entries written before stay in the log (§17.2). What this makes possible is in §26.16.

Tested: the Owner makes a Staff member Owner (`PUT /members/{user}` → 200), the new Owner makes the first one Admin (200) — the Staff member now the Owner, the first Owner an Admin —, the new Owner's Organizations tab shows no "Transfer Ownership", `POST /settings/organization/transfer-ownership` → 404, and no `organization-transfer` permission row exists. Browser-tested: the seller (an Owner), whose Organizations tab shows "Delete Organization" and no "Transfer Ownership", makes the buyer (an Admin) Owner on the Members page ("{name} is now Owner."); the buyer, signed in, makes the seller Admin ("{name} is now Admin.") and then deletes the organization from the Organizations tab, landing on the dashboard's empty state (`dashboard-empty`) with the organization row and its custom role gone and both accounts kept.

### 11.4 Delete the organization — `DELETE /settings/organization` (`organization-destroy`), exact order
*Deleting the organization is a permission — `organization-destroy`, which the Owner role starts with; the super admin may take it from the Owner role or give it to another role (owner's rule, 2026-09-16). Without it in the current organization the route's gate answers 403 before anything below (tested: an Owner whose role lost it → 403; an Admin with its starting permissions → 403; an Admin given it deletes the organization).*
1. `currentOrganization()` → 404.
2. Validate in bag `organizationDeletion`: `confirm_name` required (**"Type the organization name to confirm."**), string, **exactly equal** to the organization's current name, case-sensitive (**"Type the organization name exactly as it is shown."**). *The field is `confirm_name`, not `name`, because the details form on the same page repopulates from old `name` input — a mistyped confirmation must never reappear there as the organization's new name.*
3. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) in bag `organizationDeletion`, field `password`: **"Password is required."** / **"The password is incorrect."** / the limiter's message — each a redirect back with the error in the bag (the name is reported first, the password on the next submit; the page's own check shows both at once).
4. Count the organization's screens and media; in one transaction delete the organization — its purge runs first, then the row goes for good (§15.4).
5. Clear `session.current_organization_id`; log `organization.deleted` (subject NULL; organization = the deleted organization, §17.1 — tested); redirect to the dashboard with status **"{name} was deleted."** (tested: the organization row is gone and the session organization cleared).

---

## 12. Organizations (the platform)

*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17: the Organizations page and its writes are the platform's.* Every route under `/organizations` but the switch (§12.6) carries `global-tier` on top of its permission (§3.4), and a platform account works on every organization. An organization account gets **403** from all of them whatever its role holds — its own people change the organization they work in from the Organizations tab of Settings (§11) (tested: an Owner whose role holds `organization-view`, `organization-store`, `organization-update` and `organization-destroy` gets 403 on the page, the data, create, update, delete and "Invite owner", and nothing changes; a member holding `organization-view` and `organization-update` gets 403 from the data and the update and changes the organization from Settings → Organizations instead).

### 12.1 `GET /organizations/data` (`global-tier` + `organization-view`)
Every organization, ordered by name, searchable by `name` and `city` (§20.6). Row = every organization column plus:
- `members_count` — number of `organization_user` rows of the organization (the page's Members column, `organization-members-{id}`);
- `owners_count` — number of `organization_user` rows of the organization holding the Owner role (found by its key; the listing answers 404 while the installation has no Owner role);
- for super admins only (`campaign-manage`): `screens_count` and `ad_screens_count`;
- `can` — what the viewer may do to the row: `{update: actor.can('organization-update'), destroy: actor.can('organization-destroy'), billing: actor.can('billing-view'), invite_owner: actor.can('organization-store') AND owners_count == 0}` — "Invite owner" is offered only for an organization with no Owner (owner's rule, 2026-09-17, §10.3); Billing beside Edit with View Billing (§12.1a).

**No owners on the list** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17): an organization may have several Owners — partners — so the rows carry neither the owners nor a pending owner invitation (only how many Owners there are), and the page has no Owner column. Tested: Alpha Mart (an Owner and a Staff member) reads `members_count` 2, no `owners` or `owner_invitation` key, `can` exactly `{update: true, destroy: true, billing: true, invite_owner: false}` for the super admin; Beta Deli (a Staff member, no Owner) and Gamma Grill (no members) `invite_owner` true; a platform user holding only `organization-view` gets `invite_owner` false on every row.

`GET /organizations` (`global-tier` + `organization-view`) renders the page "Organizations" with the table "All Organizations", **Add Organization** for holders of `organization-store` (§20.1), and an organization's status shown as Active or **Paused** (§2.2).

### 12.1a Billing beside Edit — `GET` / `PUT /organizations/{organization}/billing` (owner, 2026-10-08 — "har organization ki row per edit k barabar mein"; docs/BILLING-SPEC.md)
Until billing starts (Stripe comes later), nothing is charged and the platform switches what an organization has unlocked; the prices and the month's estimate are computed once, by `BillingSummary::for(organization)`, which both this dialog and the organization's own Settings → Billing read (§20.3).
- **`GET`** (`global-tier` + `billing-view`) → `{billing: <the summary>}` for any organization.
- **`PUT`** (`global-tier` + `billing-update`): `premium_templates_unlocked` and `platform_channels_unlocked` both required booleans — missing or not a yes or no → 422 **"Say whether Premium Templates are unlocked."** / **"Say whether Platform Channels are unlocked."**; save both; log one `organization.billing_updated` per switch that changed (subject = the organization; "{Unlocked|Locked} {Premium Templates|Platform Channels} for {organization}"); 200 `{message, billing}` — message the changes joined with " and " plus " for {organization}." (e.g. exactly "Unlocked Premium Templates and Locked Platform Channels for Smart Stop."), or **"Nothing changed."**
- The dialog (`organization-billing`) shows the summary to every holder of `billing-view`, and a switch per feature (`role="switch"`) to whoever also holds `billing-update`; an organization's own people never reach either route (the tier lock), whatever their role holds (tested, `BillingAttackTest`: `billing-update` cannot even be put on an organization's role, §7.6).

### 12.2 Create
§10.2 — `POST /organizations` makes an organization for a customer, with an Owner invitation. An organization's own people open an organization of their own from Settings → Organizations (`POST /settings/organization/open`, §10.2), never here.

### 12.3 Update — `PUT /organizations/{organization}` (`global-tier` + `organization-update`)
1. Validate: `name` required string max 255; `street` required string max 255; `suite` nullable string max 100; `city` required string max 100; `state` required, string, size 2, one of the 50 US codes (the same rules as create, where `Texas` and `DC` are tested as rejected); `zip_code` required, string, max 10, digits only (**"Zip code can only contain numbers."**); `country` required string max 100; `is_active` optional boolean (absent → unchanged; tested: `is_active: false` deactivates the organization).
2. Update, and say it as what it is (*pausing an organization closes it to its own people, §2.2*): `is_active` changed to false → log `organization.paused` "Paused organization {name}", 200 **"{name} is paused."**; changed to true → `organization.resumed` "Turned organization {name} back on", **"{name} is active again."**; otherwise `organization.updated` "Updated organization {name}", **"Organization {name} updated."** — each 200 `{message, organization}` (one entry per save, the pause or resume winning over other changes made with it). **This form is the only way an organization is paused or turned back on** — the Organizations tab never reads `is_active` (§11.2). Tested (`PausedOrganizationTest`): exactly those three messages and the log's `organization.paused`, `organization.resumed`, `organization.updated`.

### 12.4 Delete — `DELETE /organizations/{organization}` (`global-tier` + `organization-destroy`)
1. Validate `confirm_name`: required (**"Type the organization name to confirm."**), string, exactly the organization's name (**"Type the organization name exactly as it is shown."**) → 422 field error (tested: `doomed deli` for "Doomed Deli" is refused). A failure here answers before the password is looked at.
2. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
3. Count screens and media; transaction: delete — the purge first, then the row, for good (§15.4).
4. Log `organization.deleted` (subject NULL; organization = the deleted organization).
5. 200 `{message: "{name} was deleted."}` — the session is not touched (a platform account works in no organization, §6.1). (Tested: a member whose custom role holds `organization-destroy` gets 403 deleting a rival organization — the tier lock answers before anything.)

The typed name AND the password confirm it (tested: without a password, or with a wrong one, the organization stays; after five wrong passwords even the right one answers 429 until the window passes). Tested: the organization row, its screen, its custom role and its Owner's membership are gone, the Owner's account stays, and the `organization.deleted` entry carries the organization.

### 12.5 Owner invitation
§10.3.

### 12.6 Switch the current organization — `POST /organizations/switch` (no permission gate)
*Reason for no gate: an organization member has zero permissions before selecting an organization, so any permission gate would lock them out of ever switching in.* Exact order:
1. Validate `organization_id`: required, integer → an HTML validation error (redirect back).
2. The actor holds a platform role → 403 **'The platform team works above the organizations. Use "Log In As" to see an organization as one of its members.'** — unconditional.
3. No organization with that id, OR the actor is not a member → 403 **"You are not a member of this organization."** (`0`, unknown and deleted ids land here).
4. `session.current_organization_id = organization.id`; log `organization.switched` (subject = the organization); redirect to **the intended URL, else the dashboard** — the page that sent the person to choose (§18; tested, `ChooseTheOnlyOrganizationTest`: a screen's page refused for somebody of two organizations with none chosen sends them to `/select-organization`, and choosing returns them to that screen) — with status **"Switched to {name}."**

Open in a paused organization (§2.2), and exempt from the stale-tab refusal: it changes the session on purpose, from a page of the session it leaves (§20.4a).

---

## 13. Accounts (the platform's alone)

*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17 (third round): the accounts pages are the platform's — an organization's people are its Members page (§6.2, §8.3).* The whole `/users` group carries `global-tier` on top of each route's own lock (§3.4, §3.7), so every actor here holds a platform role and an organization account gets **403** from every `/users` route whatever its role holds (tested: an Owner whose role was given `user-view` and `user-destroy` straight in the database gets 403 from `GET /users`, `GET /users/data` and `DELETE /users/{user}`, and the Staff member it targeted stays; an organization Owner gets 403 from `GET /users` and `GET /users/data`). `user-view` and `user-destroy` never go on an organization's role (§3.2, §7.6). An organization once had an Accounts page answering these routes for its own people; it is gone, with its "Role here" column, its disabled Delete and `delete_note`, and the upgrade of that day took the two permissions off every organization role and custom role (§3.1, §22).

### 13.1 `GET /users/data` (`global-tier` + `user-view`)
Scope per §6.3 — for a super admin narrowed by `group` (`platform` = the accounts holding an organization-0 row, `organization` = every other account, anything else — nothing, another word, an array, SQL — every account; read through `plainValue`, owner 2026-10-07); searchable by first name, last name, email, and a full name as shown ("Ali Khan": its first word in the first name and the rest in the last); paginated (§20.6). **Sorted by its headings** (owner, 2026-10-07: "sorting bhi", `UserController::sortedBy`, `sort` and `direction` read through `plainValue`): `sort=joined` → by `created_at` then id, newest first unless `direction=asc`; anything else → by lower-cased first name, then lower-cased last name, then id, A to Z unless `sort=name&direction=desc` — so a list asked for nothing opens A to Z whatever the capitals, and a column that is not a heading is ignored. The page itself first asks for Joined, newest first. Selected columns `id, first_name, last_name, email, created_at`, plus:
- `name`; `is_you`; `is_primary` (the row is the primary super admin);
- `platform_role` — the name of the row's organization-0 role, or null;
- `memberships` — `[{organization_id, organization_name, role_name, role_key}]` for the organizations the account belongs to (never the organization-0 row), ordered by organization name (tested exact shape);
- `sole_owner_of` — names of the organizations where this account is the only Owner (tested);
- `can` — what the viewer may do to the row (tested exact shape `{impersonate, manage_organizations, remove_platform_role, delete}`):
  - `impersonate` = viewer is a super admin AND not the row AND the row is not a super admin;
  - `manage_organizations` = viewer is a super admin AND the row has no platform role — Users → Organizations (§13.4), offered for every account outside the platform team, one in no organization included (tested: true for an organization Owner, false for platform staff);
  - `remove_platform_role` = viewer is a super admin AND the row has a platform role AND not the row AND the row is not the primary AND (the row is not a super admin OR the viewer is the primary);
  - `delete` = viewer holds `user-destroy` AND not the row AND the row is not the primary AND (the row has no platform role OR the viewer is a super admin) AND (the row is not a super admin OR the viewer is the primary).
  - "Row is a super admin" here = the row's organization-0 role is named exactly `Super-Admin`.

For a super admin the answer also carries **`counts`** `{all, platform, organization}` — how many accounts each tab holds, whatever is searched or shown; for any other viewer no `counts` key at all. Tested (`AccountTabsAndSortTest`): the super admin's three tabs list everybody, the platform team, or everybody outside it; with `group=platform&search=Zara` the answer reads `total` 1 and `counts` exactly `{all: 4, platform: 2, organization: 2}`; `group=`, `group=everyone`, SQL and `group[]=platform` all read as every account; name A to Z whatever the case (`Ayesha, bilal, Musa, Zara`), Z to A, joined newest and oldest first, nothing asked for and `sort=password` each in their order; support sees the organizations' people alone with `group=platform`, no `counts`, and no tabs on the page.

`GET /users` (`global-tier` + `user-view`) renders the page headed **"Users"**: for a super admin first the tabs **All · Platform Team · Organization Members** (`accounts-tab-all` / `-platform` / `-organization`, the open one `aria-pressed`, each with its count), then the table **"Accounts"** with the search ("Search by name or email") and, for super admins, **Invite to Platform Team** on its line: columns **Account** (initials, name with "You" / "Primary" badges, email — a sorting heading), **Access** ("Platform · {role}" for a platform role, a "{role} · {organization}" badge per membership coloured by the role's key, or "No access"), **Joined** (a sorting heading, newest first when pressed first) and Actions (Log In As, Organizations, Remove Platform Role, Delete — each by the row's `can`). Super admins also get the platform invitations card and the platform roles (`Role::platform()` by name, `{id, name}`) for the invite picker — **without the Super-Admin role unless the viewer is the primary super admin** (tested), matching §9.3 step 4. A constant number of queries serves any page size (tested).

### 13.2 Delete an account — `DELETE /users/{user}` (`global-tier` + `user-destroy`), exact order
1. Reach: the actor is not a super admin AND the target holds a platform role → **404** (tested: support holding `user-destroy` gets 404 for platform staff and for the primary).
2. Target is the actor → 422 message-only **"You cannot delete your own account here."**
3. Target is the primary super admin → 403 **"The primary super admin cannot be deleted."**
4. Target is a super admin AND the actor is not the primary → 403 **"Only the primary super admin can delete a super admin."**
5. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
6. `ownerless` = names of the organizations this account solely owns (§8.1).
7. Delete the account (§15.3) — allowed even when it leaves organizations without an Owner.
8. Log `user.deleted` (subject NULL); 200 `{message: "{name}'s account was deleted.", ownerless_organizations: [names]}`.

The page's dialog (`confirm-account-deletion`) asks "Delete {name}'s account?" and says **"The account and its access to every organization are deleted. What they added stays with the organizations."** "This cannot be undone."; when the row's `sole_owner_of` is not empty it adds "They are the only Owner of {organizations}. That organization / Those organizations" and then only the ways back this viewer has: "will have no owner until you give it one — Invite owner on Organizations, or Organizations here on Users." (a super admin), "will have no owner until you give it one — Invite owner on Organizations." (holding `organization-view` and `organization-store`), else "will have no owner until one is given."; then the password (§20.8) and **Delete Account**. The success toast appends " Now without an owner: {A, B}." to the message when `ownerless_organizations` is not empty. Tested: deleting an organization's only Owner answers `ownerless_organizations` ["Alpha Mart"], the organization and its Staff member stay, and the log line contains "left without an Owner: Alpha Mart"; browser-tested through the dialog ("{name}'s account was deleted.").

### 13.3 Remove a platform role — `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role` (`global-tier` + `super-admin-tier`), exact order
1. The target has no organization-0 row → 404.
2. Target is the actor → 422 **"You cannot remove your own platform role."**
3. Target is the primary → 403 **"The primary super admin keeps their role."**
4. Target is a super admin AND the actor is not the primary → 403 **"Only the primary super admin can remove Super-Admin from someone."**
5. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
6. Delete the organization-0 row (the account stays, with no access); log `platform.role_removed` (subject = the user); 200 **"{name} is no longer on the platform team."**

There is no endpoint that changes a platform member's role in place: remove it, then invite again.

### 13.4 Users → Organizations: a person's place in the organizations, from the platform (super admins)
*Owner's rules, 2026-09-16 and 2026-09-17: the super admin gives any one person the access they need — an organization role made for it on the Roles page (§7.4), then this — and puts anybody in any organization with a role, changes that role or takes them out: straight in, nobody invited.* All four routes carry `global-tier` + `super-admin-tier`; `{user}` and `{organization}` are numeric, and a missing account or organization (a deleted one included) answers 404 at binding.

**`GET /users/{user}/organizations`** →
```
{
  memberships:  [{organization_id, organization_name, role_id}],          // the organizations the person belongs to, by organization name; role_id = the role held there
  organizations:       [{id, name, city}],                          // the organizations they are not in, by name
  organization_roles:  [{id, name, description}],    // Role::organizationRoles(): the Owner role first, then by lower-cased name
  custom_roles: {<organization_id>: [{id, name, description}]}   // every organization's custom roles by name, keyed by organization id (an empty object `{}` when there are none — tested)
}
```
`description` per §4.4. The roles an organization offers are `organization_roles` followed by `custom_roles[organization id]`. The platform row is not an organization, so a platform account gets no memberships and every organization in `organizations` — its row offers no Organizations button, and adding it is refused (below). Tested: Staff in Alpha, with Beta and Gamma existing → memberships exactly `[{organization_id: Alpha's id, organization_name: "Alpha Mart", role_id: Staff's id}]`, organizations `Beta Deli, Gamma Grill`, organization roles `Owner, Admin, Staff, Viewer` (the first one's id the Owner role's — no row carries an `is_owner_role` flag), custom roles Alpha → `Cashier`, Beta → `Baker`.

**`POST /users/{user}/organizations`** — add the person to an organization, exact order:
1. Validate: `organization_id` required (**"Choose an organization."**), integer; `role_id` required (**"Choose a role."**), integer → 422.
2. The person holds a platform role → 422 on `organization_id` **"{name} is on the platform team, which works above the organizations. Remove their platform role first."** (tested).
3. No organization with that id → 422 on `organization_id` **"Choose an organization that exists."**
4. `role = availableInOrganization(organization).find(role_id)` → null → 422 on `role_id` **"Choose a role that exists in this organization."** (tested exactly for another organization's custom role; 422 for a platform role).
5. Inside `changeTeam(organization)` (§8.1): the person is already a member → 422 on `organization_id` **"{name} is already in {organization}. Change their role there instead."** (tested exactly); otherwise insert the membership `(user, organization, role)`.
6. After commit: log `member.assigned` (subject = the person; organization = that organization) **"Added {name} ({email}) to {organization} as {role}, from the platform"**; **201** **"{name} is now {role} in {organization}."**

Tested: Nadia, in no organization, added to Beta Deli as Beta's custom role Baker → 201 exactly "Nadia New is now Baker in Beta Deli.", the membership exists, no invitation is created, and the entry carries Beta with exactly "Added Nadia New ({email}) to Beta Deli as Baker, from the platform"; added to Alpha Mart with the Owner role — making someone an Owner is giving them the Owner role, beside any Owner the organization already has — she holds the Owner role there.

**`PUT /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}/role`** — change the role held there, exact order:
1. `roleOf(user, organization)` → **404** when the person is not a member of that organization.
2. Validate `role_id`: required (**"Choose a role."**), integer → 422.
3. `role = availableInOrganization(organization).find(role_id)` → null → 422 on `role_id` **"Choose a role that exists in this organization."**
4. Inside `changeTeam(organization)`: `current = roleOf(user, organization)` → 404 when they have meanwhile left; `current` is the Owner role AND the new role is not AND `ownerCount(organization) == 1` → 422 on `role_id` **"{name} is the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner first."**; if the role differs from `current`, update the membership row.
5. After commit, when the role changed: log `member.role_changed` (subject = the person; organization = that organization) "Changed the role of {name} in {organization} from {old} to {new}, from the platform".
6. 200 **"{name} is now {role} in {organization}."**

Tested: Staff in Alpha and Viewer in Beta, moved to Alpha's custom role Area Manager → 200 exactly "{name} is now Area Manager in Alpha Mart.", the Beta role untouched, the entry carrying Alpha with exactly "Changed the role of {name} in Alpha Mart from Staff to Area Manager, from the platform"; the only Owner moved to Staff → 422 with exactly that message, and once a second Owner exists they become Admin (200).

**`DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}`** — take the person out of that organization, exact order:
1. `roleOf(user, organization)` → **404** when the person is not a member of that organization.
2. The person holds the Owner role there AND `ownerCount(organization) == 1` → 422 message-only **"{name} is the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner first."** — before the password, so nobody types one for a refused removal.
3. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` / 429.
4. Inside `changeTeam(organization)`: steps 1 and 2 again under the lock, then delete the membership row only (§15.2).
5. After commit: log `member.removed` (subject = the person; organization = that organization) "Removed {name} ({email}) from {organization}, from the platform"; 200 **"{name} was removed from {organization}."**

Tested: without a password → 422 "Password is required."; with it → 200 exactly "{name} was removed from Alpha Mart.", their Beta membership and their account kept, the entry carrying Alpha; again → 404; the only Owner → 422 with exactly the message above (the password sent).

No hierarchy check applies (`mayAssign` / `mayManage` are a member's rules): the super admin gives any role the organization has, the Owner role included; the one rule kept is at least one Owner. The person's roles in their other organizations are untouched and no invitation is created (tested). Support holding `user-view`, `member-update` and `member-remove`, and an organization Owner, get 403 on all four routes (tested).

## 14. Impersonation and self-service

### 14.1 Impersonation ("Log in as")
**Start — `POST /users/{user}/impersonate`** (the `global-tier` lock runs first, so an organization account gets 403 from the middleware). Guards in order: actor is not a super admin → 403 **"Only Super Admins can impersonate."**; target is a super admin → 403 **"Cannot impersonate another Super Admin."** The target is fetched by raw id (missing → 404) with **no scoping**. Platform staff who are not super admins **can** be impersonated.
- **Two session keys** make an impersonation: `impersonating_original_id` (whom to return to) and `impersonating_user_id` (whom the admin is viewing as).
- Mechanics, in order: log `impersonate.started` **before** the identity switch (actor = the admin, subject = the target); clear `current_organization_id`; authenticate as the target; **regenerate** the session id (regeneration keeps session data); THEN write both keys (original = the admin's id, user = the target's id). Go to the dashboard — or, for a target that has not confirmed its email, to **"Check your inbox"**, where the super admin may confirm it (Confirm Email and Continue, §10.1a). *The Users page asks with axios, which would follow a redirect into the `verified` wall and read its 403 as a failure* (owner, 2026-10-06), so a request that wants JSON is answered `{redirect: <that URL>}` and the page goes there itself; a plain request gets the redirect. Tested (`ImpersonateTest`): the Users page is told where Log In As leads — the dashboard, or "Check your inbox" for an account not confirmed. Exempt from the stale-tab refusal (§20.4a).
- **Every sign-in clears both keys** — a listener on the framework's `Login` event (password login, signup, registering from an invitation, remember-me re-authentication, and the impersonation's own logins). That is why `start` writes its keys after its own login. *Reason (a real hole, now closed): when the impersonated account was deleted mid-way, the session kept the super admin's id through the NEXT person's login on that browser (regeneration keeps session data), and "stop" then signed that person in as the super admin.*

**Stop — `POST /impersonate/stop`** (no permission gate — the impersonated person is not a super admin), exact order:
1. No `impersonating_original_id` → redirect to the dashboard (harmless no-op, no log).
2. `impersonating_user_id` is not the authenticated user → forget both keys and redirect to the dashboard — no logout, no log. *The way back belongs to the account being viewed as, and to nobody else* (tested).
3. The remembered admin no longer exists or is no longer a super admin → forget both keys, log out, invalidate the session, redirect to login — **no log row**.
4. Otherwise: log `impersonate.stopped` with **explicit actor = the returning admin** (subject NULL; description names both — tested: the row's actor is the admin, not the impersonated user); forget both keys and `current_organization_id`; authenticate as the admin; regenerate the session id; redirect to the dashboard.

While `impersonating_original_id` is in the session, one shared banner (`resources/views/components/impersonation-banner.blade.php`) shows "You are viewing as **{name}** ({email})." with a **Return to Super Admin** button — on the app layout, the focused layout (the organization picker, where "Log in as" a member of several organizations lands first), the Ad Builder editor's layout and "Check your inbox" (where "Log in as" an account not yet confirmed lands, §10.1a). `POST /impersonate/stop` is `auth` + `throttle:admin` only — never `verified`, so that way back always opens — and exempt from the stale-tab refusal (§20.4a). The `network-ads-toggle` gate (§3.4) is true only in a live impersonation (both keys, matching user, original still a super admin).

### 14.2 Profile (self-service)
- **Update — `PATCH /profile`** (`auth` only: an account not yet confirmed may correct its address here, §10.1a), exact order:
  1. **Lower-case the submitted email** (when it is a string) — lowered, not refused, so "Sana@Example.com" is saved as `sana@example.com` here as at signup (*one address is one account whatever its capitals*). Then validate: `first_name`, `last_name` required string max 255; `phone` required numeric digits:10; `email` `bail`, required, string, lowercase, email, max 255, unique in `users.email` ignoring the own row (a `pending_email` of another account is not looked at, §26.23).
  2. `newEmail` = the email when it differs from the account's. With one, **ask the mail budget first** (`linkRefusal()`, §10.1a): refused → back with the reason on `email` and the input kept — nothing is saved, not even the name.
  3. Name and phone are taken. A new address: **an account not yet confirmed** simply takes it (`email` changes, still unconfirmed); **a confirmed account** keeps its address and the new one waits in `pending_email` (owner's rule, 2026-09-29: *the account keeps its old address until the new one's link is opened, so a typo can never lock anybody out*). `email_verified_at` is never nulled.
  4. Logged `profile.updated` **only when something changed**: "Updated their own profile", followed by ", asking to change their email to {new}" (confirmed) or ", correcting their email to {new}" (not yet confirmed).
  5. No new address → the profile with status `profile-updated` (the toast "Your profile is saved.", §20.7). A new one → its link goes out (`sendNewEmailLink()` to the NEW address for a confirmed account, the signup's `sendVerificationLink()` for an unconfirmed one) and the profile with status `email-pending` / `verification-link-sent` — or with `error` = why it could not go out. The profile then says, beside the email: "We sent a link to {new}. Your email changes once you open it; until then it stays {email}." with **Send the Link Again** and **Keep My Current Email** (`profile-email-pending`), or, for an unconfirmed account, "Your email is not confirmed yet. Open the link we sent to {email} to use the panel." with Send the Link Again.
- **The changed address's link** — `GET /confirm-email/{id}/{hash}` (`auth`, `throttle:verification-link`; signed, for the account's id and `sha1(pending_email)`): no change waiting → the profile with `error` **"There is no change of email waiting to be confirmed."**; `whyNot` (§10.1a) → the profile with its reason; otherwise, in one transaction, another account already holding the address (looked up `FOR UPDATE`; a unique-index failure at the same moment counts the same) → `pending_email` cleared and `error` **"{new} is used by another account now, so your email stays {old}."** — *an address another account took in between is refused, never taken*; else `email` = the new address, `pending_email` NULL, `email_verified_at` = now, log `account.email_changed` ("Changed their email from {old} to {new}"), the profile with status `email-changed` ("Your email is now {email}."). Tested (`EmailChangeTest`, `ProfileTest`): a confirmed account keeps its address until the new one's link is opened; an unconfirmed one corrects its address and the link goes to the right one; a change can be given up or its link sent again; a link that is not right changes nothing and says why; each step said in its own words, never as a toast of its key; an address another account took meanwhile is not taken from it.
- **`POST /profile/email/resend`** — no change waiting → the profile; otherwise `sendNewEmailLink()` (the budget, §10.1a) → status `email-link-resent` ("A new link is on its way."), or `error`. Not logged. **`DELETE /profile/email`** ("Keep My Current Email") — clears `pending_email`, logs `profile.updated` "Gave up changing their email to {pending}", status `email-change-cancelled` ("Your email stays {email}.").
- **Password — `PUT /password`**: §19.5 — the current password goes through the same limited re-confirmation (§19.7).
- **Your organizations** — `GET /profile` is the **Profile** tab of Settings (heading "Settings", §20.3) and also renders, for accounts WITHOUT a platform role **and without an Organizations tab** (§20.3 — no `organization-view` in the organization they work in, or no organization chosen), a "Your organizations" section; whoever has the tab finds the same card there instead (§11.1). *Reason (owner's rule, 2026-09-17): leaving is open to every member whatever their role, so the card is always somewhere.* It lists `membershipsOf(actor)` (§8.1): every membership ordered by organization name (`organization-membership-{organization id}`), each with the role name (or "No role") and "city, state". An organization where the person is the only Owner shows **"You are its only Owner — make someone else an Owner before you leave."**; every other row has a **Leave** button (`leave-organization-{organization id}`) opening a confirmation ("Leave {organization}?", confirm `leave-organization-{organization id}-confirm`) that submits `DELETE /profile/organizations/{organization}` (§8.6). No memberships → "You are not a member of any organization yet." An error in bag `organizationMembership` is shown in a red alert at the top of the card. Platform accounts get no such section (tested); a role holding `organization-view` shows the Organizations tab and no "Your organizations" on the profile, a Viewer "Your organizations" and no tab (tested).
- **Self-delete — `DELETE /profile`**, exact order:
  1. The actor is a super admin → 403 **"Super Admins cannot delete their own account."** (the page hides the section for them). Platform staff who are not super admins may self-delete.
  2. **Sole-Owner refusal** — `organizationsSolelyOwnedBy(actor)` not empty → redirect back with an error on `password` in bag `userDeletion`: **"You are the only Owner of {organizations}. Make someone else an Owner of it, or delete that organization, first."** — `{organizations}` joined as "A", "A and B", "A, B and C"; with several organizations the end reads "Make someone else an Owner of them, or delete those organizations, first." (tested verbatim for one organization). The Delete Account section says beforehand: "You leave every organization. What you added stays with the organizations. An only Owner makes someone else an Owner first." Asked **before** the password, so nobody types one for a refused delete and a refusal never counts toward the limit.
  3. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) in bag `userDeletion`, field `password`: **"Password is required."** / **"The password is incorrect."** / the limiter's message.
  4. ONE transaction:
     1. `lockOrganizationsOwnedBy(actor)` (§8.1) — *so a co-owner leaving at the same moment is seen*;
     2. the sole-Owner refusal of step 2 again, under the lock — same error; the transaction rolls back;
     3. log `account.deleted` (subject NULL) **while still authenticated** (correct actor snapshot) — so a refused or failed delete leaves no such row;
     4. log out — BEFORE the row goes: *logging out writes a fresh remember token onto the user, and saving a deleted model would insert it again*;
     5. delete the account (§15.3).
  5. Invalidate the session and regenerate the CSRF token; redirect to `/`.

---

## 15. Deletion semantics (owner's rules — nothing cascades through people)

### 15.1 Principle
No deletion ever follows who created or invited whom. `created_by` everywhere is history and goes NULL when its account is deleted.

### 15.2 Remove a member / leave an organization
Deletes the one `organization_user` row — from the Members page (§8.5), by leaving (§8.6), or from the platform's Users → Organizations (§13.4). The person's account, their uploads, paired screens, playlists and the custom roles they made **stay with the organization** (tested: the media row keeps its `created_by`, the screen stays).

### 15.3 Delete an account (Profile §14.2, or the platform §13.2)
*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17: a deleted account leaves nothing pointing at it — and takes nothing anybody else holds.* One `DELETE FROM users WHERE id = ?` — inside the self-delete transaction (§14.2), or on its own from the platform (§13.2) — followed by the model's **`deleted` hook** (in the same transaction when there is one). There is no other application cascade. Database effects:
- `organization_user` rows → deleted (FK cascade): every membership, the platform row included;
- `invitations.invited_by` → NULL: invitations the person **sent** stay with their organizations (those **addressed to** the email go — see the hook below);
- `created_by` on organizations, roles, media, screens, campaigns, channels, channel ads, Ad Builder designs and assets, and `screens.paired_by`, `builder_ads.updated_by` and `builder_fonts.installed_by` → NULL (tested for media, screens, channels) — what the person made stays with its organizations;
- `activity_logs` keep the `actor_name` snapshot; `actor_id` → NULL through the FK on drivers that have it — and, on every driver, through the hook below (on partitioned MySQL there is no FK, §17.5).

The `deleted` hook then removes what no foreign key reaches:
- the account's `sessions` rows (`user_id` = the account) — its sign-ins on every device, `User::endEverySession()`, which a completed password reset calls too (§19.4) — **only when the session driver is `database`** (the configured default; other drivers have no table to clear, and a session whose account is gone is unauthenticated anyway, §24);
- the `password_reset_tokens` row for its email — an emailed reset link stops working;
- every invitation **addressed to** its email (`User::invitationsToEmail()`: `invitations.email` = the account's email lower-cased and trimmed, §9.1) — to any organization or to the platform team, expired ones included (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: "account delete hote hi uske email ki pending invitations bhi hat jayein"). The deletion's log line counts them (§17.2);
- `activity_logs.actor_id` = the account → NULL (*the log forgets whose id it was: on MySQL the table is partitioned and cannot carry a foreign key, so nothing else would empty it*); each entry keeps the person's name in `actor_name`.

Organizations whose only Owner was deleted become ownerless (§8.2). Nothing else is touched (tested: colleagues, organizations and their content remain; a person who invited others is deleted alone). *(The hook also cleared the account's API tokens until Sanctum was deleted, §26.8 — there is no token table left to clear.)* Tested (from the platform, on the `database` session driver): the account's memberships in two organizations, its two `sessions` rows and its reset token are gone, another person's session stays, and its screen's `created_by` is NULL. Tested (invitations): deleting "Sam.Person@Example.com" from the platform removes Beta's invitation to "sam.person@example.com" and a platform invitation to the same address, keeps another address's invitation and the one Sam sent (its `invited_by` now NULL), and logs exactly "Deleted the account of {name} (Sam.Person@Example.com) and the 2 pending invitations to that email"; a self-deletion removes the invitation waiting for the person's email and logs "Deleted their own account ({email}) and the 1 pending invitation to that email".

### 15.4 Delete an organization (Settings → Organizations §11.4, or the platform's Organizations page §12.4)
*Owner's rule, 2026-09-17: "A to Z" — a deleted organization goes for good, with everything it owns but the people's accounts.* A model hook on the **deleting** event runs `purgeContents()` inside the caller's transaction, **before** the organization row is deleted — *the foreign keys would otherwise take the media rows with the row and leave their files on disk with no row to name them* — in this order:
1. every `organization_user` row of the organization (memberships);
2. every invitation of the organization;
3. every screen of the organization — their playlists, schedule rules, campaign links and pairing requests go through foreign keys, and each device is locked out on its next request (401, tested);
4. every role with `organization_id` = the organization (its custom roles; their permission links cascade);
5. the organization's own channels (`purgeChannels()`, `channels.organization_id` = the organization): the rows — which take their ads and every playlist line carrying them through foreign keys. Their files are rows of the organization's library (§6.6), which step 6 takes;
6. the media library: all media rows of the organization, **whoever uploaded them**, deleted in slices of 500 (which takes each file off every playlist, clears any screen's holding picture and removes any ad of the platform's channel that showed one of them, through foreign keys); the files (original and thumbnail) are unlinked **only after the transaction commits**, and only the paths those rows name — never a whole folder;
7. the Ad Builder (`purgeBuilder()`): every `builder_assets` and `builder_ads` row of the organization — its shelf and its designs, whoever made them — and, **only after the transaction commits**, the files those rows name: each asset's file and thumbnail, each design's poster. A published design's page is a media row, which step 6 has already taken with its files;
8. the uploads still on their way into it (`purgeUploads()`, docs/UPLOADS-SPEC.md): the `uploads` rows of the organization, and each one's part only after the transaction commits, by the name its row gives — never the folder.

Then the **organization row itself is deleted** — a real delete; there is no `deleted_at` (§2.2). Whatever a foreign key still ties to the row would go with it — `screens`, `media`, `invitations`, `channels`, `builder_ads`, `builder_assets` and `roles.organization_id` all cascade (§2.3, §2.7, §2.9) — a backstop the purge leaves nothing for (tested: deleting an organization row straight in the database takes its custom role).

*Reasons (owner): hung on the model event rather than written into each delete path, so a third path could never forget part of it; files after commit, so a rolled-back delete never leaves rows pointing at files that are already gone (tested); only named paths, because a folder can hold something no row accounts for and deleting on a guess is how real uploads were once lost.* Accounts stay — a member who worked under the organization's custom role included; the platform's channels and other organizations' channels stay (tested — the platform's own ad and its file too, while an ad of the platform's channel that showed one of the organization's files goes with that file); other organizations are untouched, a shared member's role in them included (tested). The organization's activity entries stay too — `activity_logs` has no foreign key to organizations (§2.8), and the entries keep the organization's id and, in their words, its name. *A path that bypasses the model's delete event would skip the purge: the foreign keys would take most rows, but not the memberships (`organization_user.organization_id` has no foreign key, §2.6) and not the files.*

Tested (from Settings → Organizations): the organization row is gone with its memberships, its invitation, its custom role, its screen and that screen's playlist lines (the device then answers 401), its media row and both files, and its own channel with its ad — whose file, a row of the organization's library, goes with both of its files; the platform's channel and its ad, the accounts and a neighbouring organization stay; the organization's `screen.paired` entry stays, and `organization.deleted` names the organization with "1 screen" and "2 media files" (the poster and its channel's file). From the platform: the whole library of three files (whoever uploaded them) with their files, the screen and its playlist lines, the organization row, and exactly "Deleted organization Alpha Mart with its 0 screens and 2 media files" for an organization with two files; a neighbour's library untouched; a delete rolled back inside its transaction keeps the organization, the media row and both files.

### 15.5 Delete a role
§7.8 — refused while held; its open invitations are revoked explicitly in the same transaction, and the response and the log say how many.

### 15.6 Invitations
Accepting, declining and revoking delete the row; so does the platform's "Invite owner" on an organization that has no Owner, for its Owner invitations to any other address — whether it invites somebody or makes a member Owner (§10.3); so do deleting the invitation's role (§7.8) and deleting its organization (§15.4). Revoking asks for no password (§19.7). Expired rows stay until resent (renewed), revoked, declined or replaced — nothing prunes them.

---

## 16. Permission management (`permission-*`, super admins only)

All routes carry `super-admin-tier` AND the action's permission (tested: a platform user or an organization user who somehow holds `permission-*` gets 403 everywhere, including the rename-escalation attempt; a platform user holding **every** permission row still gets 403 on `/permissions`). For a super admin both always pass (§3.3) — tested: a super admin whose role holds no rows lists and creates permissions.
- `GET /permissions/data`: paginated listing (§20.6), searchable by `name`, no explicit order.
- **Create** `POST /permissions`: `name` required, string, max 255, unique; `label` nullable, string, max 255, regex (§2.4). Log `permission.created`; 200 `{message: "Permission created successfully", permission}`. **The new row gates nothing** (no route names it); a check on its name answers yes for super admins at once (§3.3) and no for everybody else until a role carries it. It is attached to the Super-Admin role only when the seeder runs — which no longer changes anybody's access (§26.6).
- **Update** `PUT /permissions/{permission}`: the same rules, uniqueness ignoring itself. Log `permission.updated`; 200 `{message: "Permission updated successfully", permission}`.
- **Delete** `DELETE /permissions/{permission}`, double-guarded in this order: attached to any role → 422 message-only **"This permission is assigned to one or more roles and cannot be deleted. Remove it from those roles first."**; then **password re-confirmation** (§19.7) → 422 on `password` (**"Password is required."** / **"The password is incorrect."**) or 429. Log `permission.deleted` (subject NULL); 200 "Permission deleted successfully". *Reason: deleting a permission silently rewrites what every role can do.*

## 16a. Developer → TV App (`player-app-*`, super admins only)

*Owner, 2026-10-10 — "mein new apk apne super admin mein upload karu waha super admin mein developer ka tab ho sub se end mein": the super admin uploads the Android player built with the release key, and every TV with the app is offered the newest version.* The signage side (reading the APK, the Android updater) is out of scope here; what touches the people model:
- **Who:** `/developer/*` carries `super-admin-tier` AND the action's permission (§3.1); the three permissions are `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY`, so no role but Super-Admin can be given them (`permissionsFor` names them, §7.6), and a row reaching another role by hand still meets the tier lock (tested, `PlayerAppAttackTest`: every endpoint shut to anybody but the super admin, whatever their role was given by hand). The sidebar's **Developer** link is the last one, for `super-admin-tier` AND `player-app-view` (§20.2). Opening the chunked upload for an APK (purpose `app-release`) asks `super-admin-tier` and `player-app-store` too.
- **Upload** (`POST /developer/app/inspect`, then `POST /developer/app/releases`): a version the file says is the one kept, whatever is typed; another package → **"This is another app ({package}), not the TV app."**; the version code must be newer than the one TVs are offered now; a second tab's same version code → **"Version code {code} has just been uploaded. Reload the page."**; logs `player_app.uploaded`; 200 "Version {name} is uploaded: every TV with the app is offered it the next time it starts, or within a day."
- **Delete** (`DELETE /developer/app/releases/{release}`) — one confirmation, **no password** (§19.7: the file can be uploaded again); the row and its file go; logs `player_app.deleted`.
- **What a TV reads** — `GET /player-app/latest.json` (`player-app.latest`): **open**, no session needed (a television has none), the named limiter `player-app` (60 a minute per IP, §19.6), never cached; 404 "No version of the TV app has been uploaded yet." while none is uploaded. It reads and writes nothing of anybody's — tested (`PlayerAppAttackTest`): it takes nothing, writes nothing and is limited per address. `HttpSurfaceSweepTest` lists it among a guest's public doors.

---

## 17. Activity logging (audit trail)

### 17.1 Write rule
Every state-changing endpoint writes one row: `record(action, subject?, description, actor?, organizationId?)`.
- `actor_id` + snapshot `actor_name` (display always uses the snapshot, which survives the actor's deletion — tested). The name is cut to 255 characters and the description to 1000 (*a first and a last name may be 255 each, and on MySQL in strict mode a longer value is an error — the log line that failed was the sign-out's, so the person could never sign out*). The actor defaults to the authenticated user; flows whose actor is not (or not yet) the authenticated user pass it **explicitly** — password reset via email link, invitation registration and acceptance, and impersonation stop (attributed to the returning admin); a write with neither falls back to `actor_name = 'System'`.
- `subject_type` = the subject's short class name; deletions and subject-less events log subject NULL.
- **`organization_id` — which organization the entry belongs to** (owner's rules, 2026-09-16; it decides who may read it, §17.4): the explicit `organizationId` when given — passed wherever the subject is gone (a delete: `organization.deleted`, `role.deleted`, `account.pruned` and the signage deletes) or is a person (`member.role_changed`, `member.removed`, `member.assigned`), and by signup for its new organization (`user.registered`); otherwise the subject's — an Organization is its own id, any other subject its `organization_id` attribute (a screen, a media file, an organization's custom role, an organization's channel); otherwise NULL. A User subject carries none, so an account's own sign-in, profile and password, impersonation, and the platform's own work (permissions, platform invitations, organization roles and platform roles, campaigns, maintenance) belong to no organization; entries logged before the column existed have none either. Tested: `media.updated` of Beta's file → Beta; `organization.updated` → that organization; `member.removed` with an explicit organization → it; `password.changed` and `campaign.created` → none. *A new mutation endpoint inside an organization makes sure its entry carries the organization.*
- Rows for transactional mutations are written after the transaction commits (e.g. every `changeTeam` change, joins, role changes). Deliberate exceptions: `account.deleted` is written INSIDE the self-delete transaction, before logout and the delete, so it is rolled back with a refused or failed delete (§14.2); `impersonate.started` is written before the identity switch.
- Invitation rows (`member.invited`, `platform.invited`, `invitation.resent`, `organization.created`, `organization.owner_invited`) are written whether or not the email went out; the response's `email_sent` says which (§9.1).

### 17.2 Action catalog (people, access and platform)
"organization" = the entry's `organization_id` (§17.1): "explicit" when passed as `organizationId`, else from the subject.

| action | subject | organization | actor | description written | by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `auth.login` | — | — | the user | "Signed in" | successful `POST /login` |
| `auth.logout` | — | — | the user | "Signed out" | `POST /logout`, only when authenticated |
| `password.changed` | user | — | the user | "Changed their own password" | `PUT /password` |
| `password.reset` | user | — | explicit: the user | "Reset their password via email link" | successful `POST /reset-password` |
| `profile.updated` | user | — | the user | "Updated their own profile" + ", asking to change their email to {new}" (a confirmed account) or ", correcting their email to {new}" (one not yet confirmed) when the address changed / "Gave up changing their email to {pending}" | `PATCH /profile`, only when something changed; `DELETE /profile/email` when a change was waiting (§14.2) |
| `account.verified` | user | — | the user / explicit: the user (reset) / explicit: the super admin | "Confirmed their email {email}" (the signup's link) / "Confirmed their email {email} by an invitation's link" / "Confirmed their email {email} by a password-reset link" / "Confirmed the email {email} of {name} for them, by Log In As" | `GET /verify-email/{id}/{hash}`, `POST /invitations/{token}/accept`, `POST /reset-password`, `POST /verify-email/confirm-for-them` — each only for an account not yet confirmed (§10.1a) |
| `account.email_changed` | user | — | the user | "Changed their email from {old} to {new}" | `GET /confirm-email/{id}/{hash}` |
| `account.pruned` | — | explicit: the first organization removed with it / — | 'System' | "Removed {email}, never confirmed within 7 days" + ", with the organization {names}" when it made any | `accounts:prune-unverified` (§10.1a) |
| `account.deleted` | — | — | the user | "Deleted their own account ({email})" + " and the {n} pending invitation(s) to that email" when any | `DELETE /profile` |
| `user.registered` | user | explicit: the new organization | the new user | "Self-registered: {name} ({email}) with organization {organization}" | `POST /register` |
| `user.registered` | user | — | explicit: the new user | "Created an account from an invitation: {name} ({email})" | `POST /invitations/{token}/register` |
| `user.deleted` | — | — | actor | "Deleted the account of {name} ({email})" + " and the {n} pending invitation(s) to that email" when any + " — left without an Owner: {A, B}" when any | `DELETE /users/{user}` |
| `platform.invited` | — | — | actor | "Invited {email} to the platform team as {role}" | `POST /users/invitations` |
| `platform.role_removed` | user | — | actor | "Removed the platform role {role} from {name} ({email})" | `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role` |
| `member.invited` | organization | the organization | actor | "Invited {email} to {organization} as {role}" | `POST /members/invitations` |
| `invitation.resent` | organization / — | the organization / — | actor | "Resent the invitation for {email} to {organization}" / "Resent the platform invitation for {email}" | resend endpoints |
| `invitation.revoked` | organization / — | the organization / — | actor | "Revoked the invitation for {email} to {organization}" / "Revoked the platform invitation for {email}" | revoke endpoints |
| `invitation.accepted` | organization / user (platform) | the organization / — | explicit: the joining user | "{name} ({email}) joined {place} as {role}" — or, when already a member, "{name} ({email}) used an invitation to {organization}, where they were already a member" | accept and register by the link; Accept on the dashboard (§9.5a) |
| `invitation.declined` | organization / — | the organization / — | the signed-in user, else the account holding that email, else 'System' | "{email} declined the invitation to {place}" / on the dashboard "{email} declined the invitation to {organization} on their dashboard" | decline of an existing invitation by the link; Decline on the dashboard (§9.5a) |
| `member.assigned` | member | explicit: the organization | actor | "Added {name} ({email}) to {organization} as {role}, from the platform" | `POST /users/{user}/organizations` |
| `member.role_changed` | member | explicit: the organization | actor | "Changed the role of {name} in {organization} from {old} to {new}" — from the platform + ", from the platform" | `PUT /members/{user}`, `PUT /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}/role` — when the role changes |
| `member.removed` | member | explicit: the organization | actor | "Removed {name} ({email}) from {organization}" — from the platform + ", from the platform" | `DELETE /members/{user}`, `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}` |
| `member.left` | organization | the organization | actor | "Left {organization}" | `POST /members/leave`, `DELETE /profile/organizations/{organization}` |
| `organization.created` | organization | the organization | actor | "Created organization {name} and invited {email} to own it" (platform) / "Opened organization {name}, owned by {actor}" (Create organization on Settings → Organizations) | `POST /organizations`, `POST /settings/organization/open` |
| `organization.updated` | organization | the organization | actor | "Updated organization {name}" (Organizations page) / "Updated the details of organization {name}" (Settings → Organizations) | `PUT /organizations/{organization}`, `PUT /settings/organization` |
| `organization.paused` / `organization.resumed` | organization | the organization | actor | "Paused organization {name}" / "Turned organization {name} back on" | `PUT /organizations/{organization}` when `is_active` changes — in place of `organization.updated` (§12.3) |
| `organization.billing_updated` | organization | the organization | actor | "{Unlocked\|Locked} {Premium Templates\|Platform Channels} for {organization}" — one entry per switch changed | `PUT /organizations/{organization}/billing` (§12.1a) |
| `organization.deleted` | — | explicit: the deleted organization | actor | "Deleted organization {name} with its {n} screen[s] and {m} media file[s]" | both delete paths |
| `organization.switched` | organization | the organization | actor | "Switched into organization {name}" | `POST /organizations/switch` |
| `organization.owner_invited` | organization | the organization | actor | "Invited {email} to own {organization}" — or, renewed, "Sent the invitation to own {organization} to {email} again" — + ", replacing the invitation for {emails}" when any were replaced | `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation` |
| `organization.owner_assigned` | organization | the organization | actor | "Made {name} ({email}) an Owner of {organization}" + ", replacing the invitation for {emails}" when any were replaced | same, member made Owner |
| `role.created` | role | a custom role's organization / — | actor | "Created organization role {name}, offered in every organization" / "Created platform role {name}" / "Created role {name} in {organization}" (a custom role) | `POST /roles` |
| `role.updated` | role | a custom role's organization / — | actor | "Updated organization role {name}{was}, in every organization" / "Updated platform role {name}{was}" / "Updated role {name}{was} in {organization}, from the platform" (a custom role, on the platform) / "Updated role {name}{was}" (a custom role, inside its organization) — `{was}` = " (was {old name})" when renamed | `PUT /roles/{role}` |
| `role.deleted` | — | explicit: a custom role's organization / — | actor | "Deleted role {name}." + " {n} pending invitation[s] to it {was/were} revoked." when any | `DELETE /roles/{role}` |
| `permission.created` / `permission.updated` | permission | — | actor | "Created permission {name}" / "Updated permission {name}" | permissions |
| `permission.deleted` | — | — | actor | "Deleted permission {name}" | permissions |
| `player_app.uploaded` / `player_app.deleted` | release / — | — | actor | "Uploaded version {name} (code {code}) of the TV app" / "Deleted version {name} (code {code}) of the TV app" | Developer → TV App (§16a) |
| `impersonate.started` | target user | — | the admin | "Logged in as {name} ({email})" | impersonation start |
| `impersonate.stopped` | — | — | explicit: the returning admin | "Returned to {admin} from impersonating {user}" | impersonation stop |
| `activity.maintenance` | — | — | actor / 'System' | "Activity log maintenance — partitions created: {years}; dropped (with data): {years}" / "Scheduled maintenance — partitions created: …; dropped (with data): …" | §17.5 |

Pinned by tests: the `organization.deleted` wording (e.g. exactly "Deleted organization Alpha Mart with its 0 screens and 2 media files"), the `role.deleted` wording (exactly "Deleted role Cashier. 2 pending invitations to it were revoked."), the `role.created` wordings (exactly "Created organization role Floor Lead, offered in every organization" and "Created platform role Support"), the `role.updated` wordings (exactly "Updated organization role Cashier (was Staff), in every organization" and "Updated role Till (was Cashier) in Beta Deli, from the platform"), the `member.assigned` wording (exactly "Added Nadia New ({email}) to Beta Deli as Baker, from the platform"), the from-the-platform `member.role_changed` wording (exactly "Changed the role of {name} in Alpha Mart from Staff to Area Manager, from the platform"), the `organization.owner_assigned` wording (exactly "Made {name} ({email}) an Owner of Orphan Mart, replacing the invitation for partner@example.com"), the `member.role_changed` fragment "from Staff to Admin", the `user.deleted` fragment "left without an Owner: Alpha Mart", the `organization.billing_updated` wordings (exactly "Locked Premium Templates for Smart Stop", "Unlocked Premium Templates for Smart Stop", "Locked Platform Channels for Smart Stop", each carrying the organization), the order `organization.paused`, `organization.resumed`, `organization.updated` of a pause, a resume and a rename, `account.verified`'s super admin as actor with "by Log In As" in its words, and its "password-reset link"; and the organization carried by `organization.deleted`, `organization.created` (Create organization on Settings → Organizations), `member.assigned`, `member.role_changed` and `member.removed` (from the platform) and `role.deleted` (a custom role deleted from the platform). Other descriptions and success messages not quoted in bold are human text.

Signage actions (outside this document): `media.uploaded`, `media.renamed` (a file keeps its name alone, so its one edit is a rename), `media.deleted`, `screen.paired`, `screen.repaired`, `screen.updated`, `screen.deleted`, `screen.playlist_updated`, `screen.playlist_copied`, `channel.created`, `channel.updated`, `channel.deleted`, `channel.ad_added`, `channel.ad_updated`, `channel.ad_removed`, `channel.ads_reordered`, `campaign.created`, `campaign.updated`, `campaign.deleted`, `organization.network_ads_updated`, `screen.network_ads_updated`, and the Ad Builder's `ad.created`, `ad.updated`, `ad.duplicated`, `ad.copied` (Use This Template), `ad.published`, `ad.unpublished`, `ad.changes_discarded`, `ad.recompiled`, `ad.deleted`, `ad_asset.uploaded`, `ad_asset.deleted`, `ad_font.installed`, `ad_font.updated`. Each carries the organization of its screen, file, channel, design or asset (the deletes name it explicitly; tested for `channel.created` and `channel.deleted` of an organization's own channel); a platform channel's entries, the campaigns', a many-organization network-advertising switch and a font installed for the whole installation (`ad_font.installed`) carry none.

**Removed with the old model:** `user.created`, `user.updated`, `user.assigned`, `user.unassigned`, `user.onboarded`. **No longer written since the third round of 2026-09-17:** `organization.ownership_transferred` (the Organizations tab's handover, "Transferred ownership of {organization} to {name} ({email}), and became {role}" / "…, and stayed {role}" — §11.3) and the in-organization `user.deleted` "Deleted the account of {name} ({email}) from {organization}" (the Accounts page an organization once had — §13); entries written before stay in the log, the latter with its organization.

### 17.3 Deliberate exclusions (owner-accepted; do NOT log)
Failed login attempts (flood risk; the throttle handles abuse), forgot-password link requests (guest noise; the actual reset IS logged), a request refused at its password re-confirmation — missing, wrong or rate-limited (§19.7; the limiter counts the wrong ones), profile submits that change nothing, a role "change" to the same role, scheduled maintenance that did nothing, the impersonation stop no-op and failure paths, a decline of an unknown token, an accept whose link was used a moment ago (§9.5 step 6), a stop request from an account that is not the one being viewed as, and session-only organization context changes other than an explicit switch (`ChooseTheOnlyOrganization` choosing or forgetting one, the dashboard's auto-select, the session switch that comes with accepting). Asking for a verification link again — the signup's or a changed email's (`POST /verify-email/resend`, `POST /profile/email/resend`): a mail request, like forgot-password; a signup sent back by the robot traps (nothing is written at all, §10.1); a request refused as from a stale tab (§20.4a), by `verified` or by a paused organization (nothing was done); confirming an account already confirmed; and the chunks of an upload (`/uploads` — the file is logged when it joins its place, by the door that adds it). Invitations removed as part of a larger change get no row of their own: those revoked by a role deletion are counted in `role.deleted`, and Owner invitations replaced by "Invite owner" are named in `organization.owner_invited` or `organization.owner_assigned`.

### 17.4 Reading — `GET /activity`, `GET /activity/data` (`activity-view`)
- **Who, and what (owner's rules, 2026-09-16):**
  - **above the organizations** — the super admin, and any platform account whose role carries `activity-view` — **every** entry, the platform's own and every organization's;
  - **inside an organization** — an organization's role carrying `activity-view` — `currentOrganization()` (404), then only the entries whose `organization_id` is that organization: never another organization's, and never one without an organization (personal, platform, or logged before the column existed). Tested: an Owner given `activity-view` reads exactly "Paired screen Alpha Window" out of four entries (Beta's screen, a permission the super admin created and the Owner's own profile update are not shown) while the super admin reads all four; the page reads "Activity in Alpha Mart" without the storage panel; the Activity Log link is offered.
  - The page's table is titled "All Activity" above the organizations and "Activity in {organization}" inside one.
- Newest first (id DESC). LIKE search across `action`, `description`, `actor_name`. Paginated per §20.6 (50 rows).
- **Date-range bounding (timezone-correct):** optional `from` / `to`, each either a full ISO-8601 instant (what the UI sends) or a plain `Y-m-d` date; validated as a date (malformed → 422, never silently ignored — tested). `created_at` is stored in UTC and each bound is normalized to a UTC datetime: a date-only value covers the whole day (`from` → 00:00:00, `to` → 23:59:59 of that date, UTC); a full instant is used as-is. `from` → `created_at >= from`; `to` → `created_at <= to`. *Reason: partition pruning — MySQL scans only the years in range.*
- UI presets: the date inputs hold the viewer's LOCAL date (YYYY-MM-DD). Today = today..today; Last 7 days = today−7..today; **Last 30 days = today−30..today (default)**; This year = Jan 1..today; Custom = keep the dates for direct editing (any manual date edit switches to Custom). Every change resets to page 1 and refetches. There is deliberately no "All time" preset (owner removed it).
- **Local-day boundaries are sent as UTC instants:** `from` → local 00:00:00 of that date `.toISOString()`, `to` → local 23:59:59.999 `.toISOString()` — correct in every timezone, and a freshly written log (stamped in server UTC) is never hidden, because local end-of-today in UTC is always ≥ now (tested with a UTC−5 viewer). Timestamps are rendered with the browser's `toLocaleString()`.

### 17.5 Retention and yearly partitions (MySQL; other drivers emulate)
- RANGE partitioned by `YEAR(created_at)`. MySQL constraints that shaped it: the partition key must be in every unique key → **PK (id, created_at)**; `YEAR()` over TIMESTAMP is rejected → `created_at` is **DATETIME**; partitioned tables cannot hold foreign keys → the `actor_id` FK is dropped (so the account's `deleted` hook empties `actor_id` itself, §15.3). Non-MySQL drivers skip partitioning.
- Layout: `p{YYYY}` per named year + a **`pmax` MAXVALUE catch-all** (if maintenance never runs, new years land in pmax instead of failing INSERTs — and every action writes a log, so a failing INSERT would take the whole app down). Initial partitioning opens the same window maintenance enforces: p{Y}, p{Y+1}, p{Y+2} + pmax.
- **Maintenance (idempotent):** (1) FIRST drop every named partition with year < currentYear−1 — partition AND data, instantly (retention = current + previous year); (2) THEN reorganize pmax to open whichever of current, +1, +2 are missing, re-homing rows that accumulated there.
