Drop-before-create is an owner decision. Non-MySQL: `DELETE WHERE created_at < Jan 1 of (currentYear−1)`; `created` is always `[]` and `dropped` lists `{year, rows}` per calendar year that had rows (counted before the delete). MySQL retention is per whole year; the emulation cuts at the exact date — both keep "current + previous year".
- **Manual run — `POST /activity/partitions/maintain`** (`global-tier` + `activity-destroy`; delegable to a platform role — tested — and never to an organization's role, §7.6: it drops a whole year of every organization's history at once; an organization user whose role somehow holds it still gets 403 from the tier lock — tested): logs on EVERY run, `Activity log maintenance — partitions created: {years}; dropped (with data): {years}` (comma-joined, empty → `none`); response `{message: "Maintenance complete", created: [year…], dropped: [{year, rows}…]}`. The UI toasts "Maintenance complete — opened: …; deleted: … (N rows)" or "Maintenance complete — nothing to do", then refetches the status and the listing. The storage panel and its **Run Yearly Maintenance** button render only above the organizations — the page has no current organization — for holders of `global-tier` AND `activity-destroy` (tested: a platform reader holding only `activity-view` and an organization role holding both do not see it; a platform keeper does).
- **Scheduled run** — monthly on the 1st at 00:30 (needs the scheduler's cron entry): logs ONLY when something was created or dropped, prefix `Scheduled maintenance — …`, actor 'System'.
- **Status — `GET /activity/partitions`** (`global-tier` + `activity-view`): `{driver, partitions: [{name, year, rows}], cutoffYear}` with `cutoffYear = currentYear − 1`; `year` null for pmax; a named partition's rows = the count for that calendar year; pmax's rows = the count with `created_at ≥ Jan 1 of (max named year + 1)`. Non-MySQL synthesizes one `p{year}` entry per calendar year present in the data. The status counts every organization's rows, which is why it keeps the tier lock (tested: 403 for an organization role holding `activity-view`, 200 for a platform reader). The UI fetches it only when the storage panel renders.

---

## 18. Dashboard and organization selection

`GET /dashboard` (`auth`, `verified`; outside the admin throttle and outside `organization.active`, so it can say why a paused organization is closed). *Owner, 2026-09-30 — "user friendly banao puri site ko": the dashboard was an empty page.* What each view holds is decided, and permission-checked, in `App\Services\DashboardSummary`: every part only with the permission its own page asks, every link one the person may open (`DashboardSummaryTest` opens every one), counts only, and no buttons of its own (Pair a Screen, Upload Files and Create Ad are on their own pages).
- **Platform account** (`globalRole() != null`) → the platform's summary (`forPlatform`): the card **Organizations** (how many, and "{n} active"; linked with `organization-view` — the one figure every platform account is shown), **Accounts** (`user-view`: the accounts its Users page lists to the viewer, "{n} not yet confirmed"), **Screens** (`screen-view`: every screen, "{n} online now"), **Campaigns** (`campaign-manage`: those switched on), and for a super admin **Free on the server** ("uploads pause below {reserve}"); **Needs attention** (`organization-view`): each organization with no Owner (five named, the rest counted, "Invite one from the Organizations page") and, for a super admin, a server running low on space; **Recent activity**, the latest five log lines (`activity-view`). A platform account never gets the organization views, even with a stray organization row (tested).
- **Organization account** — above every one of its views, **"Invitations for you"** when any of the organizations' invitations to its own address is still open (§9.5a), and a flashed `invitation-problem` in a red alert. *The card's earlier absence rested on signup not confirming an address; every page of the panel now stands behind `verified`, so the account has proved its inbox (owner, 2026-10-07).* Then:
  - **with no memberships** → the empty state: heading **"You're not a member of any organization yet"**, text "Ask an organization's owner to invite {their email}. The invitation shows up here and in that inbox." and **Check Your Email in Settings** (`dashboard-empty-settings`, → the profile);
  - **with memberships** — resolve the current organization: the session's organization if it is one of the account's organizations; else, with exactly **one** organization, select it and remember it in the session (no log — `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization` has usually done so already, §5.1); else redirect to `GET /select-organization`, keeping any flash for one more request (*so "Invitation declined." or a deleted organization's goodbye is shown on the picker instead of vanishing on the way through*);
  - **the organization paused** (`is_active` false, §2.2) → `dashboard-paused`: "{organization} is paused" — "Its pages are closed for now, and its screens keep playing. Contact support to turn it back on." — with **Choose Another Organization** (→ the chooser) for somebody of several organizations;
  - otherwise **the organization's summary** (`forOrganization`), every count of that organization alone: cards **Screens** (`screen-view`: "{n} online now", or "None paired yet"), **Files** (`media-view`: the photographs and videos its Media page lists, and "{used} of {limit} used"), **Ads** (`ad-view`: published, "· {n} drafts"), **Channels** (`channel-view`: "made by this organization"); **Needs attention** (with `screen-view` or `media-view`: each screen offline — "Last seen …" or "It has never connected" — each screen with nothing to play, five named and the rest counted, and storage 90% full); **Get your organization on screen**, its first steps until all are done — pair a screen (`screen-view` + `screen-store`), upload a picture or a video (`media-view` + `media-store`), put something on a screen (`screen-playlist`, once a screen exists); **Recent activity**, the latest five log lines of the organization (`activity-view`).
- Tested (`DashboardTest`, `DashboardSummaryTest`, `DashboardForEveryRoleTest`): the super admin's Organizations card shows the real count; a custom platform role sees the platform's dashboard; a single-organization member is auto-selected; a multi-organization member with no organization chosen is sent to the selection page; an organization's dashboard counts that organization alone; each part needs its own permission and every link opens for the person given it; what needs attention, five named and the rest counted; first steps ticked as they are done, gone once all are; recent activity the organization's own, and only for a role that may read the log; the platform's dashboard by its permissions, an organization with no Owner said; a server low on space on the super admin's.

**Sent to choose, returned to the page.** Besides the dashboard, any page (a non-JSON `GET`) refused 403 for a signed-in person with no platform role, no organization chosen and more than one organization is redirected to the chooser with the page remembered as the intended URL (`bootstrap/app.php`), and `POST /organizations/switch` returns them there (§12.6) — *never "Your role does not allow it" (QA round, 2026-10-09)*. A test of "no organization chosen" uses a person of two organizations (`inASecondOrganization()` in `tests/Pest.php`), since a person of one is chosen at once.

`GET /select-organization` (`auth`, `verified`): a platform account, or an organization account with ≤ 1 organization, is redirected to the dashboard. Otherwise the page **"Choose an organization"** — "Choose the organization you want to work in." — shows one card per organization: name, "city, state", **Active** or **Paused**, **"Your Role"** = the role name in that organization (one batched query; unresolvable → **"No role"**), and **Open Organization** (`switch-organization-{id}`), which posts to `POST /organizations/switch` (the whole card is the click target). The organization's slug is not shown (*nobody's business on this page*). The same number of queries serves ten organizations as two (tested).

---

## 19. Validation and authentication catalog

### 19.1 Error shapes (the UI depends on all of them)
- **JSON field errors:** 422 `{message, errors: {field: [messages…]}}` — painted under the matching inputs.
- **JSON guard errors:** 403 / 404 / 422 `{message: "…"}` — surfaced as a toast (or inline in a delete dialog), never swallowed. **Every JSON error has words a page can show** (QA round, 2026-10-09 — before, a bare `abort(404)` answered `"message": ""`, a missing model said "No query results for model […]" and a server error "Server Error"): the exception handler (`bootstrap/app.php`, `respond`) rewrites, for a request that wants JSON, an empty 404 or a framework one ("No query results…", "The route …") → **"This is no longer here: it may have been deleted, or changed in another tab. Reload the page to see what is there now."**; a gate's default 403 ("This action is unauthorized.") → **"You are not allowed to do this. If your role was changed, reload the page."**; 401 and 419 → **"Your session has ended. Sign in again to carry on."** (the shared request handler's own words, so the two make one toast, and the page reloads, §20.7); 405 → **"That cannot be done here. Reload the page and try again."**; a 5xx while `app.debug` is off → **"Something went wrong on our side. Nothing was changed. Please try again in a moment."** — and keeps every message of the app's own (tested, `JsonErrorWordsTest`: a file of another organization and one that never was, and a route that does not exist, all read the 404 words with no model or route named; a refusal by permission, an ended session and a server error in words, the secret detail of the exception never in the answer; "That screen was removed." and "The platform team works above the organizations." kept). The deliberate 404s of §6 therefore all read those same words.
- **A stale tab:** 409 `{message, stale_tab: true}` (§20.4a). **A paused organization:** 403 `{message, paused: true}` (§2.2). **Malformed text:** 400 (§3.3).
- **Password limiter:** for JSON, **429** with the field-error shape `{message, errors: {password: ["Too many wrong passwords. Try again in {n} seconds."]}}` (§19.7) — the dialogs show it as a toast. (The route throttles' 429 is the framework's plain "Too Many Attempts." response.)
- **HTML forms** (auth pages, Profile, Settings → Organizations, the invitation page): a validation failure is a **302 redirect back** with the errors in a named **error bag** (`default`, `updatePassword`, `userDeletion`, `organizationDetails`, `newOrganization`, `organizationDeletion`; leaving an organization refused puts its error in `organizationMembership`, §8.6) and the old input flashed (never the password fields); success is a 302 with a flashed `status`; a guard 403 renders the app's own error page (`resources/views/errors/`, drawn with styles of its own and asking nothing of the session or the database), except the chooser redirect of §18. The dashboard's invitation answers flash `invitation-problem` (§9.5a); the verification pages and the profile flash `error` (§10.1a, §14.2). **The password limiter's refusal takes this shape too** — a 302 back with its message on the password field in the form's bag; its 429 status reaches JSON clients only.

### 19.2 Field rules by door
| field | door | rules |
|---|---|---|
| first_name, last_name | signup, invitation register, profile | required, string, max 255 |
| phone | signup, invitation register, profile | required, numeric, exactly 10 digits |
| email | signup | **lower-cased before validation**; then `bail`, required, `email` validator, max 255, regex `^\S+$` (**"Email cannot contain spaces."**), unique in users |
| email | profile | **lower-cased before validation** (lowered, not refused); then `bail`, required, string, lowercase, email, max 255, unique ignoring own row — and, when it is a new address, the mail budget first (§14.2) |
| email / owner_email | organization invitation, platform invitation, owner invitation, organization creation on the platform | required, string, email, max 255; then normalized `lower(trim)` |
| password | signup, invitation register, password reset, password change (the new password) | required, confirmed, min 8 |
| password (re-confirmation, §19.7) | JSON: remove a member; take a person out of an organization from the platform (Users → Organizations); delete an organization (the platform's Organizations page), an account (the platform), a platform role, a role, a permission (and a channel, a campaign, an Ad Builder design). Forms: delete organization on Settings → Organizations (bag `organizationDeletion`), Profile self-delete (bag `userDeletion`) | required (**"Password is required."**), string, current password (**"The password is incorrect."**); wrong ones limited — one counter per person for all of them |
| current_password (re-confirmation, §19.7) | password change (bag `updatePassword`) | the same rules, messages and counter, on this field |
| organization_name | signup | required, string, max 255 |
| name | the platform's Organizations page create/update, Settings → Organizations details | required, string, max 255 |
| street / suite / city | every organization form | street required max 255; suite nullable max 100; city required max 100 |
| organization_name, organization_street, organization_suite, organization_city, organization_state, organization_zip_code, organization_country | Create organization on Settings → Organizations (bag `newOrganization`, §10.2) | the rules of `name`, `street`, `suite`, `city`, `state`, `zip_code` and `country` in this table, under these names; the framework's messages name them "organization name", "street", "suite", "city", "state", "zip code", "country" |
| state | every organization form | required, exactly 2 chars, one of the **50 US states — DC and territories rejected** (tested) |
| zip_code | every organization form | required, string, max 10, digits only — **"Zip code can only contain numbers."** |
| country | the platform's Organizations page create/update, Settings → Organizations details | required, string, max 100 (signup hardcodes `'USA'`) |
| is_active | the platform's Organizations page create/update only | optional boolean — never read by Settings → Organizations (§10.2, §11.2) |
| confirm_name | delete organization (Settings → Organizations, the platform's Organizations page) | required (**"Type the organization name to confirm."**), string, exactly the organization name (**"Type the organization name exactly as it is shown."**) |
| role_id | change role, organization invitation, platform invitation, the platform's Users → Organizations add and change (required message **"Choose a role."**) | required, integer — then resolved per §6.5 |
| organization_id | switch | required, integer |
| organization_id | the platform's Users → Organizations add | required (**"Choose an organization."**), integer — then an existing organization (**"Choose an organization that exists."**, §13.4) |
| type | roles, create on the platform | required, one of `organization`, `platform` (**"Choose what this role is for."**, §7.5) — not read inside an organization or on update |
| role name / permissions | roles | §7.5 |
| permission name / label | permissions | §16 |
| from / to | activity data | nullable, date |
| website, form_started | signup (the robot traps, §10.1) | `website` must be empty; `form_started` a sealed moment between `signup_min_seconds` and two hours old — otherwise **"Please try again."** on `form`, before anything else |
| group, sort, direction | the users listing (§13.1) | read through `plainValue` against fixed lists; anything else is the default, never an error |
| premium_templates_unlocked, platform_channels_unlocked | the Billing dialog's save (§12.1a) | required, boolean — **"Say whether Premium Templates are unlocked."** / **"Say whether Platform Channels are unlocked."** |

The request pipeline trims every input string and turns empty strings into null before validation.

### 19.3 Login and logout
- `GET/POST /login` are guest-only (an authenticated user is redirected to the dashboard — likewise register, forgot-password and reset-password).
- `email` required, string, email; `password` required, string; optional boolean `remember` issues a persistent cookie backed by `users.remember_token`.
- Throttle: **5 failed attempts** inside a 60-second window that starts at the first counted failure (the same fixed-window counter as §24's `RateLimiter`), keyed `transliterate(lower(email) + '|' + client IP)`, checked BEFORE the credential attempt and **cleared on success**. Failure → error on `email` ("These credentials do not match our records."); locked → error on `email` from the framework template ("Too many login attempts. Please try again in :seconds seconds.") — framework texts, non-contractual.
- Success: regenerate the session id, log `auth.login`, redirect to the intended URL (e.g. an invitation link, §9.5) or the dashboard — which sends an account that has not confirmed its email on to "Check your inbox" (§10.1a). Every successful sign-in also clears any impersonation keys left in the session (§14.1).
- `POST /logout`: log `auth.logout` if authenticated; log out (rotates the remember token when one is set); invalidate the session; regenerate the CSRF token; redirect to `/`.

### 19.4 Password reset
- Link request (`POST /forgot-password`, `email` required + email; `throttle:password-reset`, §19.6): the broker still sends no new link within **60 s** of the last one for that user, but every well-formed address — an account's or not, held back by the broker or not — gets the **same neutral status**, the app's own words: **"If an account uses that address, a link to choose a new password is on its way. It can take a minute — look in spam too."**, with the address kept in its field so a typo can be seen — so the form never says whether an address has an account (owner's decision, 2026-09-17; the trade accepted: a mistyped address gets that line and no email). A malformed address is still a plain validation error on `email`.
- Token: random, stored **hashed (bcrypt)** in `password_reset_tokens` (one row per email; a new request overwrites), plaintext only in the emailed link — the app's own email shell (`ResetPasswordNotification`, subject "Reset your {app name} password"); tokens expire after **60 minutes**.
- `POST /reset-password` (`throttle:password-reset` — the same counter as the link request): `token` required, string (*`token[]=x` otherwise reached the hasher, a 500*); `email` required + email; `password` required, confirmed, min 8. Success: new password hash, `remember_token` rotated to a random 60-char value (kills every remember-me cookie), log `password.reset` with explicit actor; then **every session of the account ends** (`User::endEverySession`, the database session driver — the person resetting is not signed in, the link never signs anybody in, so nothing of theirs ends) and **an account never confirmed is confirmed now** (`account.verified`, "Confirmed their email {email} by a password-reset link", explicit actor) — *the link came to the account's own inbox; together the two give a person their address back from somebody who signed up with it and kept a session open (owner's rule, 2026-09-29; tested, `VerificationAttackTest`)*; redirect to **login** with the status — **no auto-login**. A failure — a link expired or used, or an address with no account — redirects back with the address and one sentence on `email`: **"This link has expired or was already used. Ask for a new one below."** (*the framework's "We can't find a user with that email address" told anybody which addresses have one*).

### 19.5 Password change — `PUT /password` (auth only), exact order
1. Validate in bag `updatePassword`: `password` (the new one) required, min 8, confirmed — framework messages.
2. **Password re-confirmation** (§19.7) in bag `updatePassword`, field **`current_password`**: **"Password is required."** / **"The password is incorrect."** / the limiter's message — so a wrong current password counts toward the same per-person limit as the big deletes (tested). *Reason: an open session must not be a way to guess the password here either.* An invalid new password is reported without the current one being checked or counted.
3. Update the hash, log `password.changed`, redirect back with status `password-updated` (the toast "Your password is changed.", §20.7). It does not rotate the remember token or end other sessions.

### 19.6 Rate limits (every limiter is NAMED and keyed explicitly)
Each route limiter's key is prefixed with its own name, so two limiters can never collide (`AppServiceProvider::registerRateLimiters`):

| limiter | limit | key | applied to |
|---|---|---|---|
| `admin` | 240 / minute | the user id (IP when none) | every route in the admin stack (§3.7), `POST /verify-email/confirm-for-them` and `POST /impersonate/stop` |
| `invitations` | 60 / hour | the user id (IP when none) | creating and resending organization and platform invitations; `POST /organizations` and `POST /organizations/{organization}/owner-invitation` (§9.7) |
| `invitation-response` | 20 / minute | the IP | `GET /invitations/{token}` and its accept / register / decline POSTs, one shared bucket |
| `signup` | 10 / minute | the IP | `POST /register` |
| `password-reset` | 10 / minute | the IP | `POST /forgot-password` and `POST /reset-password`, one shared bucket (§19.4) |
| `verification-link` | 20 / minute | the user id (IP when none) | opening a confirmation link — `GET /verify-email/{id}/{hash}`, `GET /confirm-email/{id}/{hash}` (*a guess at a signature is one request*, §10.1a) |
| `uploads` | 600 / minute | the user id (IP when none) | the chunked uploads `/uploads` — a 5 MB chunk is a request, so they count apart from `admin` (out of scope) |
| `player-app` | 60 / minute | the IP | `GET /player-app/latest.json` (§16a) |
| `font-install` | 10 / minute | the user id (IP when none) | `POST /builder/fonts` — the Ad Builder's font installer, which fetches from Google (out of scope) |
| `device-register` | 30 / minute | the IP (a screen asking for its first code has nothing else to be known by) | the device API's register (out of scope) |
| `device-pair` | 60 / minute and 600 / minute | the `device_uuid` (the IP for anything that is not one plain value); the IP | the device API's pair-status (out of scope) |
| `device-api` | 60 / minute and 1200 / minute | the SHA-256 of the bearer token; the IP | the device API's content endpoints, running BEFORE the token check (out of scope) |
| login (not a named limiter) | 5 failed attempts / 60 s | lower(email) + IP | `POST /login` (§19.3) |
| `confirm-password:{user id}` (not a route limiter) | 5 wrong passwords / 60 s | the user id | every password re-confirmation, shared: the JSON big deletes, the Organizations tab's organization delete, Profile self-delete, the password change (§19.7) |
| the mail budget (not a route limiter) | 3 / minute and 20 / hour per account; 30 / hour per IP | `confirm-links:account:{minute\|hour}:{user id}`, `confirm-links:visitor:hour:{ip}` | every email that confirms an address, counted in `User::sendALink` (§10.1a) |

Those twelve named route limiters (`admin`, `invitations`, `invitation-response`, `signup`, `password-reset`, `verification-link`, `uploads`, `player-app`, `font-install` and the three device ones) are **all** there are. There is no `email-verification` limiter: the confirmation links have `verification-link`, and the emails themselves are counted where they are sent, so no door can skip the budget (`POST /verify-email/resend` and `POST /profile/email/resend` carry no route throttle of their own).

*Reason for named limiters (a real incident): the bare `throttle:n,1` form keys a guest request on domain + IP only — the route is not part of the key — so every such route shared one counter per visitor; an organization's TVs behind the owner's router exhausted the signup form's budget (tested: forty device requests from one IP leave `POST /register` reachable).* Dashboard and organization selection (`auth`, `verified`), profile (including leaving an organization from it and its email links), "Check your inbox" and sending its link again, password change and logout carry no route throttle; the wrong passwords of Profile self-delete and the password change still count in the shared `confirm-password` limiter.

### 19.7 Re-confirming the password — big deletes, changing your password (owner's rule, 2026-09-16)
*Reason: a session left open on a shared computer must not be able to destroy anything with two clicks — an organization, an account, a member's place in an organization, a role — and must not become a way to guess the password either.*

`confirmPassword(request, bag = 'default', field = 'password')` — ONE check for every endpoint below, each calling it **after its other up-front refusals** (so nobody types a password for something that would be refused anyway) and before changing anything:
```
key = 'confirm-password:' + actor.id            # ONE counter per person: every endpoint below, every session and device
if counter(key) >= 5 and window(key) is open:
    refuse: error on `field`, bag `bag`: "Too many wrong passwords. Try again in {seconds left} seconds."
            # status 429; not counted; even the right password waits
validate `field` in bag `bag`: required -> "Password is required."
                              string
                              verifies against the actor's hash -> "The password is incorrect."
on a validation failure: if `field` is non-empty: counter(key) += 1   # window = 60 s from the FIRST counted failure (fixed, not sliding)
on success: delete the counter and its window
```
- **JSON request:** a failure is 422 `{message, errors: {field: [...]}}`, the limiter's 429 with the same shape (bags mean nothing to JSON).
- **Plain HTML form:** every failure — the limiter's included — is a **302 redirect back** with the message on `field` in the named bag (the framework ignores the 429 status for a redirect); password fields are never flashed back.
- An empty password is never counted. A password accepted here clears the counter even when a later check of the same request still refuses (e.g. a locked re-check).

**Where, and after what:**
| endpoint | bag · field | asked after |
|---|---|---|
| `DELETE /members/{user}` | JSON · `password` | 404 not a member · 422 yourself · 403 out of reach · 422 the organization's only Owner (§8.5) — before the locked re-check |
| `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}` | JSON · `password` | 404 not a member of that organization · 422 its only Owner (§13.4) — before the locked re-check |
| `DELETE /organizations/{organization}` | JSON · `password` | `confirm_name` validation (§12.4) |
| `DELETE /users/{user}` | JSON · `password` | 404 reach · 422 yourself · 403 the primary · 403 a super admin unless primary (§13.2) |
| `DELETE /users/{user}/platform-role` | JSON · `password` | 404 no platform row · 422 yourself · 403 the primary · 403 a super admin unless primary (§13.3) |
| `DELETE /roles/{role}` | JSON · `password` | 403 `context()` · 403/404 `ensureManageable` · 422 still held (§7.8) |
| `DELETE /permissions/{permission}` | JSON · `password` | 422 still assigned to a role (§16) |
| `DELETE /channels/{channel}` (signage) | JSON · `password` | 404 out of reach (`Channel::visibleTo`, §6.6) |
| `DELETE /campaigns/{campaign}` (signage, super admins) | JSON · `password` | nothing — first thing in the controller |
| `DELETE /builder/{ad}` (the Ad Builder; the design and its published copy) | JSON · `password` | 404 out of reach (`BuilderAd::visibleTo`) |
| `DELETE /settings/organization` | `organizationDeletion` · `password` | 403 no `organization-destroy` (the route's gate) · 404 no current organization · `confirm_name` validation (§11.4) |
| `DELETE /profile` | `userDeletion` · `password` | 403 super admin · the sole-Owner refusal (§14.2) — before the locked re-check |
| `PUT /password` | `updatePassword` · `current_password` | the new password's validation (§19.5) |

**Not asked** — everyday deletes stay one confirmation away: a media file, a screen, a channel ad, an Ad Builder asset, an invitation revoke (tested for an organization invitation), declining an invitation on the dashboard (§9.5a) and a version of the TV app (§16a — the file can be uploaded again). So do an Ad Builder ad's **Unpublish** and **Discard changes** (`POST /builder/{ad}/unpublish`, `/discard`, `ad-update`, 404 out of reach): neither deletes anything — an unpublished ad keeps every playlist line and channel ad, and Publish brings it back (docs/AD-BUILDER-SPEC.md §9).

**The table above is now the whole of it.** The scaffolded `POST /confirm-password` (the framework's "confirm your password" screen, §26.8) was the one place a password was checked *outside* this counter — unthrottled, and logged nowhere (§17.3) — and it is deleted with its route, its controller and its view. Every password check in the application now runs through `confirmPassword`, so the per-person limit has no hole left to walk around: a port must not add a second password check of its own.

Tested: each of the eight JSON big deletes (an organization, an account, a platform role, a member, a role, a channel, a campaign, a permission) answers 422 "Password is required." and then "The password is incorrect." and deletes nothing (an Ad Builder design likewise answers 422 without a password and with a wrong one, and stays — in the Ad Builder's own tests); taking a person out of an organization from the platform answers 422 "Password is required." without one (§13.4); a role still held answers its own 422 without a password; five wrong passwords on an organization delete make the right one answer 429 with a message starting "Too many wrong passwords. Try again in", and the same request succeeds 61 s later; revoking an invitation needs no password; **the limit is shared across forms** — five wrong current passwords on `PUT /password` (each "The password is incorrect." on `current_password` in bag `updatePassword`) make the right password on `DELETE /settings/organization` redirect back with an `organizationDeletion` error on `password` starting "Too many wrong passwords. Try again in", the organization intact; the plain forms answer a wrong password in their bags (`organizationDeletion`, `userDeletion`, `updatePassword`). Browser-tested on the platform: the organization dialog shows "Password is required." then "The password is incorrect." and deletes with the right password, then an account and a platform role are deleted through their dialogs.

---

## 20. Frontend contract (part of the system, not cosmetics)

### 20.1 The UI hides; the backend enforces
Every action element is wrapped in the permission check matching its route (and, on a tier-locked route, the tier); what may be done to ONE row comes from the server with the row; gates ship in the same change as the button.

| page | element | shown when |
|---|---|---|
| Members | **Invite Member** on the members list (`invite-member`) and on Pending Invitations (`invite-member-invitations`) | `member-invite` |
| Members | Change Role / Remove on a row | `member-update` / `member-remove` AND `row.can_manage` (otherwise "—"; never on the organization's only Owner, §8.3) |
| Members | Resend / Revoke on an invitation | `member-invite` AND `invitation.can_manage` |
| Members | Leave Organization | always (the route has no gate); for the sole Owner (own row is Owner and `owner_count == 1`) the button toasts "You are the only Owner of {organization}. Make someone else an Owner before you leave." instead of opening the dialog |
| Settings | the Organizations tab | `User::hasOrganizationsTab()`: an account with no platform role, a current organization AND `organization-view` there (§20.3) |
| Settings | the Billing tab (`settings-tab-billing`) | `User::hasBillingTab()`: an account with no platform role, a current organization AND `billing-view` there (§20.3) |
| Settings → Organizations | the Organization Details form / the details read-only | `organization-update` / otherwise (§11.1) |
| Settings → Organizations | the Delete Organization card | `organization-destroy` (there is no handover card, §11.3) |
| Settings → Organizations | "Your organizations" (`your-organizations`) / its Create Organization button (`open-organization-button`) | always on the tab / `organization-store` (§10.2) |
| Organizations (platform) | Add Organization | `organization-store` |
| Organizations (platform) | Invite Owner / Billing / Edit / Delete on a row | `row.can.invite_owner` / `row.can.billing` / `row.can.update` / `row.can.destroy` (§12.1) — Invite owner only on an organization with no Owner (browser-tested: offered on a new organization with no members, gone once its Owner registers from the invitation or is put in from Users → Organizations) |
| Organizations (platform) | advertising column, row checkboxes, bulk switch | `campaign-manage` |
| Organizations (platform) | the Billing dialog's switches (`billing-switch-{feature}`) | `billing-update`, besides `row.can.billing` (§12.1a) |
| Users | Invite to Platform Team; platform invitations card; the tabs All · Platform Team · Organization Members with their counts | super admin (`super-admin-tier`); the invite picker lists Super-Admin to the primary super admin only |
| Users | Log In As / Organizations / Remove Platform Role / Delete on a row | `row.can.impersonate` / `row.can.manage_organizations` / `row.can.remove_platform_role` / `row.can.delete` (the page itself is `global-tier` + `user-view`, §13.1) |
| Roles | Create Custom Role (inside an organization) / Create Role (platform) | `role-store` |
| Roles | the "What is this role for?" choice in the form | a new role on the platform (§20.8) |
| Roles | the "Custom roles made in organizations" card | the platform, once any organization has a custom role (§20.8) |
| Roles | Edit / Delete on a row; View | `row.can_edit` / `row.can_delete` — the server's flags already include `role-update` / `role-destroy` (§7.3), so the row buttons carry no page-level check of their own — a held role's Delete only toasts why it cannot go yet; View always (§20.8) |
| Permissions | Add / Edit / Delete | `permission-store` / `permission-update` / `permission-destroy` |
| Activity Log | storage panel + maintenance | no current organization on the page AND `global-tier` AND `activity-destroy` (§17.5) |
| Channels | Add Channel | `channel-store` (inside an organization the channel is the organization's own, §6.6) |
| Sidebar | Ad Builder and its Assets sub-link (the section has no tabs of its own) | `ad-view` (§20.2) |
| Ad Builder → Ads | Create Ad (`new-ad`) / Edit and the card's links into the editor / Copy / Delete | `ad-store` / `ad-update` / `ad-store` / `ad-destroy` |
| Ad Builder → Assets | Upload (`upload-asset`) / Delete on an asset | `ad-store` / `ad-destroy` |
| Ad Builder → editor | Publish (`ad-publish`) / Preview ↗ (`ad-preview`) / "← Ads" (`builder-exit`; "← Back" to the dashboard otherwise) and "Manage assets" / a font's Install | `ad-update` / `ad-view` or `ad-update` / `ad-view` / `ad-store` or `ad-update` (the `canInstallFonts` flag; otherwise a font not yet installed reads "Not installed") |
| Profile | "Your organizations" section / its Leave button per organization (here and on the Organizations tab) | no platform role AND no Organizations tab / the person is not that organization's only Owner (§14.2) |
| Profile | Delete Account section | not a super admin |
| Profile | the email's pending box (Send the Link Again, Keep My Current Email) / the "not confirmed yet" box | `pending_email` set / `email_verified_at` NULL (§14.2) |
| Check your inbox | Confirm Email and Continue (`verify-email-confirm-for-them`) | `ImpersonateController::superAdminViewingAs(account)` — a super admin viewing as this account, checked again on the POST (§10.1a) |
| Dashboard | "Invitations for you" with Accept / Decline per invitation | an account with no platform role and an open invitation of an organization to its own address (§9.5a) |
| Dashboard | Choose Another Organization (paused view) | the person belongs to more than one organization (§18) |
| Sidebar | Developer | `super-admin-tier` AND `player-app-view` (§20.2) |
| Register | closed banner + disabled submit | signup closed (§10.1) |

### 20.2 Sidebar
In order, each link gated as stated (a permission check reads the role where the person stands, §5.3 — inside an organization, their role there; in a paused organization nothing is offered, §2.2):
1. **Dashboard** — always.
2. Platform accounts only (`globalRole() != null`): the **Users** group, shown with `user-view` (a super admin always holds it, §3.3, so the group needs no super-admin case of its own) — its header links to Users — with sub-links **Roles** (super admin) and **Permissions** (super admin AND `permission-view`).
3. Platform accounts only: **Organizations** (`organization-view`) — every organization (§12) — after Users (owner, 2026-09-30; tested, `DashboardTest`). An organization member sees their organizations under Settings → Organizations or on the profile (§11.1, §14.2), never here.
4. Both tiers: **Screens** (`screen-view`), **Media Library** (`media-view`), **Ad Builder** (`ad-view`), its row opening the ads with the sub-link **Assets** (both pages ask View Ads, as their routes do — owner, 2026-09-30: no tabs inside the pages).
5. Organization accounts only: the **Team** group, shown when `member-view` OR `role-view` — its header links to Members when `member-view`, else to Roles — with sub-links **Members** (`member-view`) and **Roles** (`role-view`). There is no Accounts link: the accounts are the platform's (§13).
6. Both tiers: **Advertising** (`campaign-manage`); **Channels** (`channel-view`); **Activity Log** (`activity-view`).
7. Last: **Developer** (`super-admin-tier` AND `player-app-view`) — Developer → TV App (owner, 2026-10-10: "developer ka tab ho sub se end mein", §16a); a platform role given `player-app-view` by hand is not offered it (tested, `PlayerAppReleaseTest`).

At the foot of the bar, for everyone: the person's initial and name with "Settings" beneath (`sidebar-settings`, title "Settings"), linking to Settings — `GET /profile` — and highlighted on the Profile tab and the Organizations tab (not on the Billing tab). **The sidebar has no link to the Organizations tab** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17): the organization being worked in is a tab of Settings (§11, §20.3). Tested: an Owner's dashboard carries `sidebar-settings` and no link to `/settings/organization`.

A nav link takes the same locks as its routes: Channels and the Activity Log carry no tier lock, so an organization's role carrying their permission is offered the link — for its own organization — while Organizations and the Users group keep the platform's tier (tested: an Owner is offered none of Organizations, Users, Channels and Activity Log; once the Owner role holds `organization-view`, `user-view`, `channel-view` and `activity-view` — `user-view` written straight into the database — they are offered Channels and Activity Log, never Organizations or Users, and `GET /organizations` answers them 403; an organization role holding `activity-view` is offered the Activity Log) — and a platform user holding `permission-view` is not offered Permissions (tested). Every permission check being true for a super admin (§3.3), a super admin is offered every link of the platform side: Dashboard, the Users group with Roles and Permissions, Organizations, Screens, Media Library, Ad Builder with Assets, Advertising, Channels, Activity Log and Developer.

### 20.3 Header, Settings and banners
- **Organization switcher** — organization accounts with at least one organization: shows the current organization's name (or "Select organization"); the menu "Switch organization" lists their organizations by name (current one marked), each posting to `POST /organizations/switch`. Platform accounts never get it.
- **User menu** (`user-menu`) — **Settings** (`user-menu-settings`, → `/profile`) and signing out; the organization-selection page's focused layout carries the same menu.
- **Settings** (owner's rule, 2026-09-17) — tabs (`settings-tabs`), each a page of its own so every plain form on it returns to where it was sent from, all headed "Settings": **Profile** (`settings-tab-profile`, `/profile`, always, §14.2), **Organizations** (`settings-tab-organization`, `/settings/organization`, §11), shown only when `User::hasOrganizationsTab()` — an account with no platform role, a current organization, and `organization-view` there (owner's rule, 2026-09-17: turning View Organizations off hides it) — and **Billing** (`settings-tab-billing`, `/settings/billing`; owner, 2026-10-08 — "organization mein billing profile k ander ho jese sub ki honti ha professional kaam chiya"), shown only when `User::hasBillingTab()` — the same, with `billing-view` there. The Billing tab is a page to read (`billing.show`, `BillingSummary`, docs/BILLING-SPEC.md): "Billing has not started yet: nothing is charged, and every feature is open for now.", the month's estimate and its sum, the features with their price and Unlocked/Locked, every screen with when it was paired and its cost, and whom to ask; the switches are the platform's (§12.1a). It answers a platform account 404 — *the platform reads each organization's billing on the Organizations page* (tested, `BillingTest`: a member without View Billing has no tab and gets 403, a custom role given it opens the page, a super admin gets 404). A settings page added later joins these tabs, not the sidebar. Tested: an Owner's Profile tab links to the Organizations tab, a Staff member's does not, and a platform user holding `organization-view` gets none; browser-tested: a new Owner reaches the tab — labelled "Organizations" — through `sidebar-settings`, and finds "Organization Details", "Delete Organization", no "Transfer Ownership", and their organization under "Your organizations"; a member moved from Staff to an organization role holding `organization-view` reaches it the same way; a platform team member sees the Profile tab alone.
- **Impersonation banner** — §14.1.

### 20.4 Double-submit protection
Every AJAX action has an in-flight flag (`saving`, `deleting`, `inviting`, `changingRole`, `removing`, `revoking`, `leaving`, `busyInvitationId`, `removingRole`, `impersonating`, `loadingAccess`, `assigning`, `removingBusy`, a Users → Organizations line's own `busy`, `invitingOwner`, `savingBilling`, `maintaining`, `openingForm`; the role form also refuses to save while `loadingAssignable`) that disables its button until the request settles (browser-tested: a double click creates exactly one record). Plain forms: one global guard lets the FIRST natural submit through, marks the form, blocks further submits, disables submit buttons only on the NEXT tick (synchronous disabling would drop the clicked button's name/value from the POST), ignores submits already prevented by an AJAX handler, and re-arms on back/forward-cache restore (`pageshow`).

### 20.4a A tab left open while the session changed in another is refused, never obeyed (QA round, 2026-10-09)
*A rename sent from a tab still showing Smart Stop ran in Alpha Mart after another tab switched organization, and the super admin's own profile form, sent after "Log in as" in another tab, was taken as the person being viewed.*
- **Whose the page is.** Every signed-in layout (the app layout, the focused layout, the Ad Builder's) carries `<meta name="session-context" content="{user id}:{organization id}">` (`RefuseAStaleTab::contextFor`: the organization 0 above the organizations or with none chosen); a guest page carries none (tested).
- **It goes back with every request:** axios sends it as the header `X-Session-Context` (`core/bootstrap.js`), the chunked uploader too, and every plain `POST` form gets a hidden `_context` field (`core/form-guard.js`).
- **`RefuseAStaleTab`** (at the end of the web group, after `ChooseTheOnlyOrganization`, §3.3) compares what was sent with the session as it is now (constant-time). It lets through a request that sends nothing (a request made by hand, a page from before), a guest, and **what changes the session on purpose** — `organization.switch`, `users.impersonate`, `impersonate.stop`, `logout`, each sent from a page of the session it leaves. Anything else that differs is refused before it does anything: a page's own request → **409** `{message, stale_tab: true}`; a plain form → back to its page with flash `stale_tab` = the message (an error toast, §20.7). The words: another person in this browser → **"This page is out of date: this browser is now signed in as {name}. Nothing was changed. The page is reloaded."**; another organization → **"This page is out of date: you switched to {organization} in another tab. Nothing was changed. The page is reloaded."** (or "…you left the organization in another tab…" when none is chosen now). The page says it once and reloads itself.
- A new way of sending a request from a page sends the context too.
- Tested (`StaleTabTest`, browser `StaleTabFlowTest`): a rename from a page of another organization → 409 with exactly those words, nothing renamed and nothing logged; from a page of another person → 409; the super admin's profile form sent after "Log in as" made the session another person → back to `/profile` with the `stale_tab` words, the person's details untouched; the page as it is, and a request that says nothing, go through; switching organization and signing out from a page left over go through.

### 20.5 Client-side validation mirrors the backend
The same rules run before sending; a client failure sends NO request; the backend stays the source of truth. Two visual tracks: **AJAX modals** (errors in a shared `formErrors` object, the field wrapper paints a red border, the message sits below) and **plain forms** (a shared helper reads the named fields, adds a red border and `data-client-invalid="1"` to each invalid input, inserts the message below and scrolls to the first). Required labels carry a red `*`.

Exact client messages: `required` → "{Label} is required." · `maxLen` → "{Label} may not be longer than {n} characters." · `minLen` → "{Label} must be at least {n} characters." · `emailFormat` (`^\S+@\S+\.\S+$`) → "{Label} must be a valid email address." · `digitsExactly` → "{Label} must be exactly {n} digits." · `digitsOnly` → "{Label} can only contain numbers." · `lettersNumbersSpaces` → "{Label} can only contain letters, numbers, and spaces." · password mismatch → "Password confirmation does not match." · empty permission checklist → "Choose at least one permission." · an organization delete's typed-name mismatch (the Organizations page and the Organizations tab) → "Type the organization name exactly as it is shown." · any big-delete dialog with an empty password → "Password is required." (§20.8)

Forms covered: invite member / platform staff (Email required + format + max 255, Role required); change role (Role); the platform's Users → Organizations add (Organization required, Role required) and its remove form (password required); organizations on the platform (Name, Street, Suite, City, State, Zip code, Country; Owner email on create only; the owner-invite dialog's Email required + format); roles (Name required + max 255, the checklist); permissions (Name, Label); the big-delete dialogs (password required; the Organizations page's organization dialog checks the typed name and the password together); registration (every field; password min 8 + confirmation); profile (first/last name, phone 10 digits, email); password change (current, new min 8 + match, confirmation); account deletion (password); Settings → Organizations (details; Create organization: the same details — Organization name, Street, Suite / Unit, City, State, Zip code, Country — under their `organization_` names; delete: typed name exact + password); the invitation link's register form (First name and Last name required + max 255, Phone required + 10 digits, Password required + min 8 + confirmation match — the email comes from the invitation). Input hardening: organization zip codes accept digits only, max 10; permission labels accept letters, digits and spaces only.

### 20.6 Listings
- **Shared paginated listings** (organizations, users, permissions, activity; the signage tables alike): params `search`, `page`, `per_page`; **one page size everywhere, 50** — the client always sends 50 and the server defaults to 50 when none is sent, clamped to 1–100 (tested: `per_page=999999` → 100, `per_page=0` → 1). Response `{<itemsKey>, total, currentPage, lastPage, perPage}` with itemsKey `organizations` / `users` / `permissions` / `logs`. Search = `LIKE %term%` across the listed columns, OR-grouped, wildcards unescaped; case-insensitivity comes from the database collation (use ILIKE/LOWER elsewhere). Order: organizations by name; users by the heading pressed (`sort` / `direction`, §13.1 — the page opens on Joined, newest first; with neither, first then last name, case folded); activity id DESC; permissions none (pick a stable order in a port). A heading that sorts is a button inside the `<th>` (`aria-sort`, an arrow), and pressing it fetches page one again. Client: 400 ms search debounce resetting to page 1; a request token discards stale out-of-order responses; after a save, delete or copy the list is brought up to date where it stands (`refreshInPlace`: its rows stay until the new ones are in, a deleted one goes at once — owner, 2026-10-07), and when a delete empties the page it steps back one page.
- **Members** (`/members/data`) and **Roles** (`/roles/data`) are single unpaginated fetches; the members search filters client-side by name or email (case-insensitive substring). Platform invitations are unpaginated.
- Dates: `formatDate` renders `toLocaleDateString` (year, short month, day); invitation status → "Expired", "Expires today" (≤ 1 day left) or "Expires in {n} days". Role badges on the Members and Users pages go by the role's key, so a renamed starter keeps its colour: `owner` amber (warning), `admin` blue (info), every other role neutral. The Roles page's Type badges are in §20.8.

### 20.7 Toasts and flash
One global top-center toast container — on the app layout, the focused layout and the Ad Builder's — a polite live region; success green / error red (`window.toast(message, type = 'error')`); a success goes after 5 s and an error after 10 s, neither while the pointer or the keyboard is on it, with a manual ✕; the same words already showing are one toast, their time started again (*a refusal the page and the shared request handler both say is one toast*). The shared request handler (`core/bootstrap.js`) answers three shapes on its own, once each: 401/419 → the toast "Your session has ended. Sign in again to carry on." and a reload; a 409 with `stale_tab` → its message and a reload (§20.4a); a 403 with `paused` → its message and the dashboard (§2.2). Message-only 403/404/422 responses MUST reach a toast (a real bug once hid the Super-Admin rename error). A response carrying `email_sent: false` (organization and platform invitations and their resends, organization creation, "Invite owner") shows its message as an **error** toast; otherwise the success toast. A success carrying `redirect` (leaving the current organization from Members, §8.6) sends the browser there instead of toasting. A flashed `status` string becomes a success toast on the next signed-in page ("Switched to …", "Welcome to …!", "You are already a member of …", "{name} was deleted.", "You left {organization}.", "Invitation declined.", "Your email is confirmed. Welcome!", "{email} is confirmed.", "{name} is open, and you are its Owner. Switch to it from the organization menu." after Create organization, §10.2) — a key is never shown raw: `profile-updated`, `password-updated` and `organization-updated` become **"Your profile is saved."**, **"Your password is changed."** and **"Organization details saved."**, and the email's own keys (`email-pending`, `email-link-resent`, `email-changed`, `email-change-cancelled`, `verification-link-sent`) are no toast at all — the profile and "Check your inbox" say them beside the email (§14.2, §10.1a). A flashed `stale_tab` is an error toast (§20.4a); the dashboard shows `invitation-problem` in a red alert (§9.5a). The guest layout has no toast container: the login page shows `status` inline ("Invitation declined."), the invitation page and "Check your inbox" show `error` inline.

### 20.8 The Roles page, Users → Organizations and the big-delete dialogs
**Roles page** (`GET /roles`) — the page "Roles", its card headed **"All roles"** on the platform, **"Roles in {organization}"** inside an organization, each with one line: on the platform "Organization roles are offered in every organization. Platform roles are for your team."; inside an organization "Organization roles come from the platform. Custom roles are {organization}'s own." Create stands on the card's header line. There is no organization picker, and no permission checkbox is ever shown greyed out. One `/roles/data` fetch (§7.3) feeds:
- **Roles** table (`roles-table`) — inside an organization every row; on the platform every row but the custom roles. A row per role (`role-row-{id}`):
  - **Role** — the name (`role-name-{id}`), an **Owner** badge (`owner-role-badge`) when `is_owner_role`, and the description beneath;
  - **Type** — a badge by `kind`: `super_admin` "Super admin" (red), `organization` "Organization role" (amber), `platform` "Platform role" (blue), `custom` "Custom role" (neutral);
  - **Permissions** — "Every permission, always" for Super-Admin; otherwise the number of permissions;
  - **Members** (inside an organization) / **Holders** (platform) — `holders_count`;
  - **Actions** — View (`view-role-{id}`) always; Edit (`edit-role-{id}`) when `can_edit`; Delete (`delete-role-{id}`) when `can_delete`.
- **Custom roles made in organizations** (`organization-custom-roles`) — the platform only, shown once loaded when any organization has a custom role: "Each belongs to its own organization and is offered there alone."; columns Role (name and description), Organization (`organization_name`), Permissions (the number), Members (`holders_count`) and Actions — the same three buttons and selectors.

**Create** — "Create Custom Role" (inside an organization) / "Create Role" (platform), `create-role`, needs `role-store`; disabled while the form opens.
- **Form** (`role-form`; headed "Edit {name}" or with the create label):
  - a hint by the role in the form — the Owner role: "This is the Owner role: whoever holds it owns their organization. You can rename it and change what it allows; it is never deleted."; an organization role: "An organization role is offered in every organization. What you save here applies in all of them at once."; a platform role: "A platform role works above the organizations: what it allows reaches every organization. The permission catalogue stays with Super-Admin."; a custom role on the platform: "A custom role of {organization}: it is offered there alone."; inside an organization: "A custom role of {organization}. You can only give it permissions you hold yourself.";
  - **Role name** * (`role-name`) — editable for every role the form opens for (Super-Admin never opens: it has no Edit);
  - **What is this role for?** * — for a new role on the platform only: radios **Organization role** (`role-type-organization`, "Offered in every organization. Its permissions reach the member's own organization.") and **Platform role** (`role-type-platform`, "For your team above the organizations. Its permissions reach every organization."), Organization role chosen when the form opens; the server's `type` error shows beneath. Choosing the other one clears the form's errors and loads the checklist again for that kind;
  - **Permissions** * — opening the form fetches `/roles/assignable` (§7.9) with `role` = the role's id when editing, `type` for a new role on the platform and nothing inside an organization ("Loading permissions..." meanwhile), and keeps ticked only those of the role's permissions the list offers — the same whenever the list is loaded again, so a permission the kind cannot hold never stays ticked out of sight. The list is grouped by the prefix of the permission's name (`permission-group-{key}`) in the order Organizations, Members, Roles, Screens, Media library, Ad Builder, Billing, Channels, Accounts, Activity log, Permissions (any other prefix after, titled by the capitalised prefix — the TV app's `player-app-*` read "Player", seen only in Super-Admin's View), each group headed by a select-all checkbox (indeterminate when only some are ticked), with a checkbox per permission (`permission-{name}`). Unless the role is a platform role, the Organizations, Channels and Activity log groups carry the hint **"This organization only"** (the Accounts group is listed for a platform role alone — an organization's role is never offered `user-*`, §7.9). An empty list reads "You hold no permissions you could give to a role.";
  - **Save Role** (`role-save`) — refused while the list loads; client validation first (§20.5); `POST /roles` with `{name, permissions}` plus `type` on the platform, or `PUT /roles/{id}` with `{name, permissions}`; a 422 with field errors paints them under the fields, anything else is an error toast; success closes the form, toasts the message and refetches.

  Browser-tested on the platform: the Owner row offers Edit but no Delete, Super-Admin no Edit, and Staff (held by somebody) a Delete — which toasts exactly "Please unassign Staff from everyone first: 1 person still holds it." without opening the password dialog; a new role opens with "Organization role" chosen, `activity-destroy` and `permission-view` not in the list and "This organization only" on the Channels and Activity log groups ("Role Shift Lead created.", "Role Area Manager created."); choosing "Platform role" lists `activity-destroy` but not `permission-view` ("Role Support created."); editing Staff shows its name editable and no type choice, and renaming it "Crew" with `screen-store` added toasts "Role Crew updated in every organization." and shows the new name in its row.
- **View** — the name, the kind (with " · {organization}" for a custom role) and the grouped permissions; for Super-Admin also "A super admin passes every permission check, whatever this list says — a permission added later included."
- **Delete** — a role somebody holds (`holders_count > 0`) opens nothing: the button toasts, as an error, **"Please unassign {name} from everyone first: 1 person still holds it."** / **"Please unassign {name} from everyone first: {n} people still hold it."** — the server's own words (§7.8), said before any password is typed. Otherwise the dialog `confirm-role-deletion`: "Delete {name}?", "Nobody holds this role, so no one loses access.", plus "Its pending invitation will be revoked too." / "Its {n} pending invitations will be revoked too." when `invitations_count > 0`; then the password (`delete-role-password`) and `confirm-role-deletion-confirm` ("Delete Role").

**Organizations** (Users page, super admins; "Organizations" = `manage-organizations-{id}` on a row with `can.manage_organizations`, disabled while it loads) — opening it fetches `GET /users/{user}/organizations` (§13.4) and opens the dialog `manage-organizations`, headed "{name}'s organizations" with "Add them to an organization, change their role or take them out. It happens at once.":
- **Member of** — "Not in any organization yet." when empty; otherwise a line per organization (`membership-{organization id}`): the organization's name; a role select (`membership-role-{organization id}`: the organization roles, then that organization's custom roles, starting at the role held) with the chosen role's description beneath; **Save** (`membership-save-{organization id}`, disabled until another role is chosen) sending `PUT /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}/role` with `{role_id}` — its refusal (the `role_id` error, else the message) shows on that line; and **Remove** (`membership-remove-{organization id}`), which opens a form beneath the list (`remove-membership-form`): "Take {name} out of {organization}? What they made there stays with the organization.", the password (`remove-membership-password`, its hint "Confirm it is you.") and **Remove from Organization** (`remove-membership-confirm`) sending `DELETE /users/{user}/organizations/{organization}` with `{password}` — a password error under the field, anything else (the only-Owner refusal included) an error toast.
- **Add to an organization** — "They are already in every organization." when no organization is left; otherwise a form (`assign-organization-form`): **Organization** (`assign-organization`, options "{name} — {city}"; changing it clears the role), **Role** (`assign-role`, disabled until an organization is chosen: the organization roles, then that organization's custom roles, the chosen one's description beneath) and **Add** (`assign-organization-save`) sending `POST /users/{user}/organizations` with `{organization_id, role_id}`; field errors under the fields, anything else an error toast.
- Every success toasts the message and reloads both the dialog's lists and the accounts table; **Close** closes it.

Browser-tested: Sam's Alpha Mart role changed to Area Manager → "{name} is now Area Manager in Alpha Mart."; Casey, in no organization ("Not in any organization yet."), added to Beta Deli with the Owner role → "Casey Keeper is now Owner in Beta Deli.", the new line showing Owner selected, and the Organizations list counting Beta Deli's members 0, then 1; removing Casey with the password → the toast "Casey Keeper is the only Owner of Beta Deli. Make someone else an Owner first." and Casey stays.

**Big-delete dialogs** (§19.7) — each is a real `<form>` holding one shared password field (`<x-crud.password-confirm>`): label "Your password *", helper "This cannot be undone, so confirm it is you." — or "Confirm it is you." for a removal that can be put back (a member, a person's place in an organization, a platform role) — `type="password"`, `autocomplete="current-password"`. *Reason for the form: without a form boundary Chrome treats the field as an "unowned" password and autofills the page's search box as its username.* Behavior: an empty field shows "Password is required." and sends nothing; the password travels in the DELETE body (`{password}`; the Organizations page's organization dialog sends `{confirm_name, password}` and checks both first); a 422 carrying a `password` error shows it under the field (the organization dialog shows each field's error under that field); anything else — a guard's 403 / 422 message, the 429 — is an error toast. Password inputs: `remove-member-password` (Members), `delete-organization-password` (the platform's Organizations page), `delete-account-password` (Users), `remove-platform-role-password` and `remove-membership-password` (Users; the latter in the Organizations dialog's remove form), `delete-role-password` (Roles), `delete-permission-password` (Permissions), and on the signage pages `confirm-channel-deletion-password`, `confirm-campaign-deletion-password` and `delete-ad-password` (the Ad Builder's Ads tab, dialog `confirm-ad-deletion`). The generic delete dialog takes a `password` flag and the shared table helper a `deleteNeedsPassword` option (Permissions, Channels, Campaigns, Ads); the Members, Organizations, Users and Roles pages handle the password in their own delete code, the same way.

**Plain password forms** (§19.7) — the Organizations tab's delete dialog (`delete-organization-password`) and Profile's delete dialog reopen by themselves when their bag (`organizationDeletion`, `userDeletion`) holds errors (the Create organization dialog likewise for `newOrganization`, §10.2, though it asks no password); the password-change form is inline (bag `updatePassword`). Each shows the server's message under its field — the limiter's "Too many wrong passwords. Try again in {n} seconds." included — and checks in the browser first, with its label: "Password is required." (organization delete, account delete) or "Current password is required." (password change).

---

## 21. Seeding / bootstrap (idempotent; model events suppressed; safe to re-run)

1. For each of the 42 names in `Permission::LABELS`: upsert by `name`, setting `label` (labels repaired on every run).
2. The Super-Admin role = the role with id `Role::superAdminId()` (exact name, §4.1), or a new role named `Super-Admin` when there is none; **force `is_global = true` and save it on every run**; **sync every permission row that exists** onto it (uncatalogued ones included).
3. **The Owner role only:** the role with key `owner`; only when there is none, create `{key: 'owner', name: 'Owner', is_global: false, organization_id: NULL, created_by: NULL}` (the name from `Role::STARTERS`) and set its permissions exactly to its starting list (`Role::starterPermissions('owner')`, §7.1). An existing Owner role is never touched — its name and permissions are the super admin's to change, and a re-seed must never undo that. **The seeder touches no other organization role:** all four starters come with the migrations (§22), so a re-seed neither resets what they allow nor brings back one the super admin deleted (tested: Staff changed to `screen-view` + `screen-store` keeps exactly those after re-seeding). *Reason: public signup needs the Owner role (§10.1).*
4. The first admin: find or build by email `config('app.seed_admin_email')` = `SEED_ADMIN_EMAIL`, **admin@gmail.com** when it is not set or empty (*a live server sets the owner's own address: a "forgot password" link goes to this inbox, so it must never keep the development one* — tested); set first_name "Admin", last_name "Momin", phone "0000000000", `email_verified_at` = now (every run). The password is set **only when the account is new OR `SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is set** — from the env value, else a random 16-char alphanumeric printed once: `SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD is not set — generated admin password: {password}`. A re-run without `SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD` never touches a working password; with it, the password is reset to that value.
5. Attach the admin to the sentinel `(organization_id 0, role_id = the Super-Admin role's id)` without detaching anything (upsert of the pair) — by role object, never a hardcoded id.
6. Running twice must not error or duplicate (tested: 42 permissions, as many as `LABELS`; one Super-Admin role; four roles carrying a key; one admin).

No organization and no organization member are seeded. **`migrate` alone already leaves the whole 42-permission catalogue with its labels and the four starter organization roles with their starting permissions** (the baseline, less the four `daypart-*` the migration after it takes away, plus billing's two and the TV app's three, §22), so public signup is open before the seeder ever runs, and step 1 finds every label already right and step 3 finds the Owner role there and leaves it alone. What the seeder adds is only the Super-Admin role and the first admin, who becomes the primary super admin. Tested (after seeding): each starter role carries its `Role::STARTERS` name, is not global, has no organization and holds exactly its starting permissions — Owner `ORGANIZATION` + `organization-view` + `organization-destroy`, Admin `ORGANIZATION` + `organization-view`; and tested again **before** any seeder runs, straight off the migrations (§22).

---

## 22. Migrations — one file per table (and the upgrade path that is gone)

**`database/migrations` holds 36 files: the 26 that build the schema of this document directly — Laravel's own three, one `create_{table}_table` file for every other table, and the catalogue (§22.1) — and the ten changes made since, each a migration of its own (§22.2a, §22.2b).** The history was squashed twice — the 2026-09-10 → 2026-09-17 rebuild on 2026-09-17, and everything after it (the Ad Builder's later columns, the platform's library, the channels' move into the libraries, email verification, the uploads table, the rename of store to organization) on 2026-10-01 — each time with its effect folded into the create-table file it used to patch. Read this section as "what a fresh database is", not as a history.

*Two databases ran the old files: the owner's local MySQL and the live server. Both hold every table already, so each file's `up()` returns at once when its table is there (`Schema::hasTable`), and the catalogue file leaves a catalogue that exists alone and deletes the old files' rows from `migrations`. A file is therefore never edited to change a database that exists — that database would skip it: a change is a new migration, reversible, and harmless on a database already in its shape (each later file asks first whether its column, table or rows are there — `MigrationsTest` runs every file against a database that has them).*

### 22.1 What `migrate` creates (the 26 files)
Each one writes its final shape:

| migration | what it produces |
|---|---|
| `0001_01_01_000000_create_users_table` | `users` (§2.1 — **no `created_by`**, with `pending_email`), `password_reset_tokens`, `sessions`. There is **no `personal_access_tokens` migration at all** (§26.8) |
| `0001_01_01_000001_create_cache_table`, `0001_01_01_000002_create_jobs_table` | framework tables, outside this document |
| `2026_10_01_200100_create_organizations_table` | `organizations` (§2.2) with `accepts_network_ads` and **no `deleted_at`** |
| `2026_10_01_200200_create_roles_table` | `roles` (§2.3) with `key` string(32) unique, **no `is_signup_default`**, and `organization_id` **ON DELETE CASCADE** |
| `2026_10_01_200300_create_permissions_table`, `2026_10_01_200400_create_role_has_permissions_table` | §2.4, §2.5 |
| `2026_10_01_200500_create_organization_user_table` | `organization_user` (§2.6) — `organization_id` unsigned bigint, **no FK**, default 0 (the sentinel) |
| `2026_10_01_200600_create_invitations_table` | `invitations` (§2.7) |
| `2026_10_01_200700_create_activity_logs_table` | `activity_logs` (§2.8) **with `organization_id` and the `(organization_id, created_at)` index**; on MySQL only it then partitions the table: `created_at` → DATETIME, PK → `(id, created_at)`, the `actor_id` FK dropped, partitions `p{Y}`…`pmax` (§17.5) |
| `2026_10_01_200800_create_media_table` | `media` (§2.9, §6.6) — `organization_id` nullable (NULL is the platform's own library), its foreign key kept; a file keeps its name alone, with no dates or description |
| `2026_10_01_200900_create_screens_table`, `2026_10_01_201000_create_pairing_requests_table` | `screens`, with `timezone`, `default_media_id` and `accepts_network_ads`; and the codes of televisions not yet claimed |
| `2026_10_01_201100_create_channels_table`, `2026_10_01_201200_create_channel_ads_table` | `channels` (with `organization_id`; `name` **not** unique) and `channel_ads`, each ad holding a library row by `media_id` (required, cascades on delete) (§2.9, §6.6) |
| `2026_10_01_201300_create_playlist_items_table` | `playlist_items` with **`media_id`, `channel_id` and `duration_seconds` nullable** — a line carries a file or a channel; made after `channels`, which `channel_id` points at |
| `2026_10_01_201400_create_dayparts_table`, `2026_10_01_201500_create_daypart_exceptions_table`, `2026_10_01_201600_create_schedule_rules_table` | signage scheduling as it stood on 2026-10-01: §22.2a drops the two daypart tables again and leaves `schedule_rules` with `start_time` / `end_time` and no `daypart_id` |
| `2026_10_01_201700_create_campaigns_table`, `2026_10_01_201800_create_campaign_screen_table` | `campaigns`, with `start_time` / `end_time` and **no `daypart_id`** |
| `2026_10_01_201900_create_builder_ads_table`, `2026_10_01_202000_create_builder_assets_table` | the Ad Builder's `builder_ads` and `builder_assets` — `organization_id` nullable (NULL is the platform's, shared with every organization) and **ON DELETE CASCADE** (a backstop to the purge, §15.4), `created_by` (and `builder_ads.updated_by`) SET NULL (§2.9); otherwise outside this document |
| `2026_10_01_202100_create_builder_fonts_table` | `builder_fonts` — the fonts installed for the whole installation, not organization content; `installed_by` SET NULL (§2.9) |
| `2026_10_01_202200_create_uploads_table` | `uploads` — files on their way in, sent in chunks (docs/UPLOADS-SPEC.md) |
| `2026_10_01_202300_insert_permissions_and_starter_roles` | **the one data migration** — §22.2 |

### 22.2 `2026_10_01_202300_insert_permissions_and_starter_roles`
It writes the starting state, in one transaction — and only while the `permissions` table is empty: an installation that has its catalogue keeps it.
1. Insert a **41-permission catalogue with its labels** — the table of §3.1 less the five added since (billing's two and the TV app's three, §22.2b), and the four `daypart-*` permissions §22.2a deletes again.
2. Insert the **four starter organization roles** (`key` set, `is_global = false`, `organization_id` NULL, `created_by` NULL) and give each exactly its starting permissions (§7.1): **Owner** = the 26 organization permissions of its own list + `organization-view` + `organization-destroy` (28); **Admin** = the 26 + `organization-view` (27); **Staff** = `screen-view`, `screen-playlist`, `media-view`, `media-store`, `media-update`, `media-destroy`, `daypart-view` (7); **Viewer** = `screen-view`, `media-view`, `daypart-view` (3). The four `daypart-*` among them go in §22.2a, which leaves Owner 24, Admin 23, Staff 6 and Viewer 2; billing's migration then gives Owner and Admin `billing-view` (25 and 24) — what `Role::STARTERS` says now.

**Self-contained on purpose:** it keeps its own copies of the catalogue, the 26 organization permissions and the four starter lists and reads no model, so a later change to `Permission::LABELS` or `Role::STARTERS` cannot change what it did. `down()` deletes the four roles by key (their grants cascade) and then the 41 permission rows.

Consequences to keep in a port:
- **After `migrate` alone — no seeder — a fresh install already has all 42 permissions and all four starter roles** (this file and the three that change the catalogue after it), so public signup works before anything is seeded (§10.1, §21). The seeder then only repairs the labels, creates the Super-Admin role and the first admin, and puts the Owner role back should it be missing (§21).
- **A permission added later still ships its own migration** that inserts it with its label and grants it to Super-Admin — and, for an organization permission the Owner role (and Admin, while the installation has it) should hold, to those roles by key, because neither this migration nor the seeder ever touches an existing role (§7.1, §26.11).

### 22.2a `2026_10_02_100000_put_the_time_on_the_schedule_rule` (owner, 2026-10-01)
*"Dayparts ko hata k … Playlist mein jab schedule set karte ha waha."* The first change after the one-per-table files, and a migration of its own as the rule above asks. A schedule rule now says its hours itself (`schedule_rules.start_time` / `end_time`, both NULL for the whole day), so:
1. **Read first, refuse first.** Every rule naming a daypart is worked out before anything is changed. A daypart with one set of hours gives them to the rule, which keeps its days. A daypart with other hours on some weekdays (or closed ones) becomes **one rule for each set of hours**, repeating weekly on the weekdays that keep them — the first written over the rule itself, the others after it on the same line, the line's rules numbered again; a rule its daypart never opened for stays a rule that never fires (weekly, on no weekday) rather than vanishing, since a line with no rule plays around the clock. Two things cannot be said that way and **stop the migration with nothing changed**, naming the rule: a repeat that cannot also say "only on these weekdays" (every N days, a date of the month, once a year) on such a daypart, and a daypart one of whose weekdays opens before the night before has closed, on a rule that tells its days apart.
2. Add `start_time` and `end_time` (time, nullable, after `playlist_item_id`); write the rows in one transaction, each rule letting go of its daypart as it is rewritten (so a run repeated after a failure rewrites nothing twice); drop the `daypart_id` key and column; drop `daypart_exceptions` and `dayparts`; delete the four `daypart-*` permissions with every grant of them.
3. `down()` gives the two tables, the column and the four permissions back — a daypart for every set of hours a rule holds, per organization, named by its hours; the permissions to Super-Admin, to Owner and Admin, and `daypart-view` to Staff and Viewer.

Each step is guarded (the table or column there, or not), so it is harmless on a database already in this shape. *Tested* (`tests/Feature/System/ScheduleTimesMigrationTest.php`): for 63 pairings of a rule's days with a daypart, what the old rows said at every half hour of two months — worked out from the old rows by the old models' rules — equals what the line says afterwards; the split rows and their places on the line; the rule that never fired; each refusal leaving every table, column, rule and permission as it was; the round trip down and up.

### 22.2b The changes since the one-per-table files (each a migration of its own, reversible)
| migration | what it does |
|---|---|
| `2026_10_02_100000_put_the_time_on_the_schedule_rule` | the dayparts go and a schedule rule says its own hours — §22.2a (signage) |
| `2026_10_07_200000_add_copied_from_id_to_builder_assets_table` | `builder_assets.copied_from_id` (FK to the platform file it was copied from, NULL on delete) and an `(organization_id, copied_from_id)` index — the Premium Templates' copies (signage) |
| `2026_10_07_200100_give_every_organization_its_own_copy_of_the_platform_files_its_ads_use` | data: every organization's ad that named one of the platform's files gets the organization's own copy, its draft and published version pointing at the copies, a published page written again; `down()` points them back and deletes the copies whose platform file is still there (`OwnFilesMigrationTest`; signage) |
| `2026_10_08_100000_add_unlock_switches_to_organizations_table` | `organizations.premium_templates_unlocked` and `platform_channels_unlocked`, boolean, default true (§2.2) |
| `2026_10_08_100100_add_copied_from_id_to_media_table` | `media.copied_from_id` (FK to the platform row it was copied from, NULL on delete) and an `(organization_id, copied_from_id)` index (signage) |
| `2026_10_08_100200_give_every_organization_its_own_copy_of_the_platform_files_on_its_screens` | data: every playlist line naming a platform library row gets the organization's own copy and keeps its place, seconds and schedule — a copy the organization has no room for is not made, and said in the log, so a deploy never stops there; `down()` points every line back and takes the copies away (`PlatformLinesMigrationTest`; signage) |
