| `2026_10_08_100300_insert_the_billing_permissions` | inserts `billing-view` "View Billing" and `billing-update` "Change Billing" (only when missing); grants both to Super-Admin (picked out of the global roles by the exact name in PHP, §4.1) and `billing-view` to the roles keyed `owner` and `admin`; touches no role otherwise; `down()` deletes both with every grant |
| `2026_10_08_100400_add_copied_from_id_to_builder_ads_table` | `builder_ads.copied_from_id` (the Premium Template a copy was made from, NULL on delete), filled for the copies made before it through their pages' `media.copied_from_id` (`AdCopiedFromMigrationTest`; signage) |
| `2026_10_10_100000_create_player_app_releases_table` | `player_app_releases` — the TV app's versions: `version_code` unique, `version_name`, `version_read_from_file`, `path`, `size`, `sha256`, `notes`, `uploaded_by` FK users SET NULL (§2.9, §16a) |
| `2026_10_10_100100_insert_the_player_app_permissions` | inserts `player-app-view` "View TV App", `player-app-store` "Upload TV App" and `player-app-destroy` "Delete TV App Versions" (only when missing) and grants them to Super-Admin alone (exact name in PHP); `down()` deletes them with every grant |

The two permission migrations are what a permission added later looks like (§22.2): the catalogue's baseline is never edited, Super-Admin is found by its exact name in PHP, and an organization permission the Owner role (and Admin, while the installation has it) should hold is granted to those roles by key.

### 22.3 Tested — `tests/Feature/System/DatabaseSchemaTest.php` (before any seeder runs)
The rules that live in the schema and the starting data rather than in code: a fresh install's `permissions` table equals `Permission::LABELS` **exactly** — the same names with the same labels, and nothing else; each of the four starter roles carries its `Role::STARTERS` name, is not global, has no organization and holds exactly `Role::starterPermissions(key)`, and the table holds **exactly those four roles**; `channels.organization_id` exists with **no** unique index on `channels.name`, and `activity_logs.organization_id` has its `(organization_id, created_at)` index; `organizations.deleted_at` is absent, the `roles.organization_id` foreign key is `cascade`, and deleting an organization row straight in the database takes its custom role; `users.created_by`, `roles.is_signup_default` and the `personal_access_tokens` table are all absent.

### 22.4 The upgrade path (history — these files no longer exist)
*Squashed on 2026-10-01 as well, the same way:* `2026_09_21_100000` and `100100` (the platform's library; channel ads moved into the libraries, each old ad given a row naming its file where it lay), `2026_09_21_120000` (an ad's published version), `2026_09_22_100000` and `2026_10_01_130000` (the "Show in playlists" tick, come and gone), `2026_09_23_100000` (portrait ads), `2026_09_29_100000` (email verification, which marked every account of its day confirmed), `2026_09_29_120000` (uploads), `2026_09_29_130000`/`130100` and `2026_10_01_110000`–`120000` (shared assets and ads, and their three permissions, come and gone), `2026_10_01_100000` (a file keeps its name alone), `2026_10_01_140000` and `150000` (store renamed organization: the four labels, then every table, column, index, key, permission name and logged action). A dump from before `150000` ran (2026-10-01 22:39 UTC on the live server) still says `stores`, and is brought forward with the code of commit `0eb3740` first.

So that the shape of the schema stays explicable, these migrations did exist and have been deleted: `2026_09_10_110000`, `130000`, `140200`, `140300` and `2026_09_15_100100`, `100200` (signage and channel-permission patches), `2026_09_16_100000` (`activity-destroy`), `110000` (`roles.key`), `110300` (dropping `users.created_by` and `roles.is_signup_default`), `120000` (organization roles may hold platform permissions; the Owner's `organization-destroy`; the "View All Accounts" / "View All Organizations" relabels), `120100` (`channels.organization_id`, the unique name index dropped), `120200` (`activity_logs.organization_id` and its index), `2026_09_17_100000` (merging same-named organization copies into one organization role), `110000` (`organization-transfer`, and `organization-view` for every role that could open the old Organization settings page), `120000` (deleting `organization-transfer` with every grant, and taking `user-view` / `user-destroy` off every organization role and custom role), `120100` (deleting the organizations an older build had only hidden, and dropping `organizations.deleted_at`) — and `0001_01_01_000003_create_personal_access_tokens_table`. Their feature tests went with them.

Three things a port should carry forward:
- **The old model's data mapping**, should an old database ever have to be converted by hand. On the organizations' side of a role each held permission was translated — `user-view` → `member-view`; `user-organization` → `member-invite`; `user-organization-view` → `member-view`; `user-organization-assign` → `member-invite` + `member-update`; `user-destroy` → `member-remove`; `user-organization-unassign` → `member-remove`; `user-update` → nothing; anything else → itself — and only the 22 organization permissions were kept. Platform roles lost the five removed rows and nothing else. The old signup role (`is_signup_default`) became the Owner role **in place**, so its holders became their organizations' Owners through the same role id; a role shared across organizations was split into one copy per organization, and same-named copies were later merged back into **one** organization role, grouped by folded name (§7.5's `fold`, computed in PHP — never a SQL comparison, whose collation folds differently), the oldest of a name winning and the copies' organization permissions gathered onto it. An organization's creator who was still a member became its Owner; an organization left without one stayed **ownerless** (§8.2) until the platform gave it an Owner (§10.3, §13.4). *"No owner" on the old Organizations page came from people holding an organization's own copy named "Owner" rather than the Owner role itself — which is what the merge fixed.*
- **The Super-Admin rule inside a data migration.** Every one of them narrowed by name in SQL and then compared the **exact** name in PHP (§4.1), so a look-alike under MySQL's accent-insensitive collation was never granted the catalogue nor spared a translation. One difference from the application: they acted on **every** role whose name is exactly `Super-Admin` (ids, plural), where `Role::superAdminId()` keeps only the lowest id (§26.1). A port writing its own data migration must keep both halves of that.
- **What a `down()` can honestly undo.** None of them restored role-permission links, un-split roles, undid Owner appointments or brought a deleted organization back: which old permission a role held, which copy a person held, and what each copy allowed are recoverable from nothing once the change is made — and each said so rather than guessing. The catalogue migrations left (§22.2, and billing's and the TV app's, §22.2b) have the same property in reverse: each `down()` removes what it inserted wholesale, with every grant of it, and does not reconstruct whatever the super admin had made of it. (The Ad Builder's `2026_09_17_140100`, which granted its four permissions to Super-Admin, was squashed into §22.2 on 2026-10-01.)

---

## 23. Invariants checklist (assert in the port's tests)
1. An account's `organization_user` rows are organization_id = 0 XOR organization_id > 0 — never both kinds; every membership-creating door refuses the other tier.
2. UNIQUE(organization_id, user_id) — one role per person per organization.
3. Role kinds hold: organization role (not global, no organization; the Owner role is the one with key `owner`), custom (no key, not global, its organization), platform (no key, global, no organization); what a role is for never changes once it exists. An organization role may be held in any real organization; a custom role only in its own organization; a platform role only on organization 0; Super-Admin never in a real organization.
4. A super admin is exactly an account whose organization-0 row holds the role named exactly `Super-Admin`, compared in application code; no role created or renamed through HTTP can carry a name that folds to `superadmin`, nor one that folds like another role's in any list the role is shown in (§7.5). Every permission check answers yes for a super admin, except `network-ads-toggle`; for anybody else a check answers yes only when the role in context carries the permission (or a hand-written gate says so).
5. No endpoint sets another person's name, email, phone or password.
6. No role gains a permission that does not fit it: every organization role and custom role ⊆ `ORGANIZATION` ∪ `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` (never `user-view`, `user-destroy`, `activity-destroy`, `billing-update`, an uncatalogued permission or `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY` — the catalogue and the TV app); a custom role written by a member inside an organization ⊆ also that member's permissions there, while a super admin may write any role but Super-Admin from the platform; organization roles and platform roles are made and changed only by a super admin (an organization role for every organization at once); platform roles ∌ `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY`; Super-Admin is never edited through HTTP; neither Super-Admin nor the Owner role is ever deleted, and no role is deleted while somebody holds it.
7. Nobody hands out a role outside their reach, manages a member outside their reach, or manages themselves — reach being the permissions alone, the Owner role no exception (§8.1). One exception: a super admin's Users → Organizations from the platform, which puts anybody outside the platform team in any organization with any role that organization has, changes it or takes them out, while keeping at least one Owner (§13.4). There is no separate handover: inside an organization, an organization changes hands by giving (or inviting to) the Owner role under these same rules (§11.3).
8. The last Owner of an organization cannot be demoted or removed (from the Members page — whoever reaches them — or from the platform's Users → Organizations), leave or self-delete — each decided under a lock on the organization row, so concurrent requests cannot both pass; only a platform account deletion (or platform organization creation) leaves an organization ownerless, and the platform's "Invite owner" acts only on an organization with no Owner (§10.3).
9. Every write resolves its target inside where the actor stands (404 outside), except the documented platform and impersonation cases (§6.7).
10. Invitation tokens never touch the database in plaintext; accepting, declining, revoking and replacing delete the row; one link creates at most one membership and at most one account (row lock); an existing account never joins by the link alone.
11. The primary super admin cannot be deleted or lose Super-Admin; super admins cannot self-delete; only the primary deletes a super admin, removes Super-Admin, or invites a new super admin.
12. An impersonation can only be ended by the account being viewed as, and every sign-in clears any impersonation left in the session.
13. An organization member with no organization selected has zero permissions — and a person of exactly one organization never stays without one: it is chosen on their first request, and an organization they are no longer in is forgotten on their next (§5.1); a platform role always answers for a platform account; revocation takes effect on the next request. A paused organization's people hold no permission in it and reach none of its pages, while the platform keeps every power over it and its screens keep playing (§2.2).
14. Deleting an account removes the account with its memberships, its sessions (database driver), its password-reset token and the invitations addressed to its email, and empties `actor_id` on its log entries — nothing anybody else holds; only the platform or the person themself deletes an account. Deleting an organization leaves no memberships, invitations, custom roles, screens, own channels, media rows, Ad Builder designs and assets or unfinished uploads for it, unlinks files only after commit, and deletes the organization row for good — no custom role can outlive its organization; the accounts and the activity log stay. Deleting a role takes its open invitations with it and says how many.
15. Every state-changing endpoint writes one activity row, except the §17.3 exclusions; a refused email never fails the request (`email_sent: false`).
16. Status-code discipline: who the actor is → 403; the target's state or a requested value → 422; invisible targets → 404; throttles → 429.
17. Every password re-confirmation (§19.7) — the big deletes, self-deleting, changing the password — changes nothing without the actor's current password and asks for it only after that endpoint's up-front refusals; all of them share one counter per person, so five wrong passwords on any of them refuse all of them — even with the right password — for the rest of the 60-second window (429 for JSON, the message in the form's bag for a plain form).
18. Re-running the seeder never renames, changes or brings back an organization role or a platform role — it creates the Owner role only when none exists (and re-syncs Super-Admin with every permission row, which grants nothing new, §3.3).
19. A permission reaches where its role sits: on a platform role every organization; on an organization's role only the organization the member works in — no endpoint lets an organization's role act on another organization; inside an organization the channels found are that organization's own and the activity entries read carry that organization's id. What only works above the organizations — the Organizations page and its writes (the billing switches among them), the accounts pages, activity partitions and maintenance, giving an organization an owner, "Log in as", the platform team, the platform's Users → Organizations, the catalogue, Developer → TV App — keeps its tier lock whatever permission rows an organization's role holds; an organization never unlocks itself (`billing-update` is on no organization's role).
20. A role does not matter, its permissions do: deleting an organization is the permission `organization-destroy`, opening the Organizations tab `organization-view` (the Owner role starts with both); giving the Owner role — which is how an organization changes hands, §11.3 — or managing an Owner is reach like any other role's. The Owner role's key marks only who owns an organization — no rename or permission edit moves it — and grants nothing by itself.
21. Nothing answers 500. A parameter may arrive in ANY shape — `?search[]=x`, `?device_uuid[]=x`, `name[]=x` — and must be refused (422) or read as nothing sent, never cast: an array reaching a `like` clause, a `trim()` or a cache key is an "Array to string conversion". Two places make this easy to get wrong: a rate limiter runs BEFORE the endpoint's validation (so it has to survive anything an open endpoint is sent), and a custom validation closure still runs after `string` has failed unless the rule list starts with `bail`. Asserted by the twenty adversarial files in `tests/Feature/Security/`, including a sweep of every route as a guest, a Staff member, an Owner, platform support, a super admin and an Owner who has not confirmed their email (`HttpSurfaceSweepTest`): every one answers a page, a redirect or a 4xx; a guest is offered only the public doors (sign in, register, forgot/reset, an invitation link, the player and its manifest, the panel's manifest, the TV app's `latest.json`, the device endpoints and the health check); and an account that has not confirmed its email only the doors of §3.7 — every other signed-in route redirects it to "Check your inbox", or answers its JSON 403, and writes nothing.
22. Every account proves its inbox before the panel opens — by the signup's signed link, an invitation's link, a completed password reset, or a super admin viewing as it (§10.1a); one address is one account; every email that confirms an address is counted in one budget per account and per visitor, whichever door asks; an account never confirmed is removed after seven days with its organization, unless that organization holds anybody or anything.
23. A page drawn for one person in one organization never acts for another: a request whose session context differs is refused before it does anything (409, or back with the words), except what changes the session on purpose (§20.4a).
24. Every JSON refusal carries words a person can read; no model, route or exception detail reaches the page (§19.1).

---

## 24. Laravel → Python translation notes (framework mechanics made explicit)
- **Authentication transport:** session-cookie based. The cookie holds a session id; the server-side session record holds the authenticated user id. "Log in" = store that id + regenerate the session id (**data kept**); "log out" = remove it and invalidate the session (data flushed); impersonation swaps WHICH id is stored, remembering the original and the impersonated ids under two session keys. A session whose user no longer exists is simply unauthenticated — the session itself is not flushed, which is why every sign-in clears the impersonation keys (§14.1). (On the `database` session driver, deleting an account also deletes the session rows whose user id is that account's, §15.3 — so such a session starts over empty.)
- **Events:** the framework fires a `Login` event on every authentication (password login, `Auth::login`, remember-me re-authentication); one listener clears the impersonation keys. A port must hook the equivalent of "a session became authenticated".
- **Row locks:** `lockForUpdate()` = `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` inside a transaction — used on the organization row for team changes (`changeTeam`: change role and remove — from the organization or the platform —, adding a person to an organization from the platform, leave), on every organization a person owns before self-deletion, on the invitation row when accepting (by the link or on the dashboard) or registering, on another account holding a changed address when it is confirmed (§14.2), and on an unconfirmed account's organizations and then its own row before the seven-day prune (§10.1a). SQLite ignores it (its writers are serialized anyway); a port on MySQL or PostgreSQL must keep the lock.
- **`Str::ascii`** (the role-name fold, §7.5, and the merge migration, §22) = transliteration to ASCII (`unidecode` in Python), then lower-case, then strip every non `a-z0-9`. **`report($e)`** = send an exception to the error log without failing the request (§9.1).
- **Remember-me:** an optional long-lived cookie holding the user id + `remember_token`; with no live session the server re-authenticates when it matches — which is why rotating the token (password reset, logout) invalidates every outstanding cookie.
- **CSRF (never optional):** every state-changing request carries a per-session token — a hidden input on HTML forms, a header on the AJAX surface. A missing or stale token is rejected (HTTP 419 in the original).
- **Guest / auth middleware:** `auth` → a JSON request gets 401, a browser request a redirect to `/login` (remembering the intended URL); `guest` → an authenticated user is redirected to the dashboard. **`verified`** (the framework's `EnsureEmailIsVerified`) → an account whose email is not confirmed gets, for JSON, 403 "Your email address is not verified.", else a redirect to `verification.notice` (remembering the intended URL). **Signed URLs** (`URL::temporarySignedRoute`, `hasValidSignature`) = a URL carrying an expiry and an HMAC over the whole URL with the application key; changing any part of it, or a later moment than its expiry, fails the check. **`Crypt::encryptString`** = authenticated encryption with the application key (the signup's sealed moment, §10.1).
- **Middleware order:** §3.3 — malformed text (400) → session → CSRF (419) → authenticate → throttle → bind route models (404) → choose the only organization → refuse a stale tab (409) → `verified` → `organization.active` → gates (403) → controller.
- **`confirmed` rule:** the request must include `password_confirmation` equal to `password`; a mismatch is an error on `password`. **`current_password` rule:** the value must verify against the authenticated user's hash. **`Password::defaults()`** here = min 8.
- **Flash / redirect-back / named error bags:** flash = write-once session data consumed by the next request. An HTML validation failure is a 302 back with field errors (in the named bag) and the old input; success is a 302 with a flashed status. The JSON surface uses §19.1's shapes and JSON success = 200/201 `{message, …extras}`. `abort(403, msg)` renders an error page for HTML and `{message}` for JSON.
- **Route-model binding:** a `{organization}`/`{user}`/`{role}`/`{invitation}`/`{permission}` segment is fetched by id before gates; missing → 404 (a deleted organization is simply missing — nothing is soft-deleted).
- **"Pivot":** the join table `organization_user`; "raw pivot query" = querying it directly instead of joining through `organizations` (how sentinel rows stay reachable). **attach** = INSERT a join row; **detach** = DELETE; **sync(ids)** = make the set EXACTLY ids (insert missing, delete extras — load-bearing for the seeder); **syncWithoutDetaching** = upsert pairs, delete nothing.
- **firstOrCreate(match, extra)** = select by match, insert match+extra only if absent, never update; **updateOrCreate(match, values)** = the same lookup but update the found row.
- **Model events:** two are load-bearing — an Organization's **deleting** event runs `purgeContents()` before the row is deleted (§15.4), and a User's **deleted** event removes the account's sessions (database driver), password-reset token and the invitations addressed to its email, and empties `actor_id` on its log entries (§15.3). A port without ORM hooks must call both from every organization-delete and account-delete path. Bulk deletes and the seeder run without per-row events.
- **`DB::afterCommit(fn)`** = run `fn` after the outermost transaction commits (immediately when there is none) and never on rollback — used to unlink media files (a channel's among them — its ads are library rows), the Ad Builder's asset files and posters (`Organization::purgeBuilder`, and a design's or an asset's own delete) and a campaign's files (its old file when an edit replaces it, and all of them when it is deleted).
- **Token hashing:** invitation tokens are SHA-256 hex (deterministic, so the row is found by hashing the presented token); password-reset tokens are bcrypt (compared per email). Passwords: bcrypt, hash-on-write.
- **Notifications:** "on-demand" mail = a mail sent to a raw address with no user row; not queued here — sent inside the request, with every failure caught by `sendLink` (§9.1).
- **Collation:** MySQL `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` makes `=`, `!=`, LIKE, unique indexes and ORDER BY case- AND accent-insensitive; SQLite (the test suite) is binary. That is why the Super-Admin anchor is compared in application code (§4.1) and signup lower-cases emails (§10.1); a port must not reintroduce a collation-dependent name or email comparison (§26.1, §26.7).
- **`can:` middleware / Gate** → a decorator or dependency `require_permission('member-view')` running §5.3 with per-request caching; the four hand-written gates (§3.4) are plain predicates. **`Gate::before`** = a hook consulted before ANY ability — defined or not, route middleware, template checks and `can()` in code alike: a non-null answer is final, null falls through to the ability's own check (§3.3). A port must put the super-admin rule in that single choke point, not in each decorator's permission lookup — otherwise an undefined or renamed permission would deny a super admin.
- **`RateLimiter`** (the password limiter, §19.7) = a cache counter with a fixed window: `hit(key, 60)` creates the counter and a 60-second timer on the first hit (later hits do not extend it) and increments; `tooManyAttempts(key, 5)` = counter ≥ 5 while the timer lives; `availableIn(key)` = seconds left on the timer; `clear(key)` deletes both. **`ValidationException::withMessages([...])->errorBag(bag)->status(429)`** = for a JSON request the usual field-error JSON (`{message, errors}`) sent with status 429; for a plain form the framework ignores the status and redirects back with the errors in that bag (a request may name another bag with an `_error_bag` input). **`validateWithBag(bag, rules)`** = validate, putting any failure in the named bag.
- **Scopes** (`visibleTo`, `availableInOrganization`, `organizationRoles`, `platform`, `Channel::availableTo`) → reusable query filters applied on reads AND write-target lookups.
- **Controller middleware** (`HasMiddleware`, `ChannelAdController`) = a closure the framework runs for every action of that controller after route-model binding and the route's gates but BEFORE the action's form-request validation — which is why a channel out of reach answers 404 before an uploaded file is validated (§6.6). A **FormRequest's `authorize()`** likewise runs before its rules.
- **Soft delete** (`deleted_at` + a default filter) — **none**: users never were (owner decision), and organizations were in an older build whose column is no longer in the schema (§2.2, §22). Every delete in this document is a real `DELETE`.
- **Timezone:** the server stores and compares `created_at` in UTC; the activity filter is timezone-correct end to end (§17.4).
- **Transactions:** signup; organization creation + owner invitation; Create organization on Settings → Organizations (organization + Owner membership); "Invite owner" (a member made Owner with the invitations it replaces; or replace / renew / open); invitation join; invitation registration (account + membership, under the invitation lock); every `changeTeam` change (change role and remove — from the organization or the platform —, adding a person to an organization from the platform, leave); self-delete (owned organizations locked; the account's `deleted` hook inside); role create/update (the name and the permission sync together); role delete (with its invitations); both organization delete paths (purge inside); confirming a changed email (§14.2); each account the seven-day prune removes, with its organizations (§10.1a); the conversion and merge migrations.
- **Scheduler** = cron running the monthly maintenance (1st, 00:30) and, for the people model, `accounts:prune-unverified` daily at 03:15 (§10.1a). **Non-MySQL partition emulation** uses SQLite's `strftime('%Y', …)` — re-implement for PostgreSQL.
- Routes shown are semantic, not sacred — keep the behavior, adapt shapes. HTML-form flows (auth pages, Profile, Settings → Organizations, the invitation page) speak redirects + flash; the panel's lists and modals speak JSON.

---

## 25. Acceptance scenarios (behavioral test suite in prose — all must pass)
1. **Signup:** one form creates the account, the organization (country "USA", `created_by` = the person) and an Owner membership; a submitted role id is ignored; without the Owner role the page shows the closed banner and a POST errors on `email` before validation, creating nothing; an invalid organization half creates nothing; `user.registered` is logged with the owner as actor; `Sana@Example.COM` is stored as `sana@example.com`, and a later signup as `SANA@example.com` fails on `email` (still one account); an email longer than its column is refused as a message, not a database error; the account starts unconfirmed, the link is sent and the signup lands on "Check your inbox"; the robot traps send back a filled hidden field, a missing, forged, too quick or two-hours-old sealed moment with "Please try again." on `form`, writing, logging and emailing nothing.
2. **Members directory:** every member of the organization is listed whoever brought them in, and nobody else (not another organization's member, not platform staff); `is_you`, `can_manage` per §8.3; Admin pickers offer `[admin, staff, viewer]` + in-reach custom roles, Owner pickers add `owner`; another organization's custom roles are never offered; invitations are listed with `is_expired` and `can_manage` (an Owner invitation is not manageable by an Admin); no `member-view` → 403; a platform user holding `member-view` → 404; a removed member gets 403 on the next request; a member of another organization is 404 to change or remove; one person with Staff in Alpha and Admin in Beta sees the list only in Beta.
3. **Change role / remove / leave:** an Owner changes a role and it is logged "from Staff to Admin"; an Admin giving Owner → 422 `role_id`, touching an Owner → 403 (change and remove) — the Owner role carries `organization-destroy`, which Admin lacks; nobody changes their own role (403); another organization's role → 422 `role_id`; a custom role gives and manages only within its reach (422 for giving Staff, 403 for changing a Staff member, 200 within reach); an Owner may demote another Owner, after which the last Owner cannot leave (422 with the exact message) and cannot be removed by the demoted one (403); a "Deputy" whose custom role holds every permission of the Owner role reaches an Owner, yet the organization's only Owner always stays — `can_manage` false on that row, changing their role → 422 on `role_id` and removing them (the password sent) → 422, both with exactly "{name} is the only Owner of Alpha Mart. Make someone else an Owner first." — and once a partner Owner exists the row is manageable and the Deputy makes the first Owner an Admin (200); removing — with the actor's password (§19.7) — takes only the membership (account, media `created_by`, screens stay; logged); removing yourself → 422, leaving works, clears the session organization and returns the dashboard redirect; Staff get 403 on change and remove.
   **From "Your organizations":** the profile of a person without an Organizations tab lists every organization of the person with their role in each ("Your organizations"); a Viewer leaves one with `DELETE /profile/organizations/{id}` → redirect to the profile with status "You left Joe's Diner.", the membership gone, the other organization kept, the session organization cleared (it was that organization), `member.left` logged — and the flash, apostrophe included, renders safely on the next page; the last Owner is refused with exactly "You are the only Owner of Alpha Mart. Make someone else an Owner before you leave." in bag `organizationMembership`, back on the page they came from, and the page says "You are its only Owner — make someone else an Owner before you leave."; once a co-owner exists, the Owner may leave; an organization the person is not in → 404; a guest → redirect to login; a platform account has no "Your organizations" section; from the Organizations tab, leaving another organization comes back to the tab ("You left Beta Deli."), and leaving the organization being worked in lands on the profile with the session organization cleared; in the browser, a Staff member leaves an organization from the profile and an organization's only Owner, on the Organizations tab, is told "You are its only Owner" with no Leave button.
4. **Organization invitations:** an Owner invites `'  New.Person@Example.com '` → stored `new.person@example.com`, `invited_by` = the Owner, expiry ≈ 7 days, only the SHA-256 of the mailed token stored; the link opens in `register` state; the mail's subject reads "You're invited to join Alpha Mart" and its page names Olive Owner, Alpha Mart, Staff and the link; refused (422 `email`) for an existing member (case-insensitive), a second invitation, or a platform account; an Admin inviting an Owner and a foreign role → 422 `role_id`; resending issues a new token and kills the old link; revoking closes the link; another organization's invitation → 404; an Owner invitation revoked by an Admin → 403; with a mail server that refuses, inviting still answers 201 with `email_sent: false` and exactly "The invitation for new.hire@example.com was created, but the email could not be sent. Use Resend to try again.", the invitation exists, the exception is reported, and resending answers 200 with `email_sent: false`.
5. **Responding:** a new person registers from the link → logged in, verified, Staff in the organization, session switched, invitation gone, `invitation.accepted` logged; an existing account (email in another case) sees `login` state with the intended URL stored, cannot register a second account (redirect back to the link), then sees `accept` and joins; a stranger sees `mismatch` and gets 403 on accept and decline; expired and unknown links show the invalid page and register does nothing; declining deletes and logs, redirecting a guest to login; a guest declining an invitation whose email has an account is logged under that account (actor id and name), not System; a platform account accepting an organization invitation is refused with "Platform accounts cannot join an organization."; an organization Owner accepting a platform invitation is refused; accepting while already a member keeps the old role, deletes the invitation and logs `invitation.accepted` with the member as actor; an invitation to a deleted organization is dead; an account not yet confirmed that accepts by the link is confirmed; the 21st link request in a minute is 429.
6. **Settings → Organizations:** an Admin (holding `organization-view` and `organization-update`) opens the Organizations tab of Settings (both tabs shown) and edits the details there; Staff get 403 on the page and the update, and their Settings has no Organizations tab; the Owner's sidebar has the Settings link at its foot and no link to `/settings/organization`, while their Profile tab links to the Organizations tab; the details form cannot change `accepts_network_ads`, `is_active` or `created_by`; out of the box the Owner sees "Delete Organization" and an Admin does not; an organization changes hands on the Members page — the Owner makes a Staff member Owner (200) and the new Owner makes the first one Admin (200) — while the Organizations tab shows no "Transfer Ownership", `POST /settings/organization/transfer-ownership` answers 404 and no `organization-transfer` permission exists; deleting needs `organization-destroy` (an Admin with its starting permissions → 403), the exact name (`alpha mart` refused) and the password (a wrong one → error in `organizationDeletion`); a successful delete removes the organization row for good with its memberships, invitation, custom role (the member who worked under it keeps their account), screen (+ playlist items; the device gets 401) and media rows and both files — and its own channel with its ad and the ad's two files —, clears the session organization and keeps accounts, the platform channel, the organization's activity entries and a neighbouring organization (including a shared member's Viewer role there) untouched; the log contains "1 screen" and "1 media file"; a platform user gets 404.
7. **Account deletion:** self-deletion removes the account and its memberships only (organizations, co-owners, colleagues stay; media and screen `created_by` → NULL; logged); the sole Owner of an organization is refused with the exact message naming it (a refusal decided before the password is checked); a wrong password → error in `userDeletion`; a person who invited others is deleted alone; super admins get 403; deleting a channel's creator keeps the channel with `created_by` NULL; a platform deletion also removes, on the `database` session driver, the account's sessions on every device (another person's session stays) and its password-reset token, and empties its screen's `created_by`.
8. **Platform organizations:** the listing shows `members_count` and `can` exactly (`{update: true, destroy: true, invite_owner: false}` for an organization that has an Owner; `invite_owner` true for an organization with none, members or not; false on every row for a platform user without `organization-store`) and no `owners` or `owner_invitation`; an Owner whose role holds `organization-view`, `organization-store`, `organization-update` and `organization-destroy` gets 403 on the page, the data, creating, updating and deleting an organization and "Invite owner", nothing changing; an organization member holding `organization-view` and `organization-update` gets 403 from the data and from `PUT /organizations/{id}`, and changes the organization's details from Settings → Organizations instead (the active switch unchanged); creating an organization sends an Owner invitation (lowercased email) and creates no membership, logged; a platform account's email as owner → 422 `owner_email`; "Invite owner" on an organization with no Owner invites anybody as Owner (201) and makes a member Owner at once (email case-insensitive; 200 with exactly "{name} is now an Owner of Orphan Mart. The earlier invitation for partner@example.com no longer works.", the earlier Owner invitation deleted and logged "…, replacing the invitation for partner@example.com"), while on an organization that has an Owner it refuses every address with 422 "{organization} already has an Owner." — keeping the co-owner invitation the organization's Owner sent, and sending nothing; inviting the same owner again — even after the first link expired, and in other capitals — answers 200 with `email_sent: true`, keeps the same invitation row with a new token hash and a fresh expiry, and sends a second email; a different address answers 201 with exactly "An invitation to own Typo Treats was sent to gina@example.com. The earlier invitation for gina@exmaple.com no longer works.", deletes the mistyped Owner invitation (its link now shows the invalid page) and leaves a Staff invitation to another address alone; an address the organization had already invited as Staff answers 200 and that same row becomes the Owner invitation; deleting requires the exact typed name and the actor's password, and removes the organization row, its screens, its custom role and its memberships, keeping accounts; support with only `organization-view` reads but gets 403 on create and delete; switching works into your own organizations, 403 into a stranger's organization, 403 for a super admin; required fields incl. `owner_email`, the 50-state list (`Texas`, `DC` refused), digits-only zip, and `is_active` edits hold; guests get 401 everywhere.
9. **Platform users:** a super admin sees memberships, `sole_owner_of`, `platform_role`, `is_you`, `is_primary` and `can` exactly (`manage_organizations` true for an organization Owner, false for platform staff), the tabs All · Platform Team · Organization Members (`group`) with `counts` whatever is searched, and anything else asked for as every account; Account and Joined sort either way round, and with no sort the list is A to Z whatever the case; support sees organization accounts only, whatever `group` says, with no `counts` and no tabs; an organization Owner gets 403 from the accounts page and data (the `global-tier` lock); deleting (with the actor's password, as removing a platform role) reports `ownerless_organizations` and logs "left without an Owner: …"; nobody deletes themselves (422), the primary (403), or — unless primary — another super admin (403); support with `user-destroy` gets 404 on platform accounts; removing a platform role: a non-primary super admin removes plain staff (200) but not another super admin or the primary (403), the primary cannot remove their own (422) but removes another super admin's; support gets 403; a super admin invites staff with a platform role (201, mailed), refused for an organization account's email and for an organization role (Admin); registering from a platform link creates an organization-0 membership; support gets 403 on platform invitations; the listing's query count is constant; a stale session organization never takes a super admin's powers; a second (non-primary) super admin inviting to Super-Admin gets 422 on `role_id` "Only the primary super admin can invite a super admin." and is not offered Super-Admin in the page's picker, while the primary is offered it and the same invitation succeeds (201).
10. **Roles:** in an organization, one list — the organization roles Owner, Admin, Staff, Viewer (`kind` organization, the Owner role marked `is_owner_role`, no edit or delete) then the organization's own custom roles only (a platform role and another organization's custom role absent) — with holders counted in this organization alone; a member creates a custom role only from permissions they hold there (422 otherwise; `activity-view` refused for an Owner, whose role does not start with it); inside an organization `Owner`, `admin`, ` STAFF ` and `Viewer` are refused names (the organization roles are in its list) and `cashier` beside a custom `Cashier` reads "There is already a role called Cashier. Choose another name.", while another organization creates its own `Cashier`; `Súper-Admin`, `SUPER ADMIN` and `super_admin` read "That name belongs to the Super-Admin role. Choose another." on create and are refused on rename (the name unchanged), `Ówner`, `O-W-N-E-R` and `Ädmin` are refused, `Organization Admin Assistant` is allowed; only the exact Super-Admin role on the platform row makes a super admin (an account holding a global role named `super-admin` is not one, gets 403 on `/permissions`, and `superAdminId()` / `primarySuperAdminId()` still name the real role and person); any member holding `role-update` edits a custom role whoever made it (logged); another organization's role → 404 on delete; a role above your reach → 403 on update and delete; a held role → 422 on delete; in an organization the organization roles → 403 on update (Viewer keeps its name) and on delete ("Organization roles are changed by the super admin, for every organization at once."); a custom role with two pending invitations is listed with `invitations_count` 2, and deleting it with the password answers exactly "Role Cashier deleted. 2 pending invitations to it were revoked.", removes both invitations and logs "Deleted role Cashier. 2 pending invitations to it were revoked."; on the platform the list starts `Super-Admin, Owner, Admin, Staff, Viewer` — Super-Admin neither editable nor deletable, the Owner role editable but not deletable, Viewer (held by nobody) editable and deletable, Admin (held) offered Delete too, deleting it answering 422 "Please unassign Admin from everyone first: 1 person still holds it."; a new role on the platform needs `type` ("Choose what this role is for."): "Floor Lead" as an organization role (not global, no organization, offered in every organization; logged "Created organization role Floor Lead, offered in every organization") and "Support" as a platform role (global; logged "Created platform role Support"), while an organization role holding `activity-destroy` or `user-view` is refused; on the platform an organization role named like an organization's custom role → "Alpha Mart already has a custom role called Cashier. Choose another name.", a platform role of that name is created; `/roles/assignable?role={Staff}` lists exactly what an organization role can carry, with no `unavailable` field, `?type=organization` lists `channel-view` but neither `user-view` nor `activity-destroy`, `?type=platform` lists `activity-destroy` and `user-view` but not `permission-view`; `PUT` Staff renamed "Cashier" with three permissions answers "Role Cashier updated in every organization.", sets exactly those permissions, reads "Allows View Screens, Pair Screens and View Media Library." and logs "Updated organization role Cashier (was Staff), in every organization"; `activity-destroy` on it → 422 "An organization role cannot hold Delete Old Activity Logs: it works above the organizations only."; the Owner role renamed "Proprietor" stays the Owner role with its holders still Owners, and deleting it with the password → 403 "The Owner role is never deleted: it is how an organization has an owner. Rename it or change what it allows instead.", while Viewer (held by nobody) is deleted; editing Super-Admin on the platform → 403; a super admin creates platform roles with organization and platform permissions but never `permission-*` (422); on the platform Beta Deli's custom role "Cashier" is listed with `organization_name` "Beta Deli", editable and deletable, renamed "Till" (logged "Updated role Till (was Cashier) in Beta Deli, from the platform") and deleted with the password, its `role.deleted` entry carrying Beta; a platform role that is not Super-Admin gets 403 on the roles data; `GET /roles/{id}/permissions` answers 404 for every role — the route is gone (§7.8); `/roles/assignable` inside an organization returns exactly the permissions the actor holds there (`media-view`, `role-view`, `screen-view` for a supervisor holding those), and for a platform role on the platform everything but the catalogue.
11. **Platform tiers:** a super admin gives `activity-view` / `activity-destroy` and `channel-*` to a platform role; `activity-destroy` goes on no organization's role — not even from the super admin on an organization role (422 "An organization role cannot hold Delete Old Activity Logs: it works above the organizations only.") — and an Owner cannot give `activity-view` or `channel-view` to a custom role (not held, 422); an organization role holding `activity-view` and `activity-destroy` reads its own organization's log and is offered the link, but gets 403 on the partition status and on maintenance and sees no storage panel; a platform reader reads but cannot run maintenance and does not see the button; a platform keeper runs it (logged); the catalogue goes on no role but Super-Admin; anybody else holding `permission-*` opens nothing (403, including renaming); a super admin runs the catalogue even when the Super-Admin role holds no rows; a platform user who is not a super admin cannot create roles (403).
12. **Loophole suite with the seeded roles:** the seeded Owner runs their organization (screens, media, members, roles, the Organizations tab of Settings, playlist save); every platform page is 403 to the seeded Owner — channels (the data, creating one, removing an ad), campaigns, activity, permissions, users and deleting an account, platform invitations and impersonation, none of which the Owner role holds, and the organizations data, behind the tier lock although the Owner role holds `organization-view`; the Owner may carry the platform's channel on a playlist but gets 403 adding an ad to it; network advertising cannot be switched on by the Owner or smuggled through the screen or settings forms; another organization's screens, files, people, invitations and roles are 404 and its media cannot be put on the Owner's playlist (422); switching into it is 403; the Owner cannot give a custom role `activity-view` or `channel-store` (not held — "You can only give a role permissions you hold yourself."), nor `user-destroy`, `activity-destroy` or `permission-update` (never on an organization's role), nor name it "Owner"; the Owner may give `organization-destroy`, and a member holding that custom role gets 403 deleting a rival organization (the Organizations page is the platform's); nobody is invited or moved into Super-Admin or a rival organization's role (422, and only the seeded organization-0 row exists); platform accounts are absent from the members list and cannot be invited; `PUT /users/{id}` is 405 and `PUT /members/{id}` for a non-member is 404; an Admin cannot touch Owners, take ownership, delete the organization or make themselves Owner; Staff can neither see nor change the team or open settings; the only Owner can neither leave nor self-delete; device endpoints ignore a logged-in session and no screen payload leaks a token; with no organization selected the Owner reaches nothing (403).
13. **Impersonation:** guests get 401; a super admin logs in as a user (the session remembers the admin in `impersonating_original_id` and the user in `impersonating_user_id`) and the organization context is cleared; signing in through `/login` with a way back left in the session clears both keys, and a following stop leaves the person signed in as themselves; a stop from an account that is not the one being viewed as redirects to the dashboard, clears the keys and changes nobody's identity; stopping logs `impersonate.stopped` with the admin as actor, clears the organization context and returns to the admin; a non-super gets 403; super → super is 403; a deleted or demoted admin → stop logs out to login; stopping while not impersonating is a no-op; the users listing offers "Log in as" for everyone but super admins and yourself; the Users page's request is answered `{redirect}` — the dashboard, or "Check your inbox" for an account not confirmed — where the super admin, and nobody else, may press Confirm Email and Continue (logged with the super admin as actor; an account already confirmed changes nothing and logs nothing), and "stop" still leads back.
14. **Activity:** mutations log the actor snapshot (e.g. `organization.created`); the page and data need `activity-view` (an organization user without it → 403); login, permission creation and self-deletion are logged; password reset via link logs the user as actor; switching logs `organization.switched` with the organization as subject; the date range bounds by day or by ISO instant and rejects malformed dates; a log just past UTC midnight stays visible to a UTC−5 viewer; maintenance deletes years older than the previous one and logs; partition status needs `activity-view`, maintenance `activity-destroy` (checked with a platform reader — a super admin would pass both); entries are newest first and keep the actor's name after the account is deleted.
15. **The catalogue, the migrations and the seeder:** the three lists cover `LABELS` exactly once, and `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` is all of `PLATFORM` but `user-view`, `user-destroy`, `activity-destroy` and `billing-update`. **Straight off `migrate`, before any seeder** (§22.3): `permissions` equals `Permission::LABELS` exactly — the same 42 names with the same labels and nothing else — and the roles table holds exactly the four starter organization roles, each with its `Role::STARTERS` name, not global, no organization, holding exactly `Role::starterPermissions(key)` (Owner = `ORGANIZATION` + `organization-view` + `organization-destroy`, Admin = `ORGANIZATION` + `organization-view`, Staff 6, Viewer 2); `channels.organization_id` exists with no unique index on `channels.name` and `activity_logs.organization_id` has its `(organization_id, created_at)` index; `organizations.deleted_at`, `users.created_by`, `roles.is_signup_default` and the `personal_access_tokens` table are all absent, the `roles.organization_id` foreign key cascades, and deleting an organization row straight in the database takes its custom role. **After seeding:** the same 42 permissions with labels, Super-Admin holding all of them, the four starter roles untouched, and a super-admin admin; running the seeder twice duplicates nothing (four roles carrying a key); re-seeding keeps what the super admin changed on a starter role and never brings back one they deleted.
16. **Dashboard:** guests are redirected to login; super admins see the organization count; a custom platform role sees the global view (even with a stray organization row); a single-organization member is auto-selected into the organization view; a multi-organization member without a selection is redirected to the selection page, which lists their organizations; the selection page redirects single-organization and platform accounts away and runs the same number of queries for ten organizations as for two; an organization's dashboard counts that organization alone, each card, list and link only with its own permission (every link opening for the person given it), what needs attention named five at a time with the rest counted, the first steps ticked off and gone once done, and the recent activity the organization's own for a role that may read the log; the platform's dashboard by its permissions, naming an organization with no Owner and, for a super admin, a server low on space; a paused organization's dashboard says it is paused.
17. **Guest access:** every people endpoint answers 401 (JSON) or redirects to login (pages) for guests; the invitation link opens for guests, accepting redirects to login.
18. **Listings & limits:** 50 rows by default, the last page holds the remainder, `per_page` clamped to 1–100; named limiters keep device traffic from spending the signup budget.
19. **Double submit & client validation:** a double click on any submit creates exactly one record; client validation blocks bad input with zero requests (e.g. "First name is required.").

Contact support to turn it back on."; a plain form saves nothing; switching to another of their organizations, leaving the paused one and their profile stay open, and an invitation to another can still be answered on the dashboard; its screens keep playing; the platform still reads it and turns it back on, after which it opens again; pausing, turning back on and a rename log `organization.paused`, `organization.resumed` and `organization.updated`; every route of the panel walked as its Owner, with a forged session and through "Log in as", stays shut (`PausedOrganizationAttackTest`).

29. **Invitations for you** (§9.5a): the dashboard offers the organizations' invitations sent to this person's own address and nobody else's, above the empty dashboard too, never to a platform account and never the platform's own invitations; Accept joins at once, opens the organization and logs it; Decline deletes it and only logs it, nobody emailed; one to an organization the person is in already is used up and opens it; one that expired on a page left open is said on the dashboard and nothing is joined; a paused organization's person may still answer an invitation to another.

---

## 26. Known sharp edges (documented REALITY — port them consciously; each is an owner-level decision to revisit)

1. **The Super-Admin anchor is still a name.** Comparing it exactly in application code closed the collation hole (§4.1), but ANY role whose name is exactly `Super-Admin` makes its holders super admins (`Role::isSuperAdmin()` is by name). HTTP cannot create or rename a role into that name (§7.5); a duplicate written straight into the database would count as well — and, a super admin passing every permission check (§3.3), its holders would hold everything — while `superAdminId()` — and with it the role scopes and `primarySuperAdminId()` — follows only the lowest id.
2. **"Invite owner" is blunt — for an organization with no Owner.** It acts only while the organization has no Owner (§10.3: an organization that has one gets 422, and its next Owner comes from its own Members page or from Users → Organizations), but there it promotes an existing member to Owner at once, without their consent; inviting a new address or making a member Owner deletes the organization's Owner invitations to any other address; and sending to an address the organization had invited to another role turns that invitation into an Owner invitation with the platform user as its inviter.
3. **Expired invitations are never pruned.** They stay until resent, revoked, declined, replaced (the Owner invitations of an organization that has no Owner, §10.3) or deleted with their role or organization, and an expired invitation still blocks a new one to the same address in the same place ("resend it instead"). The dashboard's "Invitations for you" hides expired ones (§9.5a), but Decline there takes an expired one away too.
4. **Races that remain.** "One open invitation per email per place" is check-then-write with no unique index, so two simultaneous invites can create two rows; accepting both at the same moment would then hit the `organization_user` unique index as an unhandled error. Two *different* invitations to the same new address registered at the same moment both pass the account check (each holds only its own invitation's lock) and one fails on the `users` unique index. The already-a-member accept path (§9.5 step 4, and the dashboard's, §9.5a) takes no lock, so a double submit there can write two `invitation.accepted` rows. Platform account deletion takes no organization lock — by design it may leave an organization ownerless. "Invite owner" reads the organization's Owner count without a lock (§10.3), so an Owner arriving at that moment (from Users → Organizations, or an accepted invitation) is not seen: the request still makes its member an Owner, or sends its Owner invitation. A role's name is checked against its lists and then written with no lock and no unique index (§2.3, §7.5), so two saves at the same moment can still leave two roles of one folded name in a list. (The last-Owner and one-link-one-use races are closed by row locks, §8.1 and §9.5; SQLite ignores those locks because its writers are serialized, so a port on another database must keep real `FOR UPDATE` locks.)
5. **Every email failure looks the same.** `sendLink` catches any exception — a refusing mail server, but also a bug while building the message — reports it and answers "the email could not be sent"; the activity row still reads "Invited …" and says nothing about the failure. `User::sendALink` does the same for the links that confirm an address (§10.1a) — and counts the email in the budget even when the mail server refused it, so a few refusals in a row can leave the person waiting for the budget too.
6. **Permission names are de-facto anchors with no rename lock.** Routes hardwire the names: renaming a permission makes every route gated on it deny everyone **but super admins** until renamed back — the before-hook (§3.3) lets them through, so the super admin who renamed it sees nothing wrong while every organization and platform user loses the feature. Membership of `ORGANIZATION` and `ORGANIZATION_SCOPED` is by name, so renaming an organization (or organization-scoped) permission also makes it platform-only — organization roles and custom roles holding it are no longer offered it in the checklist and can no longer be saved with it. A permission created on the Permissions page gates nothing, can only go on platform roles, and is attached to the Super-Admin role only when the seeder runs (which no longer matters for access). Deleting a permission held by any role is refused — every seeded permission is held by Super-Admin, so in practice only a permission made on the page, given to no role and not yet attached to Super-Admin by a seeder run, can be deleted.
7. **Email case at login.** Signup and the profile lower-case and invitations normalize, but login and the password-reset link look the email up as typed: on a case-sensitive database (SQLite, PostgreSQL) a differently-capitalized address does not match, while MySQL's collation does. Accounts created before signup lower-cased emails may still hold capitals (the invitation lookups use `lower(email)` and still find them). A port should normalize emails at every door.
8. **The session cookie is the only way a person authenticates — there is no second door left, and that is now literal.** The API scaffold is **gone**: the `laravel/sanctum` dependency, `config/sanctum.php`, the `HasApiTokens` trait, the `personal_access_tokens` migration and table, the `$user->tokens()->delete()` line in the account-deletion hook and the framework's `/sanctum/csrf-cookie` route (§2.1, §15.3, §22). So is Breeze's `password.confirm` screen, with its route, controller, view and test — the unthrottled `POST /confirm-password` was **the one password check in the application outside the shared wrong-password counter** (§19.7) — and Breeze's inert email-verification scaffold and its `email-verification` limiter, deleted on 2026-09-17; email verification came back on 2026-09-29 written anew for this app (§10.1a: its own controller, signed links that open nothing by themselves, the `verification-link` limiter and the mail budget, §19.6). `config/filesystems.php` sets `'serve' => false` on the `local` disk, so even the framework's `GET|PUT /storage/{path}` routes are not registered. What this costs: any future machine client needs a deliberate design rather than a scaffold to switch on — the only non-session doors in the app are the screens' device API (`routes/device.php`, registered outside the `web` group with its own token-hash authentication and per-device limiters) and the TV app's open, read-only `GET /player-app/latest.json` (§16a), and the owner's no-API rule (§3.7) means a port should not re-add a token surface without being asked.
9. **Route-model binding runs before the gates (§3.3)**, so on permission- or tier-locked routes the status code tells an outsider whether an id exists: e.g. an organization Owner gets 403 from `DELETE /users/{id}` for an existing account and 404 for a missing one. The 404 discipline of §6 holds only once the gates have passed. Binding also runs before the stale-tab refusal, `verified` and `organization.active`, so even a stale tab, an unconfirmed account or a paused organization's people learn by the 404 that an id does not exist.
10. **Impersonated account deleted mid-session:** the admin's next request is unauthenticated (redirect to login) and nothing is logged; the two impersonation keys stay in that session until the next sign-in clears them (§14.1) — except on the `database` session driver, where the deletion removes the session rows naming the deleted account (§15.3), the admin's viewing-as session among them, keys included. The layout's banner reads `impersonating_original_id` alone, but no page showing it can be reached without signing in, which clears it.
11. **Adjusting an organization role can bend the hierarchy, in every organization at once.** Reach compares the permission rows actually held (§8.1), and there is no per-organization override of an organization role. When the super admin takes a permission away from the Owner role, or gives Admin (or any other organization role) one the Owner role lacks, Owners can no longer invite anybody to that role, give it, or change or remove the members holding it (422 / 403 with the usual reach messages, §8.4, §8.5, §9.2); taking `organization-update` from the Owner role turns the organization details read-only for every Owner; taking `organization-view` from it closes the Organizations tab of Settings to every Owner, whose "Your organizations" then shows on the profile (tested); taking `billing-view` from it closes Settings → Billing to every Owner; taking `organization-destroy` from it stops every Owner deleting their organization, and giving it to Admin lets every Admin do so — and brings the Owner role, and every Owner but an organization's only one, within every Admin's reach (§8.1, §8.2), so every Admin may then make anybody an Owner, which is how an organization changes hands (§11.3, §26.16); giving the Owner role `organization-store` lets every Owner open organizations (§26.14); taking `member-view` or `role-view` hides the Members or Roles page. Nothing but ownership itself stays tied to the Owner role's key, whatever it is renamed to (§7.1). `Role::STARTERS` is only the starting point (§7.1): nothing re-applies it — not the starter-roles migration, which runs once on a fresh database, and not the seeder — so an organization permission added to `ORGANIZATION` later reaches Owner and Admin only through a migration that grants it to them by key, or the super admin's edit, and an installation whose code is updated before that migration runs has Owners who cannot yet use the feature. A role may also still carry a **stale** permission row the rules would refuse today — `organization-transfer`, `user-view`, `user-destroy` on an organization role, left by an older build or written straight into the database: it opens nothing (no route names `organization-transfer`, and the `/users` group keeps `global-tier`, §13), the role form's checklist does not list it, and the form drops it on the role's next save (§7.9, §20.8). A description always tells what a role allows now (§4.4). Deleting an organization role the super admin no longer wants — Admin, Staff and Viewer included — is possible only while nobody holds it.
12. **One wrong-password counter per person.** Every password re-confirmation — the JSON big deletes, the Organizations tab's organization delete, Profile self-delete and the password change — counts into the same `confirm-password:{user id}` counter (§19.7), not one per form, dialog, session or device: five wrong passwords anywhere pause all of them for that person, everywhere, for the rest of the minute — and whoever uses an open session can cause that pause. The counter is separate from the login throttle (keyed by email and IP, §19.3), and a password accepted by the check clears it even when a later locked re-check of the same request refuses.
13. **A role carrying what the Owner role lacks is the super admin's alone to manage.** Reach inside an organization compares the permission rows actually held (§8.1), and an organization role the super admin made — or an organization's custom role the super admin changed from the platform — may carry organization-scoped platform permissions the Owner role does not start with (`organization-store`, `channel-store`, `activity-view`, …). No member of the organization — not even an Owner — can then invite anybody to that role, give it, or change or remove its holders, nor edit or delete it when it is a custom role (403 / 422 with the usual reach messages); the holders can still leave, and the super admin manages them from the platform (§7.7, §13.4) — or gives the Owner role those permissions, for every organization at once.
14. **Opening organizations has no limit.** An organization's role carrying `organization-store` opens as many organizations as its holder asks for — no cap, no confirmation, no throttle beyond `throttle:admin` — each owned by that person through the Owner role (§10.2). Once the super admin gives `organization-store` to the **Owner** role, every Owner can open organizations, and each organization opened makes its opener an Owner with the same permission again. *Owner's rule, 2026-09-16: off by default; the super admin decides who holds it.*
15. **Users → Organizations is straight in.** A super admin puts any account outside the platform team — one in no organization included — into any organization with any role that organization has, the Owner role too, with no consent step, no email and no hierarchy check (§13.4); the person simply finds the organization in their switcher. The one guard kept is at least one Owner. Adding somebody as Owner does not ask whether the organization already has one.
16. **Making somebody an Owner is handing the organization over.** There is no separate handover and no consent step (§11.3): whoever reaches the Owner role in an organization — out of the box every Owner; anybody whose role holds every permission the Owner role holds (§8.1, §26.11) — makes any member an Owner with Change role, and every Owner then reaches every other Owner, so a new co-owner may at once demote or remove the others, leaving only themselves (the last-Owner rule, §8.2, keeps one Owner — it does not say which). A partner made Owner in good faith can take the organization. (The removed `organization-transfer` permission, §3.1, once made Owners from the Organizations tab even beyond the actor's reach; that door is gone.)
17. **One address is one account only as typed.** Capitals are folded at every door, but nothing else is (owner's rule, 2026-09-29: "no aliases" means no folding of dots or `+tags`): `sana.khan+x@` and `sanakhan@` are two accounts of one inbox. The robot traps (§10.1) and the mail budget (§10.1a) bound what that costs in mail.
18. **The mail budget is per visitor too.** Thirty emails an hour per IP, across every account, so an office or a phone network behind one address shares them: the thirty-first person signing up there in an hour gets "Too many emails asked for. Try again in …" on "Check your inbox" and sends the link again later. The per-account budget counts an email the mail server refused (§26.5).
19. **An unconfirmed account can outlive the week.** The prune keeps — and only names in its output — an unconfirmed account whose organization holds anybody or anything (somebody above the organizations put a member or a file in it, or it is on the platform team): nothing removes it afterwards, and it stays behind "Check your inbox" until somebody confirms it (a super admin can, through "Log in as", §10.1a) or deletes it from the platform.
20. **The robot traps can catch a person.** A sign-up page left open more than two hours, or sent back within three seconds of being drawn, answers "Please try again." with the fields kept but the passwords emptied; sending it again from the fresh page works.
21. **The stale-tab guard is about tabs, not attackers.** A request that carries no session context — made by hand, or from a page drawn before the guard existed — goes through (§20.4a); what protects the data against a hostile request is everything else in this document. And what changes the session on purpose (switching organization, "Log in as", "stop", sign-out) is never refused, so a stale tab can still switch.
22. **A pause closes the pages, nothing else.** A paused organization's televisions keep playing and its people keep their memberships, their session keeps it chosen, and the platform can still add people to it from Users → Organizations; a person who belongs only to it sees the paused page and nothing else of the panel until the platform turns it back on (§2.2).
23. **A pending email reserves nothing.** `users.pending_email` has no unique index and `PATCH /profile` checks only `users.email`: two accounts may wait for the same new address, and a signup may take it meanwhile; whoever opens a link first wins, and the other is told "{new} is used by another account now, so your email stays {old}." (§14.2) — never the other way round.

---
*Rewritten for the organization-as-organization people model (2026-09-16), updated for the owner's later rules of the same day — a super admin holds every permission, big deletes re-confirm the password, a permission reaches where its role sits (organizations, accounts, channels and reading the activity log on an organization's role, for that organization alone), deleting an organization is the permission `organization-destroy` — and for the owner's rules of 2026-09-17: no role built in but Super-Admin (organization roles and platform roles made by the super admin, the Owner role marked by its key and never deleted, Owner / Admin / Staff / Viewer only the starter roles, a name new to every list it is shown in, a checklist listing only what the role can hold, every organization's custom roles managed from the platform, same-named organization copies merged into one organization role), the super admin putting anybody in any organization from Users → Organizations, and the organization's settings as a tab of Settings with the person handing it over choosing the role they keep — and for the second round of the 2026-09-17 rules, "a role does not matter, its permissions do": reach with no special case for the Owner role (an organization's only Owner kept by the Owner count instead), handing an organization over as the new permission `organization-transfer`, the **Organizations** tab of Settings opening with `organization-view` and carrying "Your organizations" and Create organization, the Organizations page the platform's alone with no owners on its list, and a held role offering Delete that says it must be unassigned first — and for the third round of the 2026-09-17 rules: no Transfer Ownership and no `organization-transfer` (an organization changes hands on its Members page), no Accounts inside an organization (`user-view` and `user-destroy` the platform's alone, the whole `/users` group behind `global-tier`), "Invite owner" only for an organization with no Owner, a deleted account leaving nothing pointing at it (its sessions, reset token and the invitations to its email go too), and organizations deleted for good ("A to Z": no `deleted_at`, and a custom role cascading with its organization).*

*Brought back in line with the code after the cleanup of 2026-09-17 (evening): the **migrations squashed to 21 files** that build the final schema directly, with every upgrade migration deleted and the one data migration now `2026_09_16_110200_insert_permissions_and_starter_roles` — so `migrate` alone leaves the whole 37-permission catalogue and the four starter organization roles (§21, §22); **Sanctum and the API token scaffold deleted** (no `personal_access_tokens`, no `HasApiTokens`, nothing to clear on account deletion, §2.1, §15.3, §26.8); **Breeze's email-verification and confirm-password screens deleted** with their routes, controllers, views and the `email-verification` limiter — `users.email_verified_at` kept, because an invitation link proves the inbox (§2.1, §19.6, §19.7); `GET /roles/{role}/permissions` removed, so the Roles endpoints are the page, the list, create, update, delete and the checklist (§3.7, §7.9); and the `local` disk no longer served over HTTP (§26.8).*

*Brought back in line with the code on 2026-10-13: email confirmation for every account (the `verified` wall and its pinned doors, the signed links, "Confirm Email and Continue" for a super admin viewing as an account, `pending_email`, the mail budget, the seven-day prune — §10.1a), signup's robot traps, paused organizations closed to their people (§2.2), the dashboard's summary and "Invitations for you" (§18, §9.5a), a lone organization chosen on every request and the chooser returning to the page asked for, the stale-tab refusal and the JSON error words (§20.4a, §19.1), the Users page's tabs and sorting, billing's and the TV app's permissions (42 in the catalogue; §3, §12.1a, §16a), every named limiter (§19.6), the 36 migration files (§22.2b), and the invariants, scenarios and sharp edges that follow from them.*

*Checked against the code section by section — routes, controllers, models, the OrganizationTeam service, migrations, seeder, views, scripts and the feature and browser tests. Where the design record (`docs/ORGANIZATION-SPEC.md`) and the code differ, this document follows the code.*
